## Summary

The Life-Support Graph is the ship's circulation as one integer-only, scene-tree-free simulation block: five loops (O₂, power, coolant, fuel, integrity) as typed ports, nodes and edges, plus a per-compartment atmosphere (gas amount, temperature; pressure and bands derived). It is the physical ground truth every trail, gauge, hiss, breath-fog and cold-damage tick is read from, and the only thing in the game that never lies — hallucinations are tested against it, never written into it. Failures spread one hop per 10 Hz propagation run through the quantities they change, every endogenous loss is preceded by ≥ 10 s of two-channel physical warning, and welding another ship's section joins its subgraph along a seam whose sub-edges start disconnected. Everything that fails, glows, leaks or is salvaged reads or writes it through 19 published mutators (M1–M19) and one true-value read view.

> **Quick reference** — Layer: `Core` · Priority: `MVP` · Key deps: `Command & Simulation Layer` (designed; one block `graph.state`, three hooks at divisor 6); `Ship Section & Layout Data Model` and `Item & Module Database` (designed; data-contract peers, not upstream dependencies — load order Item DB, then Layout, then this block; see Dependencies) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; art bible (trail and loop grammar). Depended on by 14 systems in the 2026-10-26 systems index and by 17 designed readers and callers at the 2026-10-27 review (Failure & Trail, Suit, Emitter State, Attention, Interaction, Assembly, Horror, Perception Filter, In-World Instruments, Adaptive Audio, Save/Load, Sanity, Web Map, Boarding & Salvage, HUD, Debug Overlay, Derelict Authoring; Ship Body has no direct edge), plus four VS systems (Wear, Failure Contract, Co-op, Onboarding) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

## Overview

The Life-Support Graph is the ship's circulation written as data: one integer-only simulation block that holds **five loops** — O₂, power, coolant, fuel and integrity — as typed ports on nodes joined by typed edges, plus one atmosphere record (gas amount, temperature) per compartment. It is the physical ground truth the whole game is measured against. Every pipe that leaks, bus that scorches, seam that frosts, scrubber that stalls and hull plate that breaches is a change to this block, made only through its published mutators inside the kernel's write scope; every gauge, trail, hiss, breath-fog and cold-damage tick is a *reading* of it. The Graph is scene-tree free, headless-testable and deterministic (same seed and command stream → identical `state_hash`), serialises as one versioned snapshot, and is the data that co-op will later sync by summary.

The player never operates the Graph as a menu or a schematic. They meet it as a place: a corridor colder than it should be, chevrons crawling slow along a pipe, mist curling from a clamp, a lamp that dims when the scrubber draws. Their verbs — patch, flip a breaker, seal a bulkhead, close a valve, vent, salvage a module, weld a section on, crank by hand — arrive as Interaction commands that call Graph mutators; welding joins another ship's subgraph to this one along a seam. Failures are physical and emergent: a starved scrubber, a frozen pipe or an overloaded cable spreads one hop per propagation run through the quantities it changes, never by script, and every endogenous loss is preceded by a readable physical warning (the fairness gate). The Graph has no notion of a hallucination: it publishes true values only, so a false "fixed" scrubber can be caught by the cold, the thinning air and the hiss the Graph still drives.

Without this system there is no Pillar 1. Without a shared, honest, falsifiable physical state, the hallucination design (Pillar 4) has nothing to lie *against*, the web has no power draw to watch (Pillar 3), and salvage and welding have nothing to act on (Pillar 5). It is the design-order-3 bottleneck (14 dependents in the systems index; 17 designed readers and callers at the 2026-10-27 review), and the MVP hypothesis — *the boarding→breach loop over a visible life-support graph is tense for 30+ minutes* — cannot be tested without it.

*Implementation questions raised while designing and deferred to ADRs (→ `/architecture-decision`): struct-of-arrays packed fields vs objects (ADR-LSG-01 — which also records the GDScript constraints of the 2026-10-27 review: double-buffered start-of-phase arrays, underscore-prefixed fields with a writer lint, `GraphFault.raise` instead of the release-stripped `assert`, raw canonical bytes carried as base64 through `Marshalls`, the signed form of the 0x9E37… multiplier, an `integer_division` warning ignore (`@warning_ignore_start`, verified by S0-b), and the extended S0-a / S0-b probes); the fixed-point unit system (ADR-LSG-02); the explicit one-pass propagation scheme (ADR-LSG-03); append-only topology identity (ADR-LSG-04); snapshot/restore and migration (ADR-LSG-05); dirty-key encoding (ADR-LSG-06); keyed RNG (ADR-LSG-07); stable content type IDs (ADR-LSG-08); and two routed amendments outside this system — the kernel's per-block cached hash (K-1) and Save/Load's binary interchange for 64-bit values (S-1).*

## Player Fantasy

**Framing: indirect, with a direct layer on top.** The player experiences the Graph through its consequences and acts on it through physical verbs; they never see "the Graph". *(creative-director consulted; Framing A adopted with B as its competence and C as its guarantee.)*

> The ship is a body the crew assembled from other ships' corpses, and the Life-Support Graph is its circulation: oxygen, power, coolant, fuel and the hull that holds them. The player never operates the Graph as a menu. They tend the ship: a corridor colder than it should be, chevrons crawling slow along a pipe, frost fanning out from a weld seam, a hiss behind a panel. They go to it with a light and their hands, and they reroute, seal, weld or cut.
>
> Competence is literacy, not optimisation. Every failure leaves a trail along the edge it travels, thickest at its source, so a veteran crew does not run the ship better than a new one; they understand sooner what it is telling them and reach the right seam faster.
>
> And the body never lies. Gauges can be wrong and eyes can be wrong, but the cold, the thin air and the falling pressure in a compartment come from the Graph's true state for every player. When the instruments and the room disagree, the room wins, and the player learns to trust it.

**Anchor moment.** You come back up the pod tube with a salvaged scrubber on your back, and the corridor is colder than you left it. The O₂ pipe along the ceiling is still lit cyan, but its chevrons are crawling slower than they should, and there is a fine white fan of frost on the elbow by the galley. Your own breath hangs in the beam. You set the scrubber down, put your glove on the pipe, and follow the cold back to where it started — the way you would check a sleeping friend's forehead. The emotion is *caretaking under threat*: tenderness and vigilance. The power is not control; it is keeping something alive by attention — the Project Zomboid base you built with your own hands, made precarious (concept, Core Fantasy).

**Pillar alignment.** Pillar 1 verbatim: *"Pipes leak, frost forms, air hisses; the physical world is ground truth. Only instruments and perception can be wrong … A pipe may look fixed, but the room still gets cold, the air still thins, the pressure still drops."* The Graph is that ground truth. Pillar 2 (Dread Before Chaos): every fault has a long, readable tell before it cascades. Pillar 3: keeping the air clean draws power, and power draw is something the web can read. Pillar 4: the Graph is the falsification ground every hallucination is tested against. Pillar 5: salvage, welding and wear act on its nodes; loss is the failure contract's business, not the Graph's.

**Design test.** *When a choice makes the ship easier to read from a screen vs. easier to read from the room → choose the room. When a choice rewards running the ship well vs. rewards noticing it is unwell → choose noticing.* Corollaries: a fault persists and spreads until someone goes to it (never times out); a failure's origin is shown by a trail the player follows back along the edge, never by a marker; a hallucinated "fixed" state never quiets the real hiss or warms the real room.

**Under hallucination the fantasy deepens rather than breaks.** Pillar 4 lets the lie extend from the gauge to the sprite — a broken scrubber may render whole and spinning. What cannot change is the **consequence channel** — a closed list: compartment temperature, O₂ and pressure as the body feels them; the frost, mist, scorch and drip trails; the LS-14 acoustic classes at a descriptor's anchor (HISS, DRIP, HUM_DROP, CLANK, WIND — the whistle —, TICK, SIZZLE, CREAK; there is no WHISTLE or loop-whisper class) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from adaptive-audio-and-tells, boarding-and-salvage)*; lamp brightness; the draught of AIRFLOW and the strain of a closed hatch under pressure (SEAL_STRAIN) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from perception-filter)*. Failure & Trail, Suit and Adaptive Audio render these as pure readers of the Graph's true values, and the Filter may neither remove nor add to them. What *can* lie is the module-body channel: sprites, gauges, inspect cards and a module's own wear state. The caretaker who can no longer trust their eyes falls back on breath, cold and touch — the oldest reading of a body. Verification costs rise in order: the room itself; a second instrument elsewhere on the ship; a saner crewmate (VS-B); time.

**Reference techniques, taken precisely.** Project Zomboid — the base degrades on its own clock and competence is the routine of noticing (*adopt*); its numeric context-menu condition readouts (*reject*). Barotrauma — repair is a place you go with the right tool; a junction box fails *where it is* (*adopt*); the Status Monitor ship-overview screen (*reject*). Dwarf Fortress fluids — propagation is visible in the medium, not announced (*adopt*). The Long Dark — cold is read from breath vapour before any number (*adopt*). SS13 / Stationeers — precision is something you go and get (*adopt* as the lit inspect card); their gas chemistry (*reject*: five loops, not chemistry). FTL — door-control causality (*adopt*); per-room O₂ shading on a schematic (*reject*). Alien: Isolation — checking costs you (*adopt*). Pandorum — the failing ship as the only reliable narrator (*adopt*).

**What this GDD does not promise (anti-pillar guard rails).**
1. No mastery-by-optimisation: no efficiency, "optimal layout", throughput score, uptime grade or any reward for running loops near capacity. Staying alive is the only payoff.
2. The web is not a puzzle to solve: failures are not authored puzzles with one correct sequence; there is no "solved" state and no completion ping.
3. No schematic, overlay, minimap or ship-status screen. Precision exists only as the lit inspect card of a specific instrument (art bible 7.0), which lies when that gauge lies. The suit's O₂ numeral on the critical strip is the *suit's* reading, not a Graph overlay.
4. No progression from the Graph: nothing unlocks or levels from repairs; better modules come from salvage.
5. No readability in the dark: "What You Light, You Can Read" — unlit compartments are readable only through the body channels (cold, breath, hiss, pressure), never by sprite state.
6. No sixth loop and no "heat loop": heat and pressure are per-compartment atmosphere.
7. No freeform plumbing: rerouting is valves, breakers, hatches, seams and cuts on pre-shaped modules.
8. No guarantee the ship can always be saved. Losing a section or the ship is allowed; the failure contract (Pillar 5) is the safety net, not the Graph.

## Detailed Design

### Core Rules

*Every rule below is a sim rule of the Graph owner module (`src/sim/graph/`), stated so that an implementer can write it and a tester can falsify it. Registry constants are cited by their registered name (`TICK_RATE`, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS`, `STEP_BUDGET_MS`, `rng_derive`, `state_hash`, `hook_runs_this_tick`, `TILE_PX`); every constant this GDD introduces is written by the registry name it proposes (Section G marks the candidates). Type IDs are explicit, stable, checked unique at boot and never reused (R19). Where a rule records an alternative that was considered and rejected, the rejection is part of the rule: a later change must answer it.*

**LS-1 — One block, one determinism contract.** The Graph is exactly one persisted SIM state block, `graph.state` (owner Graph; `class` SIM; `persist` true; `persist_scope` WORLD; `scope` SHARED; `repl_hint` SHARED_SUMMARY; `schema_version` 1; `restore_after: [layout.state]`), registered by the Graph's composition-root call during BOOTING. It is a struct-of-arrays object (`GraphState extends RefCounted`; every field is underscore-prefixed and a lint forbids writes outside the four writer files `graph_mutators.gd`, `graph_propagate.gd`, `graph_restore.gd` (decode, validate, swap) and `graph_derived.gd` (non-persisted caches only), because GDScript has no private members) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `PackedInt64Array` for conserved quantities and anything that can exceed 2^31, `PackedInt32Array` for ids, indices and basis points, `PackedByteArray` for enums and flags; the only strings are the append-only interned `def_table` (one `PackedStringArray` entry per distinct Item DB `def_id`, persisted in `canonical_bytes()` as a CSR pair — `def_off` (`PackedInt32Array`) and `def_utf8` (`PackedByteArray`) — and referenced from a node by an int32 `def_ix`, 0 = none) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; there are no per-node objects, no `Resource` for state or for saves, and no `Node` anywhere in the module (kernel H10; gdUnit4 exit 101 is impossible by construction). The contract: (a) **units** — gas in mGU (`GAS_SCALE` 1000; 1 GU is the amount giving 0.1 kPa in one tile), volume in tiles, pressure derived in dkPa (`STD_PRESSURE_DKPA` 1000), temperature in mC, power in W ≡ dJ per step, energy in dJ, liquids in mL, integrity in bp 0..10000 (`HP_FULL` 10000), fractions in permille, opening area in milli flow units on the gas path (`A_m` = 1000 × flow units, D.1), and a per-compartment gas maximum `n_max(c) = P_MAX × V_c × GAS_SCALE` with `P_MAX` 1500 dkPa (at most 3 × 10^8 mGU at `V` 200; 4.2 × 10^7 at standard pressure in the 42-tile cryo bay) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; (b) **zero floats** stored, hashed, replicated or used on the propagation path — a designer float in the data file is converted once at boot by `int(round(x × scale))` into the immutable `GraphTuning` and is not block state; (c) **store extensive, derive intensive** — `n_o2`, `n_inert`, `(T, t_rem)` as the energy pair `U = T × C + t_rem`, battery `stock` in dJ, liquid `stock_ml`, `hp`; `P`, `p_o2`, `o2_pm`, `served_pm`, `circ`, every descriptor and every cache are derived on read or in the run and never persisted — except the **hysteresis memory** of the three bands, of `pressure_trend` and of SEAL_STRAIN, which depends on the run before and is therefore persisted (`band_o2`, `band_p`, `band_t`, `p_prev`, two live bits per stored OPENING; LS-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; (d) **every flux is a non-negative magnitude with an explicit direction**, so integer division is applied only to non-negative operands and truncation is floor everywhere except the two signed remainder-carrying thermal steps, which truncate toward zero and carry the remainder (each file of `src/sim/graph/` opens with `@warning_ignore_start("integer_division")`, because every flux division is intentional integer division and would otherwise flood the editor with warnings; S0-b verifies that this annotation exists in 4.7.2 and silences the warning — the engine reference has no entry for it [UNV] — and if it does not, the fallback is the project setting `debug/gdscript/warnings/integer_division` set to Ignore for the module's directory, never a per-statement annotation on every flux) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; (e) **exact conservation** — each section carries persisted counters `vented_mgu` (gas lost to EXTERIOR), `radiated_dj` (net heat exchanged with the exterior sink through hull and radiators) and `spilled_cool_ml` (coolant lost from the loop), **plus the production and consumption counters `produced_mgu` (O2 the scrubber adds to the pool plus the inert make-up it supplies, D.5), `breathed_mgu` (O2 removed by M15), `produced_dj` (signed net heat injected, counted at its full value: the machine's whole `Q_src` — captured and uncaptured alike — the heater, BURN and CHILL footprints), `burned_uml` (fuel burned, in micro-millilitres: 10^6 µmL = 1 mL) and `spilled_heat_mdj` (heat carried out of the world by coolant that leaves the loop, in milli-dJ, signed because `t_cool` may be below 0)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; renamed from `burned_ul` and extended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; fuel lost from a pipe lands in the compartment's `fuel_puddle_ml`; the invariants — gas `Σ (n_o2 + n_inert) + Σ O2_TANK stock + vented_mgu + breathed_mgu + discarded_mgu + withdrawn_mgu − produced_mgu`; heat, in milli-dJ so that the equality is exact, `Σ_c (T_c × C_c + t_rem_c) + Σ_r (C_COOL × level_ml_r × t_cool_r + tc_rem_r) + 1000 × (radiated_dj − produced_dj) + spilled_heat_mdj` — the compartments' `U` **plus every RESERVOIR's heat store** (D.8 moves `Q_cap` and `Q_rad` into and out of that store, so it must be in the sum), where `spilled_heat_mdj` is credited in phase 5 at every coolant leak as `leak_ml × C_COOL × t_cool` (the coolant leaves at the loop's temperature) plus the reservoir's residual `tc_rem` on the run its `level_ml` reaches 0, and likewise for coolant discarded or withdrawn with a store (M6, M16, LS-16); coolant `Σ stock_ml + spilled_cool_ml + discarded_cool_ml + withdrawn_cool_ml`; fuel `10^6 × (Σ stock_ml + Σ fuel_puddle_ml + discarded_fuel_ml + withdrawn_fuel_ml) + burned_uml − Σ fuel_rem`, where `fuel_rem` (0..999,999 µmL per REACTOR) is the sub-mL part of the burn that `burned_uml` has already counted — worked check: a step at 2500 W burns 2500 × 120 = 300,000 µmL, so `burned_uml` and `fuel_rem` each gain 300,000 and the stock is unchanged; on the fourth step `fuel_rem` reaches 1,200,000, the stock loses 1 mL and `fuel_rem` becomes 200,000 with `burned_uml` 1,200,000, and `10^6 × (S − 1) + 1,200,000 − 200,000 = 10^6 × S` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; carried-over blocker, the factor is 10^6 and the counter is in µmL)* — are exact integer equalities over any number of steps **on a running ship with sources, sinks and crew active, not only with everything switched off**, and every test asserts them; (f) **every product the propagation code forms has documented operand maxima** and the boot assertion of D.13 refuses a build whose table violates `max_a × max_b < 2^62`; (g) **no `sqrt`, `exp`, `pow` or `sin` on the propagation path** — laws are linear or piecewise-linear integer tables, and per-step decay is written `x −= x × k_ppm / 10^6`; (h) **stability** — `Σ_j k_ij ≤ 1` per compartment (D.12), asserted at boot and re-checked by `can_join` at every weld; (i) **iteration is by ascending slot over dense arrays** and slot order equals ascending-id order at all times (slots are append-only within a session, removal tombstones, no compaction in MVP), so no result depends on a Dictionary's order and sim code never iterates a Dictionary; (j) **one explicit local-exchange pass per loop per run**, computed from a start-of-phase snapshot into accumulators and applied afterwards (Jacobi) — the snapshot is a double-buffered pair of arrays (`_cur` is read, `_next` is written, swapped when the phase ends), never a local alias: whether assigning a GDScript packed array to a `var` aliases it, copies it or copies on write is **not assumed anywhere in this document** (S0-a records it, ADR-LSG-01), and the working rule until it does is that a snapshot is taken only by an explicit `.duplicate()` or element copy into the preallocated scratch member and that writes go only through members of `GraphState` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* — with the source-depletion clamp resolved by scaling all outflows of an over-drawn node by `available / requested` in ascending edge id (the one exception is the VENT allocation of a short O2 pool, D.5, which is sequential-greedy in ascending node id because a vent is a draw on a shared store and the lower id is served first) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* — never an iterative or global solver, never catch-up ("we are behind, run two steps"): the kernel's lag policy drops time and the Graph must not re-add it. *Alternative considered:* two blocks (topology; flow/failure/atmosphere) as the kernel's Interactions row 1 says in the plural — rejected because a weld writes both and G4 wants one snapshot (R2; a routed wording fix "Blocks" → "Block" goes to the kernel owner). *Alternative considered:* SI units (mmol, J, mK) with an ideal-gas constant — rejected because a 2D game with tile volumes needs no gas constant and the worst product of the game units is 2 × 10^13 (D.13), which makes TD's two-stage temperature form and tank-pressure caveat moot (R1).

**LS-2 — Five loops, no sixth.** Loop ids: `0 NONE` (non-loop couplings), `1 O2`, `2 POWER`, `3 COOLANT`, `4 FUEL`, `5 INTEGRITY`. What "flow" means per loop is fixed here and nowhere else: **O2** — a pool of connected O2_TANK stocks (each with its def's `stock_cap`); SCRUBBER adds O2 (throttled to the free tank space) and offers inert make-up, VENT withdraws into its compartment (D.5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, consumers are bounded by `path_cap` (the bottleneck DUCT, seam or VALVE on the unique tree path, derived at topology refresh); **POWER** — an instantaneous island balance with no inventory except BATTERY, allocated by tier (D.6); edge load is the net flow through the edge on the island's tree — the served power below the edge minus what the sources below it deliver (D.6; one source at the root reduces to the served power below the edge) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* — and exists only for D.9; **COOLANT** — `circ` ‰ (D.8) says how much of the nominal heat exchange happens; heat is carried as the RESERVOIR's `(t_cool, tc_rem)` pair; **FUEL** — stock moves tank → burner limited by `path_cap`, burned per reactor output; **INTEGRITY** — static; `hp` is the store, STRUT load `ρ = load / cap` exists for VS-A redistribution and is not computed in MVP. Heat and pressure are **per-compartment atmosphere values** (LS-6), not loops: the O2 loop feeds compartments, the COOLANT loop moves heat between machines and radiators, and passive conduction and gas exchange between rooms are properties of OPENING edges. Anything proposed later (CO2, humidity, advection, fire) must either live per compartment or be out of scope; it may not become loop 6.

**LS-3 — Node kinds.** Stable ids: `1 COMPARTMENT`, `2 EXTERIOR` (one per world, node id 0, `P_EXT` 0 dkPa and `T_AMB_MC` −30000 in MVP, R14), `10 REACTOR`, `11 BATTERY`, `12 CABLE_JUNCTION` (holds `breaker_closed`), `13 LOAD` (with `subtype`; `HEATER` is a LOAD subtype with `waste_permille` 1000 — one in the MVP home-ship, R8 — whose effective request carries a stateless thermostat, D.7 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*), `20 SCRUBBER`, `21 O2_TANK`, `22 VENT`, `23 DUCT_JUNCTION`, `30 PUMP`, `31 RADIATOR`, `32 RESERVOIR`, `33 PIPE_JUNCTION`, `34 HEAT_EXCHANGER` (VS-A), `40 FUEL_TANK`, `41 FUEL_JUNCTION`, `42 FUEL_PORT` (VS-A), `50 HULL_SEGMENT`, `51 FRAME_NODE` (VS-A). Common persisted fields on every node: `id` (u32, never reused), `kind`, `section_id`, `def_ix` (index into the interned `def_table` of Item DB `def_id` strings, LS-1: 0 for COMPARTMENT/EXTERIOR/HULL_SEGMENT; the Item DB and `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF` see the string) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `state` (LS-10), `hp`, `hp_cap` (default `HP_FULL`), `forced_mode`, `forced_mode_next` (the cascade queue, LS-12), `fail_since_step`, `warn_since_step`, `mode_since_step`, `mode_key`, `isolated_at_step` (the three GA-T onset fields, LS-14: the step the subject's dominant descriptor entered its current mode and severity, that descriptor's `(mode << 3) | sev` as of the last run — hysteresis-class memory, persisted so a restore does not read a spurious change — and the step a closed valve first isolated it, 0 = not isolated) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, `thaw_until_step` (the persisted thaw-burst roll window of a liquid-carrying subject, LS-9: 0 = closed) and a `has_warn` flag bit (a warning is running; `warn_since_step` is meaningful only while it is set, so a back-dated authored value ≤ 0 is valid, D.11) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `patched_at_step`, `compartment_id` (where it physically sits — the reader's anchor), `ports[]`. Kind-specific persisted fields: REACTOR `out_w`, `fuel_rem`, `scram`, `starve_steps`; BATTERY `stock`; LOAD `req_w`, `tier`, `subtype`, `energised_at_step`; VENT `setpoint_pp`; PUMP `throttle_pm`; RESERVOIR `stock_ml`, `t_cool`, `tc_rem`; FUEL_TANK `stock_ml`; O2_TANK `stock`; HULL_SEGMENT `bound_compartment`, `exposed`, `tiles`, `fatigued`; COMPARTMENT per LS-6. Ports: `port_type_id`, `local_index`, `cap` (loop units per step, from the module def), `open_end`. Modules in the Item & Module Database declare `node_kind`, `ports[] = (port_type_id, cap, local_index)`, `tier`, `req_w`, `hp_start`, `waste_permille` and reference the port-type ids of LS-4 by number — the Item DB does not define them.

**LS-4 — Port types and the connectability matrix.** Encoding `loop × 100 + role` (roles: 1 SOURCE, 2 SINK, 3 STORE, 4 THRU); 9xx are non-loop couplings. `101 O2_SRC`, `103 O2_STORE`, `104 O2_THRU`; `201 PWR_SRC`, `202 PWR_SNK`, `203 PWR_STORE`, `204 PWR_THRU`; `301 CLT_HEAT_IN`, `302 CLT_HEAT_OUT`, `303 CLT_STORE`, `304 CLT_THRU`; `401 FUEL_SRC`, `402 FUEL_SNK`, `403 FUEL_STORE`, `404 FUEL_THRU`; `501 STR_ANCHOR`, `504 STR_THRU`; `901 ATMO_VENT` (node ↔ its compartment's gas), `902 THERMAL_COUPLE` (waste heat ↔ compartment, and the machine proxy), `903 HULL_FACE` (segment bounds a compartment and the exterior), `904 OPENING_FACE` (door, hatch or seam-gap side). `connectable(a, b)` is pure and used by data load and `can_join`: same loop; THRU joins anything of its loop; SRC–SNK, SRC–STORE, STORE–SNK allowed; SRC–SRC, SNK–SNK, STORE–STORE rejected (must go through a THRU); 301 and 302 attach only to 304; 904 only to 904; 901–903 never carry edges. Ids are append-only forever; a new port type is a new number.

**LS-5 — Edge kinds.** `1 DUCT` (O2; `cap` mGU/step, `len`, `exposed` — a hull-run duct whose leak goes to EXTERIOR instead of its compartment), `2 CABLE` (POWER; `cap` W), `3 PIPE` (COOLANT or FUEL by its `loop` field; `cap` mL/step), `4 STRUT` (INTEGRITY; VS-A), `5 VALVE` (O2/COOLANT/FUEL; `open`; closed is impassable and leak-isolating), `6 SEAM` (any loop; a loop edge crossing sections with `seam_id` and the seam's `q`; created only by `weld_join`), `7 OPENING` (no loop; `area` in flow units where 64 = one standard door aperture — the 96-px two-tile door, `A_HOLE_MAX` — so one unit is 1/64 of a door, not of a tile; `door_state`; `area` 0 is a sealed door *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from ship-section-and-layout-data-model)*). Breach holes and seam weeps are **virtual OPENINGs to EXTERIOR derived from `hp` and `(q, leak_bp)` each run** (D.2), never stored; a virtual opening counts toward a compartment's degree for D.1/D.12 exactly like a stored one. Common persisted edge fields: `id`, `kind`, `loop`, `a_node`, `a_port`, `b_node`, `b_port`, `hp`, `hp_cap`, `state`, `forced_mode`, `forced_mode_next`, `fail_since_step`, `warn_since_step`, `mode_since_step`, `mode_key`, `isolated_at_step` (as LS-3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, `thaw_until_step` and `has_warn` (as LS-3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `patched_at_step`, `seam_id` (0 = none), `capped`. `SeamRecord`: `seam_id`, `section_a`, `section_b`, `q`, `hp`, `leak_bp`, `edge_ids[]`, `opening_ids[]`, `L_seam`, `built_step`, `mode_since_step`, `mode_key` (the seam weep's onset is `built_step`, so `mode_since_step` starts there; a later BREACH pre-stage or `sev` change on the seam restamps it like any other subject; a seam has no `isolated_at_step`, because no valve isolates a weep) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*, `state`.

**LS-6 — Compartments, atmosphere and who owns what.** A COMPARTMENT is the Graph's model of a Layout room, keyed by Layout `room_id` (`layout_room_ref`). **Layout owns** room identity, polygon, `V` (tiles) and `exposed_tiles`; the Graph caches `V` and `exposed_tiles` as derived fields (not hashed; rebuilt in `post_restore`, which is why `restore_after: [layout.state]`). **The Graph owns** the atmosphere — `n_o2`, `n_inert`, `T`, `t_rem`, `fuel_puddle_ml` — and every opening/hatch `area` and `door_state`, every VALVE `open` and every breaker `closed`; Interaction issues the commands, the Graph publishes the mutators (R11; one writer per fact, kernel H3). Derived each run: `P`, `p_o2`, `o2_pm`, `band_o2`, `band_p`, `band_t`, `pressure_trend` (D.3); the previous run's three bands and `P` (`band_*`, `p_prev`) are the hysteresis memory and are persisted (LS-21) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, as are the three warn trackers `warn_since_o2/p/t` and `warn_bits` (LS-13 (b)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. Scope of the physics (R14): pressure from `n` and `V` only (no T → P coupling), exterior always vacuum (`P_EXT` 0) with one radiative sink `T_AMB_MC`, no CO2, humidity or advected heat (heat moves only by conduction through openings and hull), no global rate dial — the constants are tuned, not scaled. The couplings the player reads are physical chains through these fields: scrubber (needs POWER) → pool → vent → `n_o2`; crew → `draw_air` → `n_o2`; opening → D.1 and D.7; breach → D.2 → D.1; machine → `THERMAL_COUPLE` → `Q_src_room`; coolant → D.8 → radiator; power loss → tier shedding → scrubbers/pumps/heater stop; cold → coolant FREEZE → burst on thaw; seam → weep; patch → vent and make-up → pressure recovers. **Air has two species and each is regulated to its own setpoint** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `n_o2` to the vent's `SP` (default 210 dkPa) and `n_inert` to `SP_IN` = `STD_PRESSURE_DKPA − STD_O2_PP_DKPA` = 790 dkPa. Respiration removes O2 only, while every leak and every vent-to-space removes both species, so a room that has lost gas could never return to NORMAL pressure on O2 alone — the first draft's gap, in which total pressure could only fall and one breach damaged the ship for good (Pillar 5). The O2 comes from the pool (tank stock plus scrubber), the inert from the scrubber's demand-driven make-up (D.5, accounted in `produced_mgu`); both reach a room only through a vent with a live `path_cap`, so V5 and a cut duct still isolate a room. *Alternatives considered:* the literal "tanks and vents carry standard air" — rejected, it needs 4.76 mGU of pool per mGU of O2 against a 600 mGU/step vent, so one crew's respiration (300) would outrun the supply; a finite inert reserve in each tank — rejected for MVP by the user's confirmation of the scrubber make-up (2026-10-27, Open Question 30 resolved) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. *Alternative considered:* per-tile atmosphere so frost can creep tile by tile — rejected (TD R4): it multiplies N by 20–60 per room; spatial gradients are presentation derived from the true compartment values plus the leak anchor and `since_steps`, which still honours P1 because the frost reflects the true temperature.

**LS-7 — Bands and thresholds (the single source of truth).** The Graph publishes these; Suit, Failure & Trail, Instruments and Adaptive Audio consume them and never redefine them. Hysteresis `H_P` 5 dkPa / `H_T` 500 mC on re-entry (D.3). Registry candidates in bold are the constants Suit reads for damage onset: **`COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` = −15000**, **`HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC` = 45000**, **`HYPOXIA_PP` = 140**, **`DECOMP_P` = 400**; the O2 band edges 180 (NORMAL/THIN, registry candidate `O2_BAND_THIN_PP`) and 100 (HYPOXIC/ANOXIC, registry candidate `O2_BAND_ANOXIC_PP`) are published with them, read by name by the HUD & Suit Layer *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from hud-and-suit-layer)* (suit insulation shifts the cold onset; that is Suit's formula over this constant).

| O2 partial pressure `p_o2` (dkPa) | Band | Felt as (Suit owns the exact effect) |
|---|---|---|
| ≥ 180 | NORMAL | none |
| 140..179 | THIN | breath audio shortens *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from suit-and-equipment)* |
| 100..139 | HYPOXIC (`HYPOXIA_PP` 140 is the boundary) | stamina loss (Suit's walk-only state, SE-27) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from suit-and-equipment)* |
| < 100 | ANOXIC | rapid incapacitation |

| Total pressure `P` (dkPa) | Band | Felt as |
|---|---|---|
| ≥ 700 | NORMAL | none |
| 400..699 | LOW | draught strong at openings *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from suit-and-equipment)* |
| 100..399 | DECOMP (`DECOMP_P` 400 is the boundary) | suit required; pull toward the hole |
| < 100 | VACUUM | — |

| Temperature `T` (mC) | Band | Felt as |
|---|---|---|
| ≥ 70000 | SCORCH | equipment damage, burn |
| 40000..69999 | HOT (`HEAT_DMG_ONSET_MC` 45000 inside it) | sweat, heat shimmer |
| 10000..39999 | COMFORT | none |
| 0..9999 | COOL | breath fog |
| −15000..−1 | FROST | frost on surfaces, shivering |
| −40000..−15001 | FREEZING (`COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` −15000 is the boundary) | cold damage for the unprotected |
| < −40000 | DEEP_COLD | — |

Failure thresholds live in the same table family: `HP_OK` 7000, `HP_FAIL` 3000, `LEAK_HP` 4000, `BREACH_HP` 4000, `FREEZE_WARN_MC` −10000, `FREEZE_ON_MC` −20000, `FREEZE_OFF_MC` −10000, `OVERHEAT_ON_MC` 70000, `OVERHEAT_OFF_MC` 50000, `OVERHEAT_ON_STEPS` 50, `OVERHEAT_SCRAM_STEPS` 300, `FUEL_STARVE_STEPS` 100, `RUPTURE_HP` 3600, `PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000. Time scale anchors (D.15): a door-sized breach decompresses a 20-tile room to LOW in 5.6 s; a pinhole takes 6 min to ANOXIC with one crew; a breached room frosts in 4 min and freezes its pipes in 12 min, while a healthy ship on one 500 W heater stays at COMFORT indefinitely and an unpowered one fogs breath at 6 min, frosts at 13 and — with no hull hole at all — freezes its liquid pipes at 41 min (FREEZE pre-stage at 23 min), the intentional dark-ship clock *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; one battery cell runs the life-critical tiers for 6 min.

**LS-8 — Three hooks, twelve phases, fixed order.** The Graph registers three real step hooks at divisor 6 with offsets 0 (power), 2 (atmo) and 4 (failure) — **the staggered baseline** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* — still chained by `after:` (kernel CR-3, `hook_runs_this_tick` = `(T mod divisor) == offset`) — the two chain edges (`graph.propagate.atmo` after `.power`, `.failure` after `.atmo`) are declared `{hook_id, tolerates_stale: true}`: at offsets 0 / 2 / 4 each pair never runs in one tick and CR-3 warns on an undeclared never-co-running edge; the edge still fixes the resolved order. A same-tick reader shares the offset of the hook it follows (Suit (6, 2) with `.atmo`, Attention (6, 0) with `.power`); a reader at another offset (Sanity (12, 0)) declares `tolerates_stale` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: `graph.propagate.power` (root; phases 0–3), `graph.propagate.atmo` (`after: [graph.propagate.power]`; phases 4–7), `graph.propagate.failure` (`after: [graph.propagate.atmo]`; phases 8–11). One run = one step = `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` 6 ticks = 0.1 s; `step_no = ⌊T / 6⌋` for all three hooks (they run on T = 6n, 6n + 2 and 6n + 4), persisted as the per-hook cursors `last_step_power`, `last_step_atmo` and `last_step_failure` (`last_step_no` is the last step whose failure hook has run, i.e. the last complete step) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Dependents name the hook they read: Suit vitals `after: graph.propagate.atmo`, Attention `after: graph.propagate.power`; Failure & Trail is presentation and reads dirty keys. Because the kernel has no alias concept, this is CR-3 as written. The stagger is the **baseline**, not a fallback *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the three hooks spread over three of the six ticks, so no tick carries more than one Graph hook (the home ship alone is estimated at 825 µs per step at the review's coefficients against a 0.5 ms share, so the unstaggered tick cannot meet the share; the coefficients are [UNV] and S0-f decides the number, not the structure). A reader must share the offset of the hook it follows or see a run-old value (kernel edge case): Suit's vitals register at offset 2, Attention at 0, Emitter State reads at offset 4 — cross-GDD registrations, applied 2026-10-28: Suit SE-1/A12/AC-SE-2/AC-SE-3 at (6, 2), Attention (6, 0), Emitter State reads after `.failure`, the kernel rows and fixtures, the Overlay per-hook-tick sample, Assembly tick-order note, Interaction lag note, Sanity stale edges (Open Question 41). **Data-only revert:** if S0-f shows the stagger buys nothing, the three Graph offsets and Suit become 0 (two registrations); Suit A12 compares offsets, not values, so both states boot; no Graph rule changes *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. **Topology refresh is one idempotent function** run at the head of whichever Graph hook runs first after a structural mutator (M1-M5): power (`T mod 6` = 0), atmo (2) or failure (4), not only at phase 1; a weld, cut, discard or install applied in P2 on any tick is seen whole by the next Graph hook and no hook reads a half-applied topology; zero cost while `topo_dirty` is clear *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. The order of effects within a step is unchanged (power, then atmo, then failure, then the next step's phase 0), so no rule of this document depends on the stagger. The phases:

| Phase | Hook | Work | Order | Reads | Writes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | power | Apply queued unconditional inputs: `draw_air` requests, footprint impulses, `set_load_request` changes, `forced_mode_next` → `forced_mode` | ascending target id | the three persisted input queues (LS-21), `forced_mode_next` | node/compartment fields; the queues are emptied *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| 1 | power | Topology refresh if `topo_dirty` (one idempotent function, run at the head of whichever Graph hook runs first after a structural mutator M1-M5 — power, atmo or failure — so a mutation applied in P2 on any tick is seen whole by the next hook; zero cost while clear) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: components per loop, power trees rooted at the lowest node id, `path_cap`, degrees, `deg_max` | by loop, lowest id roots | edges | derived caches only |
| 2 | power | POWER solve per island (D.6): HEATER thermostat scaling of the request (D.7), reactor ramp, inrush, tiers, battery flow limited by `BATT_DIS_CAP`, each source's delivery in merit order (reactors first, then cells) with the `avail` = 0 guard *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; re-review)* | island by lowest node id | nodes | `served_pm` cache, `stock`, `out_w`, fuel burn request |
| 3 | power | FUEL: burn `out_w × FUEL_UML_PER_W` µmL per step, added to both `fuel_rem` and `burned_uml`, whole mL leaving `stock_ml` as `fuel_rem` crosses 10^6 (120 µmL per W per step → 300,000 µmL = 0.3 mL/step at 2500 W; a 20,000 mL tank lasts 111 min); transfer tank → burner limited by `path_cap`; `starve_steps` counts runs with no fuel delivered *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | tank id ascending | stocks | `stock_ml`, `fuel_rem`, `burned_uml`, `starve_steps` |
| 4 | atmo | O2: scrubber outputs, vent injections, duct/tank leaks (D.5, D.2) | scrubber, vent, then leaking subject id | pool, compartments | O2 stocks; compartment `n_o2` deltas (buffered) |
| 5 | atmo | COOLANT: `circ`, capture, radiator, `t_cool`, pipe/reservoir leaks, `T_mach` (D.8) | reservoir id | | `t_cool`, `tc_rem`, `stock_ml`, `spilled_cool_ml`, `spilled_heat_mdj`; room heat inputs (buffered) |
| 6 | atmo | Atmosphere exchange: stored OPENINGs, virtual holes, seam weeps (D.1) from the start-of-phase snapshot | edge id ascending, then hull id, then seam id | snapshot `n` | `n_o2`/`n_inert` (all deltas applied at the end), `vented_mgu` |
| 7 | atmo | Thermal step (D.7): opening conduction from the snapshot, hull loss, machine and heater input, remainder carry | compartment id | buffered `Q` | `T`, `t_rem`, `radiated_dj` |
| 8 | failure | Damage: overload (D.9), wear on period steps, footprint GOUGE/RUPTURE already applied in phase 0; `hp` floors from the gate (D.11) | edge id, then node id | `load`, modes, flags | `hp` |
| 9 | failure | State evaluation: `state` from `hp`, mode triggers and pre-stages from thresholds, `warn_since_step`, `fail_since_step`, `sev`; then the compartment warn trackers (LS-13 (b)), compartment id ascending after nodes and edges *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | node id, then edge id, then compartment id | `hp`, `T`, `T_mach`, `served`, compartment bands *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* | compartment warn trackers (`warn_since_o2/p/t`, `warn_bits`), `state`, `forced_mode` (gated), `warn_since_step`, `fail_since_step`, and the GA-T onset stamps: `mode_since_step` and `mode_key` (restamped on the run the subject's dominant descriptor changes mode or `sev`, from the persisted `mode_key`), `isolated_at_step` (stamped when the closed-valve rule first drops a flow-fraction descriptor's `intensity_pm` to 0, cleared when the valve reopens, restamped with `mode_since_step` on a `sev` change while isolated) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)* |
| 10 | failure | Cascade rules (LS-12) evaluated on this run's phase-9 states; effects queued to `forced_mode_next`, bounded by `MAX_SPREAD_PER_STEP` in ascending subject id | rule id, subject id | states | `forced_mode_next` (persisted) |
| 11 | failure | Emit: dirty keys (LS-20), descriptor list (LS-14, including the derived `mode_steps` and `grow_steps` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*), `block_hash` dirty flag | — | — | dirty marks, derived list |

The one-run delay on cascades (phase 10 queues, phase 0 of the next run applies) removes any dependence on update order inside a run and gives every hop a minimum latency of 0.1 s, so a cascade reads as a sequence, never as an instant. Cross-loop coupling therefore has up to one run of latency per hop; nothing in this GDD assumes same-tick cross-loop consistency, so the VS-A stagger changes no rule.

**LS-9 — Randomness is keyed, never counted.** The only stochastic rules are thaw-burst (C5) and, in VS-A, spark ignition (C9). Each draw is `rng_derive(world_seed, "graph.fail", (step_no << 20) | subject_id)` — the registry form is `mix64(world_seed XOR mix64(fnv64(stream_name)) XOR (counter * 0x9E3779B97F4A7C15))` — in GDScript the multiplier is written as the signed int `-7046029254386353131`, because 0x9E3779B97F4A7C15 exceeds int64 and S0-b probes how the literal parses *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* with "counter (u64 stored in the owning block)" *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from command-simulation-layer)*; the Graph supplies the key as that counter, so the draw is a pure function of state, order-independent, and unchanged by weld, cut, restore or content added elsewhere. `subject_id < 2^20` is asserted (`MAX_GRAPH_NODES` 4096 and `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES` 8192 fit with room), leaving 43 bits of `step_no`. The clarification that "a stored running counter is one option; any owner-defined 63-bit key is valid" is a **routed amendment to the kernel's D.10**, not this GDD's rule (R4). *Alternative considered:* a sequential counter in the block — rejected (TD R10): a weld adds candidates and the draws of an unrelated derelict change. **Replay is exact and not steerable (user ruling D16 / X8, 2026-10-27)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*: the Graph's physical failures and persisted counters replay exactly after a restore — a thaw-burst roll is a pure function of `(world_seed, step_no, subject_id)` and the persisted state, the Graph holds no restore-epoch input and reads no epoch (nor `PERCEPTION_OUT`, LS-22), and a reload therefore produces the identical roll, the identical burst or non-burst, at the identical step. Quitting or dying and reloading cannot reroll a Graph outcome. The threat draws of Attention and Horror Entities are re-keyed per restore epoch by their own owners (X8); that is their rule and the Graph neither supplies nor consumes the epoch; the epoch's storage and advance rules (delegated ruling R1 as amended by R13 / R14: a Continue of the newest QUIT generation keeps the epoch, death restores and other Continues advance it, held in Save/Load's per-slot `slot_epoch.bin` and never in a generation file) are Save/Load's and the kernel's, and change no Graph byte *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R13 / R14)*. **The thaw-burst roll window** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: the roll window is persisted state, never inferred. When a FREEZE clears (phase 9, `T_room > FREEZE_OFF_MC`) the subject's `thaw_until_step` is set to `step_no + THAW_ROLL_STEPS` (default 100). In phase 8 of every later run with `step_no ≤ thaw_until_step` — exactly `THAW_ROLL_STEPS` runs — the subject rolls once: the draw is `rng_derive` as above and the burst happens iff `(draw & 0x7FFFFFFFFFFFFFFF) % 1000 < BURST_P_PM`. A burst sets `hp = min(hp, BURST_HP_TO)` (default 3500, strictly between `HP_FAIL` and `LEAK_HP`, so a burst always starts a LEAK and never FAILs the pipe by itself — the first draft's `hp −= 3000` left a healthy 10000 pipe at exactly `HP_OK` 7000, which is no LEAK at all, so the "readable cost of growth" was invisible on the home ship), is ENDOGENOUS (the FREEZE pre-stage has held the D.11 gate open) and **sets `thaw_until_step` to 0: a success closes the window and the closure is persisted**, so a restore on any tick neither re-rolls a spent window nor forgets an open one. A window that runs out unspent is set to 0 on the first run with `step_no > thaw_until_step`; a re-freeze (forced FAILED again) closes it at once, and the next thaw opens a new one. P(burst in a window) = `1 − (1 − BURST_P_PM / 1000)^THAW_ROLL_STEPS` = 77.9 % at 15 ‰ and 100 steps, mean 6.7 s from thaw.

**LS-10 — Failure lifecycle: six states, five severities.** States: `0 OK` (`hp ≥ HP_OK`, no forced mode), `1 DEGRADED` (`HP_FAIL < hp < HP_OK`; `eff` from D.4; hazard modes may be active; with none active the subject carries mode WORN, LS-11 — the module-body trace *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*), `2 FAILED` (`hp ≤ HP_FAIL` or `forced_mode ≠ 0`; no function; leaks continue; repairable), `3 DESTROYED` (`hp` = 0; permanent scrap; repair rejected `NOT_REPAIRABLE`; salvage then replace), `4 SALVAGED` (removed by `salvage_node`; tombstone slot; fluid ports `open_end`), `5 ABSENT` (never populated; inert; no ports connected; no descriptors). Severity `sev` 0..4 from D.4 (0 none; 1 trace — the weep, the flicker, the frost on the lagging; 2 minor; 3 major — hazard to nearby play, first `alarm_eligible` level; 4 critical — arcing, jetting, runaway), raised by hazard rules (breach `A_hole` ≥ 32 → 4; OVERLOAD `ρ` > 1000 → ≥ 1 from the first step over (LS-11) and `ρ` ≥ 1500 → 3; any forced mode → ≥ 3). Damage is monotonic per cause; repair raises `hp` only through `repair_step` up to `hp_cap`. The complete transition table is in *States and Transitions*.

**LS-11 — Failure modes, triggers, pre-stages.** Mode ids: `1 LEAK` (DUCT, PIPE, tanks, reservoir; `hp < LEAK_HP`; clears when `hp ≥ LEAK_HP` or isolated by a closed VALVE upstream — isolated means the leak draws from nothing, the mode stays, the descriptor's `intensity_pm` is 0, and the subject loses no hp to leak wear while it is 0 (D.9) — a re-opened valve resumes the leak from the stored `hp` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from derelict-authoring-handmade-kits)*; the run the closed valve first drops the descriptor's `intensity_pm` to 0 stamps `isolated_at_step` = `step_no`, and the run the valve reopens (or the mode clears) resets it to 0 — the freeze point of the trail's `grow_steps`, LS-14 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*), `2 SHORT` (CABLE, CABLE_JUNCTION, LOAD; `hp ≤ HP_FAIL` — the FAILED boundary of LS-10, not a strict `<` — **while energised**: a CABLE is energised while an island at either of its end nodes holds a live source (`S + D` > 0, D.6), a CABLE_JUNCTION while an island at the far end of any incident cable does, a LOAD while `served_pm` > 0 (powered and drawing). Energised is NOT "carries watts": a FAILED cable has `cap` 0 and carries none, which is why the test is the live source behind it, the same test as the D.9 burn guard. Evaluated in phase 9 from the run's phase-2 island results and persisted as the mode flag, so it is set on the very run the subject lands on `HP_FAIL`; clears on repair (`hp > HP_FAIL`) or when no end island holds a live source (the upstream breaker opened, M9); never set on an authored dead cable or junction with no source behind it; a FAILED CABLE in SHORT keeps burning at `SHORT_BURN_BP_PER_STEP` until then, D.9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — confirming review, blocker 1)*, `3 OVERHEAT` (REACTOR, LOAD machine, RESERVOIR; `T_mach > OVERHEAT_ON_MC` for `OVERHEAT_ON_STEPS`; clears at `T_mach < OVERHEAT_OFF_MC`; pre-stage: `T_mach` in HOT → sev 2 shimmer), `4 FREEZE` (coolant PIPE, RESERVOIR, liquid tanks; **pre-stage** sev 1 "frost on the lagging" at `T_room < FREEZE_WARN_MC`; forced FAILED at `T_room < FREEZE_ON_MC` once the D.11 gate is open; clears at `T_room > FREEZE_OFF_MC`, which opens the persisted thaw window and its burst rolls, LS-9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `5 BREACH` (HULL_SEGMENT, SEAM; `hp < BREACH_HP`, gated; pre-stage is the sev-1 hairline at `hp < HP_OK`; clears when `hp ≥ BREACH_HP`), `6 STARVE` (any powered node with `req_w > 0` and `served_pm < 1000`; a status, not a state change — it exists so the player can tell "off" from "broken"; its `sev` is the subject's D.4 `sev` (hp-derived, no STARVE-specific raise) floored at 1 while the status is live, so a live status is never a sev-0 descriptor, and it is never `alarm_eligible` (`alarm_capable(6)` is false, LS-13 (a)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*), `7 OVERLOAD` (CABLE; `ρ > 1000`; sev 1 from the first step over — the hum climbs, the box warms — which is the warning that opens the window for the SHORT/FAILED that follows; `warn_since_step` is set on that first step, so the D.11 window opens there and not at the `HP_OK` crossing, and every D.11 figure is computed from this rule *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*), `8 SEVERED` (edge or port cut or salvaged with `open_end`; clears by `cap_port`), `9 SCRAM` (REACTOR; OVERHEAT sustained `OVERHEAT_SCRAM_STEPS` or fuel starve `FUEL_STARVE_STEPS`; clears by `reset_node` when `T_mach < OVERHEAT_OFF_MC` and fuel is present), `10 WORN` *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* (any node or edge with `hp < HP_OK`, no mode 1–9 live on it and no patch on it — a patched subject emits PATCH_WEEP instead *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* — the un-modal trace of a worn module, the one a subject losing hp to duty wear or a GOUGE carries when nothing leaks, shorts or freezes; `sev` from D.4, `medium` NONE, `acoustic` NONE, never `alarm_eligible` (`alarm_capable(10)` is false by definition, LS-13 (a): a FAILED or DESTROYED module with no live mode 1-9 carries WORN at `sev` 3-4 and stays quiet whatever its gate) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*; it is the module-body channel of LS-14 — readable on the sprite and the gauge, and on the inspect card only through the module's reduced output reading — the card carries no wear frame, word or mark *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view)* — corruptible by Perception — and it counts toward `warn_since_step` like any `sev ≥ 1` descriptor; a subject emits WORN only while no mode 1–9 and no PATCH_WEEP is live on it, a hazard mode replaces it, and it clears at `hp ≥ HP_OK`). The closed-hatch descriptor `SEAL_STRAIN` is an OPENING descriptor (id 26, LS-14, D.14), the closed sibling of `AIRFLOW`, not a subject mode; it is never emitted for an OPENING with an EXTERIOR endpoint *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. **`PATCH_WEEP` (descriptor-only id 27)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (creative-director ruling: a patch weeps): a subject with `hp_cap < HP_FULL` (patched), `hp < HP_OK` and no mode 1–9 live emits PATCH_WEEP instead of WORN — sev 1, on the **consequence channel**: `medium` by the subject's loop (GAS for a DUCT or O2_TANK, COOLANT or FUEL for a PIPE, RESERVOIR or FUEL_TANK, ELECTRIC for a CABLE, junction or LOAD, STRUCT for the rest), `acoustic` HISS / DRIP / TICK accordingly, `intensity_pm` 0 and never silenced by it (LS-14 provenance: no Graph-defined `intensity_pm`). It carries no flow — LEAK stays cleared at `hp ≥ LEAK_HP` — but it witnesses the patch itself: `hp_cap` is Graph truth, so a hallucinated "fully repaired" sprite or inspect card cannot hide a patch that is about to fail (P1, P4), and the sev-1 window it opens is the one the gated re-leak of R15 relies on. A patched hull or seam already carries the mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage and emits no PATCH_WEEP. Every mode that can force FAILED has a pre-stage at sev ≥ 1 that necessarily precedes it by more than `W_MIN_STEPS`, so LS-13 never has to invent a warning.

**LS-12 — Cascades are physical chains, one hop per run, bounded.** No scripted "if A fails then B fails" exists; the chains below are what the numbers do, listed so that content, tests and the fairness audit can name them. Phase 10 applies at most `MAX_SPREAD_PER_STEP` (32) new forced modes per run in ascending subject id; the rest wait a run, so a failure front moves at a bounded speed — good for cost, better for dread.

| # | Chain | Mechanism | Typical time (D.15) | MVP |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | Cable overload → cable FAILED (`cap` 0) → island splits → downstream STARVE; the FAILED cable burns on in SHORT to DESTROYED unless the breaker is opened (D.9 short burn) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | D.9; phase 1 re-derives the tree | 1.5–4 min by `ρ` to FAILED; DESTROYED 750 steps later at the default burn | yes |
| C2 | Power loss → scrubber off (tier 0 is shed last) → pool stops refilling → rooms fall through THIN/HYPOXIC on respiration | D.6, D.5 | per crew in a sealed 20-tile room once the pool is spent: THIN 3.3, HYPOXIC 7.8, ANOXIC 12.2 and no O2 left at 23.3 min (the 23.3 min is the time to empty, not to THIN) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | yes |
| C3 | Power loss → pump off → `circ` 0 → `T_mach` climbs → OVERHEAT → SCRAM | D.8, D.6 | 35 s after `circ` 0 (pre-staged by HOT) | yes |
| C4 | Coolant PIPE leak → reservoir falls → cavitation below `L_CAV_LO` → C3 | D.2, D.8 | 9 min | yes |
| C5 | Cold room (breached, or a derelict authored cold) → FREEZE pre-stage → coolant FREEZE (forced FAILED) → `circ` 0 → C3; on thaw each frozen subject rolls `BURST_P_PM` on each run of its persisted `THAW_ROLL_STEPS` window (LS-9) until one succeeds, which sets `hp = min(hp, BURST_HP_TO)` → LEAK *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | D.7, LS-11 | 12 min to freeze a breached room (pre-stage at 7); burst within ~7 s of thaw at 15 ‰ (78 % within the 100-step window) | yes |
| C6 | Hull breach → room `P` falls → stored OPENINGs carry the loss to neighbours as a wave | D.2, D.1 | seconds to minutes by hole | yes |
| C7 | Breach or cold → `T` falls toward `T_amb` (holes add conductive area) → C5 | D.7 | 4–10 min | yes |
| C8 | Exposed DUCT leak → pool vents to space → vents starve → rooms sag | D.2, D.5 | 9 min to pool empty | yes |
| C9 | SHORT sparks ignite a fuel puddle | keyed roll `SPARK_P_PM` when SHORT and `fuel_puddle_ml > 0` in the same compartment | VS-A (R8: no fire in MVP) | no |
| C10 | STRUT failure redistributes load → overload ripple | D.9 on STRUT | VS-A | no |

**LS-13 — The fairness gate is a sim rule.** `W_MIN_WARNING_S` = 10 (`W_MIN_STEPS` 100) is a hard rule of this block (R7) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN: raised from 8 s to 10 s)*: an **endogenous** transition to FAILED or DESTROYED (node or edge) and a hull or seam LEAK → BREACH escalation require that the subject has emitted a descriptor of `sev ≥ 1` for at least 100 consecutive steps (`warn_elapsed`, D.11). While the gate is closed `hp` is floored at `HP_FAIL + 1` (or `BREACH_HP` for the breach case) and the excess damage is discarded. **Exogenous** causes bypass the gate: player verbs (cut, vent, salvage, overload by breaker — the damage of the `INRUSH_STEPS` after a breaker close is tagged EXOGENOUS, D.9) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, footprint-channel RUPTURE and GOUGE, and `apply_damage` with `cause = EXOGENOUS`. **Exported requirement — exogenous sources carry their own tell** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: the gate protects against the sim, not against agency, so it never delays an exogenous apply; every non-player exogenous source — M17 RUPTURE and GOUGE from Horror Entities and M17 STRESS from Attention (its only caller) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from horror-entities, attention-system)*, and environmental M18 `apply_damage(…, EXOGENOUS)` — must therefore be preceded by that system's own Pillar 2 tell of 10–30 s (the stalk, the scrape, the rhythm change) stated in its GDD. The Graph neither emits nor checks that tell; a source that applies exogenous damage with no tell is in breach of this requirement, which the (review) criterion in Section H discharges. Player death is Suit's rule; the Graph guarantees that the *cause* — the room's band, the module's failure — was warned. `alarm_eligible` on a descriptor is true only for an alarm-capable mode (LS-13 (a) below), at `sev ≥ ALARM_SEV_MIN` (3 by default) **and** with the gate open *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*, so alarm-class presentation (the rotating sweep) is never the first channel; exported requirement to Perception Filter, Sanity & Hallucination and Failure & Trail (R17): no consumer may suppress a consequence-channel descriptor's acoustic or physical channel (LS-14: any descriptor with `medium ≠ NONE`; a WORN descriptor is module-body and is the Filter's to corrupt *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*), a filter may add or alter sprites and gauges but never remove a hiss, a cold or a thin-air, and `alarm_eligible` never first. **Alarm capability, compartment gate, scope, exemption** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; Blocker 2: the owner adds the surface, the consumers' ruled behaviour — Suit `suit.alarm`, HUD sixth mark, Audio annunciator — is the spec, G6)*: **(a) Capability.** `alarm_capable(mode)` is true for modes 1 LEAK, 2 SHORT, 3 OVERHEAT, 4 FREEZE, 5 BREACH, 7 OVERLOAD, 8 SEVERED, 9 SCRAM and 20-23, and FALSE BY DEFINITION, whatever `ALARM_SEV_MIN` (range 2-4), for 6 STARVE, 10 WORN, 24 AIRFLOW, 25 PUDDLE, 26 SEAL_STRAIN and 27 PATCH_WEEP *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* (creative-director ruling under G6 / R20: 10 WORN and 6 STARVE leave the capable set). WORN is the module-body channel (`medium` NONE, `acoustic` NONE, corruptible by the Filter) and takes `sev` 3-4 from D.4 on any FAILED or DESTROYED module with no live hazard mode (a FAILED scrubber at `hp` 2000 that is switched off, `req_w` 0, is WORN `sev` 4), so keying the alarm on it would sound the klaxon, the Suit `suit.alarm` bit and the HUD sixth mark for a dead, silent fault through a channel the Filter may corrupt — a breach of P2 and of the two-channel rule. STARVE is the status that tells "off" from "broken" (every load on a dead branch carries it, so a FAILED load on an unserved branch, the Derelict A boarding case, carries STARVE and not WORN; the commonest cause is a breaker the player opened): it is a status, not a hazard to nearby play, and its `sev` is the subject's D.4 `sev` floored at 1 while it is live (LS-11). A module with no live hazard therefore never raises the ship alarm, even with an open gate (including an authored back-dated `warn_since_step`); the same module with a live mode 1-5 or 7-9 (a forced FREEZE, a LEAK, a BREACH, or SHORT on a FAILED load that is powered and drawing) does, at `sev` >= `ALARM_SEV_MIN` once its gate is open — WORN yields to the hazard mode (LS-11), so the alarm of a worn-out machine begins when its fault becomes a hazard. OPENING descriptors therefore need no gate input; the alarm presentation of a decompression rides the room's ATMO_DECOMP (LS-14 already says so). **(b) Compartment gate input.** Each compartment carries three persisted warn trackers, one per band state machine: `o2` (drives ATMO_THIN: THIN 1, HYPOXIC 3, ANOXIC 4), `p` (ATMO_DECOMP: LOW 2, DECOMP 3, VACUUM 4) and `t` (ATMO_COLD: COOL 1, FROST 2, FREEZING 3, DEEP_COLD 4; ATMO_HOT: HOT 2, SCORCH 4; exclusive). Tracker x = `warn_since_x` (i32) plus bit x of `warn_bits` (u8). In phase 9, compartment id ascending after nodes and edges, with `sev_x` = severity of the descriptor `band_x` currently maps to (0 if none): `sev_x` ≥ 1 and bit clear → set the bit, `warn_since_x` = `step_no`; `sev_x` = 0 → clear the bit, `warn_since_x` = 0. A worsening (THIN → HYPOXIC) does not reset the window; any return to OK does (the band hysteresis `H` is the only debounce). `warn_elapsed_x` = `step_no − warn_since_x` if the bit is set, else 0; `gate_open` = `warn_elapsed_x ≥ W_MIN_STEPS` (100). The gate delays no physics (a compartment has no `hp`); it only withholds `alarm_eligible`. M1 may supply back-dated `warn_since_x` for an authored derelict room as for nodes; absent one the tracker opens on the first run, so a room authored thin or empty is silent for its first 100 steps and the body cues come first (R7/R17). Cost: three compares per compartment per run. **(c) Alarm scope = the loop set.** The Graph publishes one pure read `alarm_sections() -> PackedInt32Array`: ascending, duplicate-free `section_id`s (`section_of(compartment_id)` of the descriptor) owning at least one descriptor with `alarm_eligible` true in the last committed run; empty = no alarm. The "seat's loop set" (Suit SE-29, HUD HS-25 / OQ-HS-79) and the "listener's body" (Audio AA-27) are ONE predicate: all six loops (0..5, compartment descriptors included) of every section of the body the seat or listener stands on; a consumer tests `alarm_sections()` ∩ `sections_of(body_of(section_id))` non-empty with Layout's pure reads. Consequences: a derelict breach IS in the seat's loop set (OQ-HS-79 (1): yes); a home-ship fault alarms a seat on the home body; a fault on another body does not. The read names no loop or place beyond sections the consumer already knows. **(d) CX-A1 — exemption.** The Graph's annunciator `ann` (Audio AA-27) is gated by `alarm_eligible`; Attention's breach-entry `attn` cadence (Attention AT-18, Audio AA-20) is Attention's own tell-contract event, not a Graph descriptor, and is preceded by a tell of at least `TELL_MIN_TICKS` by construction, so it is exempt from this gate and is never "the first warning". This closes CX-A1 (the consumers' alarm-gate lints, in the Audio and Attention GDDs, carry the exemption). *Alternative considered:* a content guideline rather than a sim gate — rejected (GD Q5): deaths must have a nameable cause in every build, not only in well-authored content.

**LS-14 — `FailureDescriptor` schema.** Derived each run in phase 11 from state (only `fail_since_step`, `warn_since_step` and the three GA-T onset fields `mode_since_step`, `mode_key` and `isolated_at_step` are persisted *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*); the Graph emits semantics, never visuals.

| Field | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `subject_kind` | enum NODE 1 / EDGE 2 / COMPARTMENT 3 / SEAM 4 / OPENING 5 | What is affected |
| `subject_id` | u32 | Stable id |
| `compartment_id` | u32 | Room where it manifests (Layout turns this into pixels) |
| `anchor` | `(node_id, port_local_index)` or `edge_id` | Where on the pipe or panel (Layout supplies the polyline) |
| `loop` | 0..5 | Which loop (0 for compartment descriptors) |
| `mode` | 1..10 (LS-11; 10 = WORN) or `ATMO_COLD` 20 / `ATMO_HOT` 21 / `ATMO_THIN` 22 / `ATMO_DECOMP` 23 / `AIRFLOW` 24 / `PUDDLE` 25 / `SEAL_STRAIN` 26 *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* / `PATCH_WEEP` 27 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | What is wrong |
| `medium` | NONE 0 (WORN only) / GAS 1 / COOLANT 2 / FUEL 3 / ELECTRIC 4 / HEAT 5 / STRUCT 6 *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | What comes out; NONE means nothing comes out — the module-body channel |
| `sev` | 0..4 | D.4 and hazard rules |
| `intensity_pm` | 0..1000 | Flow fraction (leak rate / cap; hole area / `A_HOLE_MAX`; `min(1000, |ΔP|)` in dkPa for AIRFLOW — 1 permille per dkPa, ≥ 1 whenever the descriptor is live *(Amended 2026-10-28 — confirming review, blocker 2)*) for particle density and hiss volume |
| `since_steps` | int | `step_no − fail_since_step` (scorch and frost accumulation timing) |
| `mode_steps` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings; Failure & Trail request GA-T, user ruling D30)* | int ≥ 0 | `step_no − mode_since_step`: the steps since the subject's descriptor entered its current mode **or last changed `sev`** (age of the current mode; read by Failure & Trail for dullness). 0 on the first run of a new mode or severity. COMPARTMENT and OPENING descriptors read 0 |
| `grow_steps` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings; GA-T)* | int ≥ 0 | `max(0, (isolated_at_step if isolated_at_step > 0 else step_no) − mode_since_step)`: `mode_steps` frozen at the isolation step while a closed valve isolates the descriptor (read by Failure & Trail for extent). Never negative. COMPARTMENT and OPENING descriptors read 0 |
| `warn_elapsed` | int | D.11; COMPARTMENT 20-23: the tracker's `warn_elapsed_x` (LS-13 (b)); OPENING 24 / 26 read 0 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `alarm_eligible` | bool | D.11; constant false for modes 24-27 (LS-13 (a)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| `cause` | ENDOGENOUS 0 / EXOGENOUS 1 | Last damage cause tag on the subject |
| `acoustic` | NONE 0 (WORN only) / HISS 1 / DRIP 2 / HUM_DROP 3 / CLANK 4 / WIND 5 / TICK 6 / SIZZLE 7 / CREAK 8 (a strained seal, SEAL_STRAIN) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | Audio class for Adaptive Audio; NONE means no sound is ever played for this descriptor |
| `dir` | 0..7, 8 = omni | Outflow direction hint from edge orientation |

**Normative mode table (loop, medium, acoustic, intensity source, LIVE predicate)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The first draft left the per-mode values of the fields above to the consumers, so the audible tells Pillar 2 depends on were undefined for OVERLOAD, SCRAM, STARVE and SEVERED. This table is the Graph's declaration; Failure & Trail keeps the rendering and Adaptive Audio the level and rate. Rows marked (proposed) are the Graph's choice where no sibling GDD had fixed one and are routed to Adaptive Audio and Failure & Trail to confirm or amend (Open Question 40). A descriptor is **LIVE** when its predicate holds, and then it is in `failures()`.

| mode | subject kinds | `loop` | `medium` | `acoustic` | `intensity_pm` source | LIVE predicate |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 LEAK | DUCT, O2_TANK | 1 | GAS | HISS | flow fraction (leak rate over the subject's cap); 0 when isolated by a closed valve | `hp < LEAK_HP` |
| 1 LEAK | PIPE (coolant), RESERVOIR | 3 | COOLANT | DRIP | as above | `hp < LEAK_HP` |
| 1 LEAK | PIPE (fuel), FUEL_TANK | 4 | FUEL | DRIP | as above | `hp < LEAK_HP` |
| 1 LEAK | SEAM weep | 1 | GAS | HISS | `max(1, min(1000, ⌊A_seam_m / A_HOLE_MAX⌋))` | `A_seam_m` > 0 |
| 2 SHORT | CABLE, junction, LOAD | 2 | ELECTRIC | SIZZLE | none (reads 0) | `hp ≤ HP_FAIL` while energised (LS-11: live source at an end of a CABLE / junction, `served_pm` > 0 for a LOAD), until repair (`hp > HP_FAIL`) or breaker open *(Amended 2026-10-28 — confirming review, blocker 1)* |
| 3 OVERHEAT | REACTOR, RESERVOIR; LOAD machine | 3; 2 | HEAT | SIZZLE (proposed) | none | `T_mach > OVERHEAT_ON_MC` for `OVERHEAT_ON_STEPS`; pre-stage HOT at sev 2 |
| 4 FREEZE | coolant PIPE, RESERVOIR; FUEL_TANK | 3; 4 | COOLANT; FUEL | TICK (proposed) | none | `T_room < FREEZE_WARN_MC` (pre-stage sev 1); forced FAILED at `FREEZE_ON_MC` after the gate |
| 5 BREACH | HULL_SEGMENT, SEAM | 5 | GAS (hole open); STRUCT (pre-stage) | WIND; TICK | `⌊1000 × A_hole / A_HOLE_MAX⌋`; none (pre-stage) | `hp < BREACH_HP`; `hp < HP_OK` |
| 6 STARVE | any powered node with `req_w > 0` | 2 | ELECTRIC | HUM_DROP | `1000 − served_pm` (≥ 1 while live, so never silenced) | `served_pm < 1000` |
| 7 OVERLOAD | CABLE | 2 | ELECTRIC | TICK (proposed: the box ticking as it warms; the climbing hum is Audio's running-loop layer, not a witness class) | none | `ρ > 1000` |
| 8 SEVERED | edge or port with `open_end` | the severed loop | by loop: GAS / COOLANT / FUEL / ELECTRIC | HISS / DRIP / DRIP / TICK (proposed) | `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` as a fraction of port cap | until `cap_port` |
| 9 SCRAM | REACTOR | 3 (overheat trip); 4 (starve trip) | HEAT; FUEL | CLANK (the trip) | none | `forced_mode` SCRAM |
| 10 WORN | any node or edge | the subject's | NONE | NONE | none | `hp < HP_OK`, no mode 1–9 live and no patch |
| 20 ATMO_COLD | COMPARTMENT | 0 | HEAT | TICK (proposed) | none | `band_t` ≤ COOL |
| 21 ATMO_HOT | COMPARTMENT | 0 | HEAT | SIZZLE (proposed) | none | `band_t` ≥ HOT |
| 22 ATMO_THIN | COMPARTMENT | 0 | GAS | HISS (proposed) | none | `band_o2` ≤ THIN |
| 23 ATMO_DECOMP | COMPARTMENT | 0 | GAS | WIND (proposed) | none | `band_p` ≤ LOW |
| 24 AIRFLOW | OPENING | 0 | GAS | WIND | `min(1000, abs(ΔP))` (integer dkPa, `ΔP = P_a − P_b` of the floored display pressures, D.3; ≥ 1 while live) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — confirming review, blocker 2)* | opening `area` > 0 and `ΔP` ≠ 0 |
| 25 PUDDLE | COMPARTMENT | 4 | FUEL | DRIP | `min(1000, fuel_puddle_ml)` | `fuel_puddle_ml` > 0 |
| 26 SEAL_STRAIN | OPENING between two COMPARTMENTs | 0 | GAS; HEAT | CREAK; TICK | `min(1000, abs(ΔP))` | D.14 closed-hatch rule |
| 27 PATCH_WEEP | patched node or edge | the subject's | GAS / COOLANT / FUEL / ELECTRIC / STRUCT by loop | HISS / DRIP / DRIP / TICK / TICK | none (0) | `hp_cap < HP_FULL`, `hp < HP_OK`, no mode 1–9 live |

STARVE is in neither the flow-fraction list nor the no-intensity list of the provenance paragraph below: its `intensity_pm` is the under-service `1000 − served_pm`, which is at least 1 whenever the status is live. Alarm capability by row (LS-13 (a)): rows 1-5, 7-9 and 20-23 are alarm-capable; rows 6 STARVE, 10 WORN and 24-27 are never `alarm_eligible`, whatever `ALARM_SEV_MIN`. STARVE's `sev` is the subject's D.4 `sev` floored at 1 while live (LS-11). *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* The table is the schema of record for the field-count and triple criterion in Section H.

Compartment descriptors: `ATMO_COLD` (band ≤ COOL; sev 1 COOL, 2 FROST, 3 FREEZING, 4 DEEP_COLD), `ATMO_HOT` (HOT 2, SCORCH 4), `ATMO_THIN` (THIN 1, HYPOXIC 3, ANOXIC 4), `ATMO_DECOMP` (LOW 2, DECOMP 3, VACUUM 4), `AIRFLOW` per OPENING with direction and `|ΔP|`, `PUDDLE` while `fuel_puddle_ml > 0`, `SEAL_STRAIN` per CLOSED OPENING between two COMPARTMENTs (`area` 0 — any interior door, hatch or seam hatch; never an OPENING with an EXTERIOR endpoint, which holds the full pressure difference for the whole game and would creak permanently on a healthy ship) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* while `|ΔP|` across it exceeds `WELD_DP_WARN` or `|ΔT|` exceeds `HATCH_DT_FROST_MC` (D.14) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. The rendering map is Failure & Trail's (informative: LEAK/GAS → hiss + mist; LEAK/COOLANT → drip + frost fan at every temperature, no mist *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from failure-and-trail-system)*; LEAK/FUEL → drip, ember-edged puddle; SHORT/OVERHEAT → scorch + glow; FREEZE → frost on the lagging; BREACH → hiss + wind + debris; STARVE → dim through `served_pm`; SEAL_STRAIN → creak and a bowed rim, rim frost when its `medium` is HEAT; PATCH_WEEP → a bead of the loop's medium at the patch (hiss, drip or tick by medium), sev 1; WORN → nothing but the body: sprite wear and gauge; the inspect card shows it only as reduced output).

**Two channels (P4)** *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. Every descriptor is on exactly one of two channels, fixed by its `mode`. The **consequence channel** is every COMPARTMENT, EDGE, SEAM and OPENING descriptor and every leak/hazard mode 1–9 and PATCH_WEEP (27) on a node — everything with `medium ≠ NONE`. It reaches the player only through a closed list: trail VFX (mist, drip, frost, scorch, debris, puddle), the `acoustic` classes played at the descriptor's anchor, Suit body cues from the bands (Suit SE-27's set: breath — the reserve valve click and the load creak are in the breath family —, visor fog, shiver, rime, stagger / walk-only / DOWNED, the seam vapour jet and the HEAVY back-frame; no vignette) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from suit-and-equipment, sanity-and-hallucination-rules, perception-filter)*, lamp brightness from `served_pm`, and the draught of `AIRFLOW` and the strain of `SEAL_STRAIN`. Perception may neither remove, alter nor add to a consequence-channel descriptor or to any item on that list. The **module-body channel** is a module's own body state — its sprite, its gauge, its inspect card and its WORN descriptors (`medium` NONE, `acoustic` NONE); Perception may corrupt it. The split is what makes LS-22 falsifiable: a lie can live only on the body channel, and the consequence channel is the witness against it. **TRUTH world-state witnesses that are not descriptors** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from sanity-and-hallucination-rules, perception-filter, modular-ship-assembly)*: pipe emission, flow chevrons, pipe pattern, pipe diameter and fitting silhouette, and the lever positions of every VALVE `open` and breaker `closed` — including the seam-lane PIPE/DUCT valve and CABLE breaker — are Graph-owned physical state (LS-6) drawn as world, never as module body, so Perception may not corrupt them (registry `truth_channel_union`, immune list). This is the answer to Assembly's G8: an open seam valve or a closed seam breaker at zero gradient needs no descriptor, because its sprite is TRUTH until Graph ASK G8 and F&T ASK FT1 publish a witness (registry `truth_channel_union`, immune list) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from perception-filter)*. The steady running hum of a served machine is not a separate list item; its loss is HUM_DROP, a listed class (whether the steady bed joins the list is the Perception Filter's call, Open Questions).

**Descriptor field provenance (FT-30)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from failure-and-trail-system, adaptive-audio-and-tells)*. `intensity_pm` is a flow fraction only for LEAK, the hole-area BREACH flow, AIRFLOW (`min(1000, |ΔP|)`, never 0 while the descriptor exists, so the closed-valve silence rule can never silence a live draught, as for the seam weep) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — confirming review, blocker 2)*, SEVERED (stub leak / port `cap`), the seam weep and SEAL_STRAIN GAS (`|ΔP|`); a flow-fraction descriptor at `intensity_pm` 0 is silent (the closed-valve rule). The ten ids `2 SHORT`, `7 OVERLOAD`, `3 OVERHEAT`, `9 SCRAM`, `4 FREEZE`, `20`–`23` and `27 PATCH_WEEP` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* have **no** Graph-defined `intensity_pm` (it reads 0 and means nothing): they are presented from `sev`, `since_steps` and a per-row LIVE predicate (the descriptor is in `failures()`), as is the mode-5 STRUCT pre-stage and the SEAL_STRAIN HEAT/TICK branch (whose `intensity_pm` measures `|ΔP|`, not its thermal cause); none of these is ever silenced by `intensity_pm` 0. `since_steps` is `step_no − fail_since_step` for a FAILED NODE/EDGE and `step_no − warn_since_step` otherwise; a SEAM descriptor counts from the SeamRecord's `built_step`; COMPARTMENT and OPENING descriptors (20–26) carry `since_steps`, `mode_steps` and `grow_steps` 0 at MVP; compartments 20-23 carry only the persisted gate onset of LS-13 (b), used for `warn_elapsed` and `alarm_eligible`; OPENING descriptors have none; spatial spread is derived from the true compartment values (LS-6) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. The seam weep is `subject_kind` SEAM, `mode` 1 LEAK, `medium` GAS, `acoustic` HISS, `sev` 1 while `A_seam_m > 0`, `intensity_pm = max(1, min(1000, ⌊A_seam_m / A_HOLE_MAX⌋))` with `A_seam_m` in milli flow units (D.2) — never 0 while the weep physically flows, so it is never silenced by the closed-valve rule *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. `25 PUDDLE` is `sev` 1, `medium` FUEL, `acoustic` DRIP, magnitude `intensity_pm = min(1000, fuel_puddle_ml)`. `24 AIRFLOW` is `sev` 1 below `WELD_DP_WARN` and 2 at or above it (alarm presentation rides the room's `ATMO_DECOMP`, never AIRFLOW). An OVERHEAT descriptor carries `loop` 3 (COOLANT) on a REACTOR or RESERVOIR and 2 (POWER) on a LOAD machine; a SCRAM carries 3 when tripped by OVERHEAT and 4 (FUEL) when tripped by starve. A FREEZE on a liquid tank that is not coolant (a FUEL_TANK) has `medium` FUEL. The BREACH pre-stage (hull or seam at `HP_OK` > `hp` ≥ `BREACH_HP`) is `mode` 5, `medium` STRUCT, `acoustic` TICK (the groan) at flow zero — consequence channel, never WORN. A patched subject at `hp < HP_OK` carries PATCH_WEEP (id 27, LS-11), a consequence-channel sev-1 tell, instead of WORN — Open Question 23 is closed *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

**GA-T — the descriptor time axis (`mode_steps`, `grow_steps`; user ruling D30, 2026-10-27)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. Failure & Trail asked for a per-mode age because `since_steps` counts from `warn_since_step` (a LEAK that was WORN for half an hour would arrive fully grown) or `fail_since_step`, and because the fairness gate guarantees every endogenous failure at least 100 steps of prior warning (LS-13), so every endogenously failed subject arrives with `since_steps` of 100 or more — 600 px of trail at sev 3 and 1500 px at sev 4 — and its onset can never be seen to *begin*. The ruling was to adopt the request unless it breaks a Graph invariant. The invariant check, one by one: **(a) fairness (LS-13, D.11)** — the gate reads `warn_since_step` and nothing else; GA-T adds fields beside it and never writes it, so `warn_elapsed`, `gate_open`, `alarm_eligible` and the ≥ 10 s window are unchanged, and `mode_steps` may be smaller than `warn_elapsed` (a `sev` rise restamps the former, never the latter). **(b) Determinism (LS-1, LS-21)** — the three new fields are persisted integers in `canonical_bytes()`, written only in phase 9 from persisted inputs; a restore reproduces them bit for bit. **(c) Falsifiability (LS-22)** — they are physical onsets (steps), not believed states, and no mutator takes them as an argument. **(d) Conservation and bounds (LS-1 (e)–(f))** — they hold step numbers below 2^53 and enter no flux. **(e) Dirty keys (LS-20)** — the age fields dirty no key by themselves; every restamp coincides with a mode or `sev` change, which already dirties the subject's key (Failure & Trail reads that change on `N/`, its FT-15 table), and Failure & Trail reads the age from its per-step poll cache. **Result: no invariant breaks; GA-T is adopted.**
