**Output Range:** `out_scr` 0..400 and `inj_o2 + inj_in` 0..600 at defaults; never negative, never more than the pool or the make-up offer holds, never past either setpoint, never more than the ducts carry. A healthy scrubber (400) outruns one crew (300) by 100 mGU/step; a half-degraded one (200) loses to one crew by 100 — the margin is deliberately thin so that scrubber integrity and scrubber power are both felt. With `path_cap` = 0 (closed valve, cut seam, FAILED duct) the vent is silent and the room is on its own air: the valve verb V5 has an immediate, audible consequence. **Recovery from a patched breach** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: a room that lost gas is refilled by its vent at 600 mGU/step shared between the species — O2 first, then inert make-up — and the scrubber's make-up is shared in ascending vent id. Fixture BREACH-RECOVERY (D.15): a `V` 20 room with its vent running and one crew breathing, a RUPTURE hole (`A` 7) open for 600 steps and then patched, reads P 664 / p_o2 145 at the patch, is back in NORMAL (P ≥ 705, p_o2 ≥ 185) after 2,712 steps (4.5 min, by O2 alone) and at both setpoints after about 22,000 steps (37 min); patched after 900 steps (P 542) it takes 18 min to NORMAL and 50 min to the setpoints — slow because one vent and one scrubber carry the whole refill, which is the cost of the breach and not a permanent state. A room with no live vent path keeps the pressure it has. With the scrubber FAILED or unpowered the pool's O2 still reaches the vent (tank stock, until it empties), so `p_o2` returns to its setpoint, but `mk_offer` is 0 and the inert deficit is never filled: total pressure tops out at the O2 setpoint plus the inert partial pressure the room still holds — NORMAL (P ≥ 705) is reachable only if the room kept about 63 % or more of its inert gas (the 600-step BREACH-RECOVERY room does and tops out near 729), otherwise the LOW or DECOMP band persists until the scrubber runs (C2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G4)*. The loss is real and recoverable (P5). Respiration is `R_BREATH × N_crew`, so with two crew the 600 mGU budget leaves 0 net O2 and with four the room falls even with the vent at full tilt (D.15 O2-ROOM-N4).
**Example:** scrubber `hp` 5000 (`eff` = 500), `sat` = 1000 → `out_scr` = `⌊400 × 500 × 1000 / 10^6⌋` = 200. Vent: `R_VENT` = 600, `eff_v` = 1000, `pool` = 1,500,000, `SP` = 210, `V` = 20, `n_o2` = 2,900,000, `n_inert` = 15,800,000 (the D.3 room: p_o2 145, inert partial 790), `path_cap` = 800 → `def_o2` = 4,200,000 − 2,900,000 = 1,300,000, `def_in` = 0 → `inj_o2` = `min(600, 1,500,000, 800, 1,300,000)` = 600, `inj_in` = 0. One crew in that room draws 300; net +300 mGU/step, so the room climbs toward 210 at 0.015 dkPa/step — about 7 min from THIN back to NORMAL on one vent. The same vent after a patched breach (`n_inert` = 10,380,000, P 664): `def_in` = 5,420,000 but `inj_in` = `min(600 − 600, …)` = 0 until the O2 deficit closes (step 4,333); the inert make-up then fills the rest at 600 mGU/step.

**D.6 Power allocation by tier — island balance and brownout**

The `power_allocation` formula is defined as:

`avail = S + D` ; `served_k = min( R_k , avail − Σ_{j<k} served_j )` for `k` = 0..4 in order ; `sat_k = ⌊1000 × served_k / R_k⌋` (`R_k` = 0 → 1000)

Derived *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `batt_used = max(0, Σ served − S)` drawn from BATTERY stocks in ascending node id, each cell limited to `min(BATT_DIS_CAP, stock_b)`; `charge = min(S − Σ served, CH_CAP, Σ_b (BATT_CELL − stock_b))` when there is surplus (`S > Σ served`), allocated across the batteries in ascending node id with each cell taking at most `BATT_CELL − stock_b`; the surplus that does not fit is not delivered anywhere (power has no inventory, and the reactor burns and heats by `out_w`, not by load), so a full cell stays at `BATT_CELL`, no energy is created and LS-24 never has an overfill to clamp (`graph_saturations` stays 0)
Derived: reactor output `S_reactor' = clamp(target, S_reactor − RAMP, S_reactor + RAMP)` where `target = out_w_rated × eff` (0 under SCRAM or fuel starve) — the ramp is why `out_w` is persisted
Derived: inrush — a LOAD re-energised within the last `INRUSH_STEPS` requests `⌊req_w × INRUSH_PM / 1000⌋` instead of `req_w`
Derived: every consumer in tier `k` receives `⌊req × sat_k / 1000⌋` and publishes it as `served_pm = sat_k`; edge load for D.9 is the **net flow** through the edge: `load_e = abs(Σ over nodes v below the edge of (served_v + charge_v − gen_v))` on the island's tree (rooted at the lowest node id, islands are trees by R12), where `gen_v` is the power a source node delivers, dispatched in **merit order** — reactors first, batteries only for what the reactors cannot cover: with `used = Σ served + charge`, `batt_used = max(0, Σ served − S)` and `R_out = used − batt_used = min(S, Σ served) + charge` (the reactors' total delivery), each reactor `i` delivers `⌊S_i × R_out / S⌋` and the integer remainder goes to the lowest reactor id, and each battery delivers its share of `batt_used` in ascending node id (at most `min(BATT_DIS_CAP, stock_b)`); a charging battery is a consumer carrying `charge_b` and is never a source in the same step — a deterministic dispatch, no iteration. The first draft's proportional share `⌊(S_i + D_i) × used / avail⌋` treated a charging battery as a source, so reactor-side cable loads read about 45 % under their true value (hiding real overloads) and the battery's own cable showed a false outflow *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **Guard** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G5)*: `S` = 0 gives every reactor `gen` 0, `used` = 0 gives every `gen` 0, and when `avail` = `S + D` = 0 (the reactor SCRAMmed or failed and every cell flat) `gen_i` = 0 for all sources with no division attempted — nothing is served, `sat_k` = 0 for every tier with `R_k` > 0 and every consumer reads `served_pm` 0 (the dark ship of C2 is a defined state, not a 0/0 fault). One source at the root reduces to "the served power below the edge"; two reactors on a welded island share the load in proportion to what they can give, and the seam cable carries only the difference between its side's demand and its side's delivery *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| S | int | 0–10000 W | calculated | Sum of source outputs × eff in the island (reactor ramp-limited) |
| D | int | 0–Σ over batteries of min(`BATT_DIS_CAP`, stock_b) | calculated | Battery discharge available this step *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| BATT_DIS_CAP | int | 1000–5000 W (default **1000**) | data file (a module-def field once the Item DB carries one) | Discharge limit of one BATTERY node — 1000 W is exactly the tier-0+1 emergency load (scrubber 600 + pump 400), so one cell serves life support and nothing else and a SCRAM sheds lamps, doors and heater at once: the dark, then fog, then frost sequence of LS-7 and C2. At 2000 W one cell carried the whole home-ship demand (about 1,860 W) for about 3 min with no shedding, which deleted that sequence; the first draft named `batt_dis_cap` without defining it *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; default changed 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2: the creative-director's lean is tiered shedding)* |
| R_k | int | 0–5000 W | calculated | Total requested in tier `k` (0 life-critical: scrubber; 1 pumps; 2 doors; 3 lights and the HEATER — light and heat go first, air and coolant last, so a SCRAM is felt as dark, then fog, then frost; 4 other) |
| served_k | int | 0–R_k | calculated | Allocated to tier `k` |
| sat_k | int | 0–1000 ‰ | calculated | Satisfaction of tier `k` |
| CH_CAP | int | 0–500 W (default 300) | data file | Battery charge rate cap |
| RAMP | int | 10–1000 W/step (default 100) | data file | Reactor output change per step |
| BATT_CELL | int | 10^6–10^7 dJ (default 3,600,000) | data file (module def) | One battery's full stock = 360 kJ |
| INRUSH_PM, INRUSH_STEPS | int | 1000–3000 (default 1500) / 0–20 (default 5) | data file | Re-energise surge magnitude and duration (V3's Attention spike and overload risk) |

**Output Range:** tiers are served fully in order; `Σ served ≤ S + D`; no value is negative. The lowest tiers go dark first, and dim in proportion (`sat_3` drives lamp brightness through `served_pm`, P1). A full cell at the tier-0+1 load of the MVP home-ship (1000 W) lasts `3,600,000 / 1000` = 3600 steps = 6 min — the emergency-power clock after a SCRAM; because `BATT_DIS_CAP` equals that load the cell never serves tiers 2–4, so every SCRAM sheds light, doors and heat on the first run *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G2)*.
**Example A:** `S` = 2500, `D` = 1000, `R` = [600, 400, 200, 1500, 300] → all 3000 served, `batt_used` = 500. **Example B (SCRAM):** `S` = 0, `D` = 1000 → `served` = [600, 400, 0, 0, 0], `sat` = [1000, 1000, 0, 0, 0]: lamps out, scrubber and pump running on the battery for 6 min. **Example C (inrush):** breaker closed on a branch carrying a 600 W scrubber and 200 W of lamps at `INRUSH_PM` 1500: for 5 steps the branch requests 900 + 300 = 1200 W; on a 1000 W cable that is `ρ` = 1200 → D.9 takes 16 bp/step for half a second — a scorch tick at the box, and a 1200 W spike in `power_use.delta_w_last5` for Attention. **Example D (two sources, the welded island, fixture F-TWO-SRC)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: home reactor `S_a` 2500 W, derelict reactor `S_b` 1500 W, 1800 W of loads (tiers 0–2) on the home side and 1200 W (tier 3) on the derelict side, the seam breaker closed on a seam cable of `cap` 2775: `avail` 4000, `used` 3000; `gen_a` = ⌊2500 × 3000 / 4000⌋ = 1875, `gen_b` = ⌊1500 × 3000 / 4000⌋ = 1125; the derelict-side subtree has served 1200 and gen 1125, so the seam cable carries 75 W (`ρ` 27). With the derelict reactor SCRAMmed (`gen_b` 0, `avail` 2500) tiers 0–2 take their 1800 W and tier 3 gets 700 W: the seam cable carries 700 W (`ρ` 252) — no overload. Derelict Authoring recomputes its own seam-breaker figure (Open Question 24) under this rule. **Example E (one reactor plus a battery, merit order; `S` 2500, one cell)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `Σ served` 2200 with the cell at 1,000,000 dJ: surplus 300, `charge` = `min(300, CH_CAP 300, 3,600,000 − 1,000,000)` = 300, `R_out` = 2500, the reactor delivers 2500 and the cell's cable carries 300 inward; the same island with a full cell: `charge` = `min(300, 300, 0)` = 0, the cell's cable carries 0 and nothing overfills; the reactor derated to `S` 1500 with `Σ served` 1800: `batt_used` 300, `R_out` 1500, the cell delivers 300 (its cable carries 300 outward) and the reactor-side cables carry 1500, their true load. **Example F (`avail` = 0)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G5)*: reactor SCRAMmed (`S` 0), every cell flat (`D` 0), `R` = [600, 400, 200, 1500, 300]: `avail` 0, `served_k` = 0 for all five tiers, `sat_k` = 0, `gen_i` = 0 for every source, `used` 0 — no division, every load reads `served_pm` 0.

**D.7 Compartment thermal step — conduction through hull and openings, remainder-carried**

The `thermal_step` formula is defined as:

`Q_net = Q_src_room + Σ Q_in − Σ Q_out − Q_hull` ; `E = t_rem + 1000 × Q_net` ; `ΔT = clamp( trunc(E / C) , −MAX_DT , +MAX_DT )` ; `t_rem' = E − ΔT × C` ; `T' = T + ΔT` with `C = V × C_TILE`

Derived: hull loss `Q_hull = ⌊K_HULL × S_ext × |T − T_amb| / 100000⌋ + ⌊K_HOLE × Σ A_hole × |T − T_amb| / 1000⌋` — an insulated-plate term and a breach-hole term — from the warmer side to the colder; outward loss is added to the section's `radiated_dj`, inward gain subtracted (a signed net counter)
Derived: opening conduction `Q_open = ⌊K_OPEN × A_o × |T_i − T_j| / 4000⌋` per OPENING edge (stored or virtual), moved from the warmer compartment to the colder, computed from the start-of-phase snapshot for every edge before any is applied (Jacobi)
Derived: the pair `(T, t_rem)` is the conserved quantity `U = T × C + t_rem` in milli-dJ; `T` is its intensive projection — this is how "store extensive, derive intensive" (R1) holds for heat while the readable field stays in mC
Derived *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: HEATER thermostat — a HEATER's effective request is `req_eff = ⌊req_w × duty_t / 1000⌋` with `duty_t = clamp( ⌊1000 × (T_SET_MC − T) / T_PROP_MC⌋ , 0 , 1000 )` from its compartment's start-of-phase `T`: the full request at or below `T_SET_MC − T_PROP_MC`, none at or above `T_SET_MC`, linear between; stateless (nothing to persist), applied in phase 2 before the tiers are solved. A closed door has `A_o` = 0 and conducts nothing

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| Q_src_room | int | 0–4000 dJ/step | calculated (D.8: machine waste heat after coolant capture, plus HEATER output, plus footprint CHILL/BURN impulses) | Heat entering the room this step |
| K_HULL | int | 10–100 (default **25**) centi-dJ/step/°C per tile (0.25 dJ at default) | data file | Insulated hull conductance — a ship that stays warm on ~1 kW of waste heat needs this below 1 dJ |
| K_HOLE | int | 1–10 (default **2**) dJ/step/°C per area unit | data file | Conductance of a breach hole — uninsulated, and the proxy for heat leaving with the gas (R14 forbids advection) |
| S_ext | int | 0–80 tiles | state (`exposed_tiles`, cached from Layout) | Exterior-facing hull tiles |
| A_hole | int | 0–64 each | calculated (D.2) | Breach hole area |
| T, T_o | int | −200000..+150000 mC | state | Room and neighbour temperatures |
| T_amb | int | −200000..+150000 (default **−30000**) | data file (`T_AMB_MC`, per world region; one value in MVP) | The exterior's effective radiative sink |
| K_OPEN | int | 1–8 (default **4**) | data file | Opening conduction per area unit — 640 dJ/step per 10 °C across an open door, which is what lets one heater warm five rooms |
| A_o | int | 0–512 | state / calculated | Opening area |
| C_TILE | int | 1000–6000 (default 3000) dJ/°C per tile | data file | Thermal mass per tile |
| C | int | 12,000–1,200,000 dJ/°C | calculated | Room thermal mass |
| t_rem | int | −C..+C (exclusive) | state | Carried milli-dJ remainder |
| MAX_DT | int | 500–5000 (default 2000) mC/step | data file | Per-step clamp; a hit increments `graph_saturations[DT_CLAMP]` — a content bug, not a mechanic |
| T_SET_MC | int | 18000–30000 mC (default **22000**) | data file; ★ registry candidate | HEATER thermostat setpoint: above it the heater requests nothing *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| T_PROP_MC | int | 500–5000 mC (default **2000**) | data file | Width of the thermostat's proportional band below `T_SET_MC` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| ΔT | int | ±MAX_DT | calculated | Temperature change this step |

**Output Range:** `T` is bounded below by `T_amb` (the plate term stops within `⌈100000 / (K_HULL × S_ext)⌉` mC of it — 182 mC for the example room: the thermal truncation stall, expected) and above by the balance between `Q_src_room` and the loss terms, `ΔT_eq = Q_src_room / (K_HULL × S_ext / 100 + K_HOLE × Σ A_hole)` °C above ambient. The plate time constant `τ = 100 × C / (K_HULL × S_ext)` is 16,364 steps = 27 min for the example room on its own — a sealed, unheated room is a slow cold — but a hole of area `A` adds `K_HOLE × A` dJ/°C to a plate conductance of only 5.5, so a RUPTURE hole (`A` 7) cuts `τ` to 4,615 steps and a breached room reaches FROST in 3.9 min and freezes its pipes in 12 min (D.15), which is the cold the player meets. **The healthy baseline is the constraint these defaults were set to:** the MVP home-ship (Σ `S_ext` 80 → 20 dJ/°C of plate loss) keeps every compartment in COMFORT on one 500 W heater plus lamp, scrubber and pump waste (≈ 1,020 dJ/step → about 21 °C uniform against a −30 °C sink, with `K_OPEN` 4 moving 640 dJ/step per 10 °C across each open door); an earlier draft at `K_HULL` ≡ 1 dJ and `T_amb` −40000 lost 1,320 dJ/step per outer room and would have held a *healthy* engine room in FREEZING with its pipes' pre-stage permanently lit — a constantly warning healthy ship, which P1 and P2 forbid. With the heater off (power loss) the whole ship cools with `τ_ship` ≈ 15,000 steps: breath fog at 6 min, frost at 13 min, and — with no hole at all — its liquid pipes show the FREEZE pre-stage at 23 min and freeze solid at 41 min (D.15 SHIP-COLD, the intentional dark-ship clock): the dark is cold and, given time, freezing, and a breach or a derelict brings it forward to 12 min, which is why `T_amb` is −30000 (a sink only 10 °C below `FREEZE_ON_MC`, the Section G build assertion) and not deeper *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Energy is conserved exactly: `Σ U + radiated_dj × 1000` over a section is constant when no machine runs, because every `Q_open` is subtracted once and added once and every `Q_hull` is counted into `radiated_dj`. The remainder is carried, never dropped, so a room 0.5 °C from equilibrium still moves (the stall above is from the hull term's own floor, not from the step). **Thermostat** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the first draft's heater was a fixed 500 W with no thermostat, so closing doors — the main decompression response — left the heater room with nowhere to put its heat and it reached HOT in about 8 min, a healthy ship starting to warn because the player did the right thing (P1, P2). With the thermostat the heater room settles where `500 × duty_t` equals its loss, just under `T_SET_MC` (a sealed 42-tile cryo bay about 21.3 °C), and the HEALTHY uniform equilibrium of the open ship moves from 21 °C to about 20 °C (heater duty ≈ 0.96). The `MAX_DT` saturation of a heater in a 4-tile closet (41 mC per step at full request) is still a content bug, but the room can no longer be driven past `T_SET_MC` by the heater.
**Example:** `V` = 30 → `C` = 90,000; `S_ext` = 22; `T` = 40,000; `T_amb` = −30,000; `Q_src_room` = 516 (D.8 example); `t_rem` = 12,500; no openings or holes. `Q_hull` = `⌊25 × 22 × 70,000 / 100,000⌋` = 385 outward; `Q_net` = 516 − 385 = 131; `E` = 12,500 + 131,000 = 143,500; `ΔT` = `trunc(143,500 / 90,000)` = 1; `t_rem'` = 143,500 − 90,000 = 53,500; `T'` = 40,001; `radiated_dj` += 385. Check: `U` before = 40,000 × 90,000 + 12,500 = 3,600,012,500; after = 40,001 × 90,000 + 53,500 = 3,600,143,500; difference 131,000 = 1000 × (516 − 385) exactly. With a RUPTURE hole (`A` 7) in the same room the hole term adds `⌊2 × 7 × 70,000 / 1000⌋` = 980, `Q_net` = −849, and the room cools 9 mC per step.

**D.8 Coolant circulation, heat capture, radiator and the machine temperature proxy**

The `coolant_loop` formula is defined as:

`lvl_f = clamp( (level_pm − L_CAV_LO) × 1000 / (L_CAV_HI − L_CAV_LO) , 0 , 1000 )` ; `circ = ⌊pump_eff × sat × lvl_f / 10^6⌋` ; `Q_cap = min( Q_src , ⌊K_CAP × circ × max(0, T_room − t_cool) / 10^6⌋ )`

Derived: radiator `Q_rad = ⌊K_RAD × eff_r × circ × max(0, t_cool − T_amb_r) / 10^9⌋`, added to the section's `radiated_dj`
Derived: coolant temperature `E_c = tc_rem + 1000 × (Σ Q_cap − Σ Q_rad)`; `Δt_cool = trunc(E_c / (C_COOL × level_ml))`; `tc_rem' = E_c − Δt_cool × C_COOL × level_ml` (with `level_ml` ≥ 1; at 0 the reservoir carries no heat and `Q_cap` is already 0)
Derived: uncaptured heat `Q_src_room = Q_src − Q_cap` enters the machine's compartment (D.7)
Derived: machine temperature proxy `T_mach = T_room + ⌊Q_src_room × K_MACH⌋` — the OVERHEAT trigger (LS-11) and the HOT/shimmer pre-stage that opens the fairness window before a SCRAM
Derived: `circ` through a path is 0 when any PIPE on it is FAILED (cap 0), any VALVE closed, or the PUMP's node is not OK/DEGRADED; `level_pm = ⌊1000 × level_ml / cap_ml⌋`
Derived (guards) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: a RESERVOIR with `cap_ml` 0 reads `level_pm` 0 (`circ` 0, no division); `L_CAV_LO ≥ L_CAV_HI` refuses to boot; a PIPE leak debits the reservoir in the same phase 5 before `level_pm` is read, in ascending reservoir id

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| level_ml, cap_ml | int | 0–cap_ml / 5,000–100,000 (default 20,000) | state / data file (module def) | Reservoir fill and rated capacity |
| L_CAV_LO, L_CAV_HI | int | 0–399 / 401–1000, LO < HI asserted at boot (defaults 200 / 600) | data file | Below LO the pump cavitates (circ 0); above HI full circulation; the `lvl_f` denominator is `max(1, L_CAV_HI − L_CAV_LO)` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| pump_eff, sat | int | 0–1000 | calculated (D.4, D.6 tier 1) | Pump integrity and power |
| circ | int | 0–1000 ‰ | calculated | Circulation fraction |
| Q_src | int | 0–4000 dJ/step | calculated | Waste heat the jacketed machine produces: `⌊out_w × waste_permille / 1000⌋` |
| K_CAP | int | 20–150 (default 75) dJ/step/°C | data file | Jacket coupling |
| K_RAD | int | 20–150 (default 60) | data file | Radiator coupling |
| eff_r | int | 0–1000 | calculated (D.4) | Radiator integrity |
| T_room, t_cool, T_amb_r | int | mC | state / state / data file (`T_AMB_MC`) | Machine room, coolant and radiator-face temperatures |
| C_COOL | int | 2–8 (default 4) dJ/°C per mL | data file | Coolant thermal mass per mL |
| tc_rem | int | ±(C_COOL × level_ml) | state | Carried remainder |
| K_MACH | int | 10–100 (default 40) mC per dJ/step | data file | How far a machine's casing runs above its room per unit of uncaptured heat |
| T_mach | int | mC | calculated | Machine proxy temperature |

**Output Range:** `circ` 0..1000; `Q_cap ≤ Q_src`; heat not captured stays in the room and in the machine's casing, so losing coolant makes the engine room climb through HOT (1,500 dJ/step against 5.5 dJ/°C of plate loss would head for SCORCH, but door conduction at `K_OPEN` 4 spreads it and the SCRAM below cuts it within 35 s) and the reactor casing hot: at `circ` = 0 with `Q_src` = 1500 and `K_MACH` = 40, `T_mach` = `T_room + 60,000` = 80 °C from a 20 °C room — above `OVERHEAT_ON_MC` 70000, so a stalled pump or a cut coolant line leads to OVERHEAT (sev 3, scorch and shimmer at the casing) and after `OVERHEAT_SCRAM_STEPS` to SCRAM. The pre-stage is continuous: between `L_CAV_HI` and `L_CAV_LO` the capture falls linearly and `T_mach` climbs through HOT (40,000..69,999, sev 2 shimmer) before it reaches OVERHEAT — the player sees the casing sweat for minutes before it scorches. Radiator output always goes to `radiated_dj`, so section energy is exactly accounted.
**Example:** `level_ml` = 11,000 of 20,000 (`level_pm` = 550), `pump_eff` = 1000, `sat` = 1000 → `lvl_f` = `350 × 1000 / 400` = 875 → `circ` = 875. `Q_src` = 1500, `T_room` = 40,000, `t_cool` = 25,000, `K_CAP` = 75 → `Q_cap` = `min(1500, ⌊75 × 875 × 15,000 / 10^6⌋)` = `min(1500, 984)` = 984; `Q_src_room` = 516 (used in D.7); `T_mach` = 40,000 + 516 × 40 = 60,640 (HOT, below OVERHEAT). Radiator `K_RAD` = 60, `eff_r` = 1000, `T_amb_r` = −30,000 → `Q_rad` = `⌊60 × 1000 × 875 × 55,000 / 10^9⌋` = 2887. `E_c` = `0 + 1000 × (984 − 2887)` = −1,903,000; `C_COOL × level_ml` = 44,000 → `Δt_cool` = −43; `tc_rem'` = −1,903,000 + 1,892,000 = −11,000; the coolant cools toward the point where rejection equals the capped capture (about −1.4 °C for this reactor at `circ` 875), which is the designed steady state — and at full circulation on a healthy loop the capture is the full `Q_src`, so a healthy engine room receives only its pump's and lamps' waste.

**D.9 Overload, wear and the damage multipliers — every endogenous hp loss**

The `endogenous_damage` formula is defined as:

`ρ = ⌊1000 × load / cap⌋` ; `Δhp_ovl = min( DHP_MAX , ⌊OVL_K × max(0, ρ − 1000)² / 10^4⌋ )` per step ; `Δhp_wear = W_base` on steps where `step_no mod WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS = 0`, else 0 ; `Δhp = ⌊(Δhp_ovl + Δhp_wear + Δhp_short) × rate_pm × stress_pm / 10^6⌋` with `Δhp_short = SHORT_BURN_BP_PER_STEP` on every step (not only period steps) while the guard below holds *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

Derived: `W_base` is the sum of the wear terms that apply to the subject: `LEAK_WEAR_BP_PER_100` while in mode LEAK because `hp < LEAK_HP` **and** its descriptor's `intensity_pm > 0` (the seam weep is a geometry-driven mode-1 descriptor at `hp` ≥ `LEAK_HP` and carries **no** LEAK wear, so a default seam never ends below `HP_OK` with a permanent pre-stage *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*) (an isolated leak behind a closed valve draws from nothing and holds its `hp`; re-opened, it resumes from the stored `hp`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from derelict-authoring-handmade-kits)*; `HEAT_WEAR_BP_PER_100` while in mode OVERHEAT; `HULL_FATIGUE_BP_PER_100` while a HULL_SEGMENT's `fatigued` flag is set; `PATCH_DECAY_BP_PER_100` while `hp_cap < HP_FULL` and `step_no − patched_at_step ≥ PATCH_DECAY_STEPS`; **duty wear** `⌈W_DUTY_BP_PER_100 × duty_pm / 1000⌉` on a SCRUBBER, PUMP, HEATER or REACTOR in state OK or DEGRADED while `duty_pm > 0` (a RADIATOR only if its module def sets `duty_wear` true — off in MVP), where `duty_pm` = `run_pm` (LS-19) — `served_pm` for a SCRUBBER or PUMP whose request is above 0, `⌊duty_t × served_pm / 1000⌋` for a HEATER (its `req_w` stays 500 while the thermostat is satisfied, so `served_pm` alone would wear an idle heater at full rate), `⌊1000 × out_w / out_w_rated⌋` for the REACTOR, and 0 for any machine whose request is 0 (a pump at `throttle_pm` 0, a thermostat-satisfied heater, a reactor at `out_w` 0) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* — the ceiling means a machine that runs at all wears at least 1 bp per period *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN: no safe harbour, P5)*
Derived: `rate_pm = 1000` for a HOME section, `10^6 / DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION` = 2000 for a DERELICT section; `stress_pm = BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT` while `step_no < stress_until_step`, else 1000
Derived (cause tag) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: overload damage taken during the `INRUSH_STEPS` after a breaker close (M9, the branch's `energised_at_step`) is tagged EXOGENOUS — a breaker closed onto an overload is a player verb (R7) — and bypasses the D.11 gate; after that window the same overload is ENDOGENOUS and gated
Derived *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the result is applied as `Δhp_applied = min( Δhp , max(0, hp − floor_hp) )` and `hp' = hp − Δhp_applied` — the floor **limits the reduction and can never raise `hp`** (the first draft's `hp' = max(hp − Δhp, floor_hp)` healed a plate ruptured to 3600 back up to 4000 on the same run, and lifted any gouge below `HP_FAIL` to 3001). While the D.11 gate is closed, `floor_hp` is `BREACH_HP` for a HULL_SEGMENT or SEAM whose `hp ≥ BREACH_HP` (the LEAK → BREACH escalation is gated), otherwise `HP_FAIL + 1` for a subject above `HP_FAIL`, and `1` for a subject already FAILED (the gated FAILED → DESTROYED case); once the gate is open it is 0. A subject already below its floor takes no further endogenous damage until the gate opens and is never moved; `Δhp` never carries over — damage discarded by the gate is gone
Derived (guard) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; short burn added 2026-10-27 — re-review, carried-over blocker: the guard alone stopped all damage at FAILED)*: `ρ` is evaluated only for a CABLE (or seam CABLE) with `cap` > 0, so `ρ` never divides by 0; a FAILED or DESTROYED cable has `cap` 0, carries no load (the island re-splits, C1) and takes no *overload* damage. **Short burn** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — confirming review, blocker 1)*: a FAILED CABLE carrying mode SHORT (LS-11 defines SHORT for a CABLE as `hp ≤ HP_FAIL` with a live source at an end, so the mode and this guard are ONE predicate; phase 8 reads the mode flag persisted by the previous run's phase 9 and re-checks `S + D` > 0 on this run's phase-2 result) keeps burning — `Δhp_short` = `SHORT_BURN_BP_PER_STEP` on every step. Worked timeline (PWR-OVL, HOME, 4 bp/step): step 1750: `hp` = 10000 − 4×1750 = 3000 = `HP_FAIL` → FAILED (`cap` 0) and SHORT set in phase 9 of that run (`sev` = min(4, 1 + ⌊4000/1500⌋) = 3); step 1751: phase 1 splits the island, phase 2 gives the reactor-side island `S` > 0, phase 8 reads SHORT and burns 4 → `hp` 2996; burns on steps 1751..2500 = 750 × 4 = 3000 → `hp` 0 at step 2500, DESTROYED in that run's phase 9. DERELICT (×2, 8 bp/step): FAILED at step 875 (10000 − 8×875 = 3000), 375 burns × 8 = 3000 → DESTROYED at step 1250. Breaker opened in P2 after run 1760 takes effect from run 1761: 10 burns (1751..1760) × 4 = 40 → `hp` 2960, never DESTROYED. Without the burn the clock stops at FAILED (2.9 min), a cable can never reach DESTROYED, and the PWR-OVL fixture, its criteria and the R21 power band (3.8–5.2 min) are unreachable. The burn is inside the `rate_pm` and `stress_pm` multiplier and under the D.11 gate (floor 1 for a FAILED subject while the gate is closed; by then the gate has been open for ≥ 100 steps); the player stops it by opening the upstream breaker (M9: the island at that end then has no live source) or by repair; it never applies to a CABLE_JUNCTION or LOAD (a static BROKEN-BREAKER fault does not burn itself out) or to an authored dead cable with no source behind it. 4 bp per step is 400 bp per 100 steps: the FAILED → DESTROYED leg of PWR-OVL is `3000 / 4` = 750 steps. (The first re-review's suggested `SHORT_WEAR` of 70 bp per period was wrong: 3000 → 0 in 750 steps needs about 400 per 100 steps.)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| load, cap | int | 0–20000 / 500–6000 W | calculated (tree flow, D.6) / state (post D.14 for seams) | Power carried / rated |
| ρ | int | 0–40000 ‰ | calculated | Utilisation |
| OVL_K | int | 1–16 (default 4) | data file | Overload damage scale |
| DHP_MAX | int | 500–5000 (default 2000) bp | data file | Arc-flash clamp per step |
| SHORT_BURN_BP_PER_STEP | int | 1–40 (default **4** = 400 bp per 100 steps) | data file; ★ registry candidate | Burn of a FAILED cable still in mode SHORT with a live source behind it (guard above); sets the FAILED → DESTROYED leg of D.15 PWR-OVL *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS | int | 50–200 (default 100) steps | data file | Wear is applied once per period (10 s) — the groan-and-tick cadence the player hears |
| LEAK_WEAR_BP_PER_100 | int | 0–100 (default **16**) | data file | A leaking fitting erodes |
| HEAT_WEAR_BP_PER_100 | int | 0–200 (default 40) | data file | An overheating machine cooks itself |
| HULL_FATIGUE_BP_PER_100 | int | 0–200 (default **50**) | data file | A fatigued plate creeps toward breach |
| PATCH_DECAY_BP_PER_100 | int | 0–500 (default 100) | data file | A decayed patch lets go |
| PATCH_DECAY_STEPS | int | 600–6000 (default **2400** = `PATCH_DECAY_S` 240 × 10) | data file (derived from registry candidate `PATCH_DECAY_S`) | How long a patch holds before it starts to decay |
| W_DUTY_BP_PER_100 | int | 1–6 (default **2**) | data file (registry candidate `W_DUTY_BP_PER_100`) | Duty wear per period on a running machine at full duty *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: at 2 bp per 100 steps a healthy machine loses 720 bp per hour (7.2 %/h) and crosses `HP_OK` after `⌈3000 / 2⌉` = 1500 periods = 150,000 steps = 4.17 h of continuous duty, `HP_FAIL` after 3500 periods = 9.7 h — quiet for a session, never permanently safe |
| duty_pm | int | 0–1000 ‰ | calculated (`run_pm`, LS-19: D.6 `served_pm` of a consumer with a non-zero request; HEATER `⌊duty_t × served_pm / 1000⌋`; REACTOR `⌊1000 × out_w / out_w_rated⌋`; 0 when switched off) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | How hard the machine ran this period *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* |
| DERELICT_FAULT_COMPRESSION | int | 250–1000 (default **500** ‰) | data file (registry candidate) | Derelict time-band compression; 500 ‰ doubles the wear rate |
| BREACH_PROPAGATION_MULT | int | 1000–3000 (default **2000** ‰) | data file (registry candidate) | Phase-C multiplier applied while a section is stressed (M17 STRESS) |
| stress_until_step | int | ≥ 0 | state (per section) | Set by M17 STRESS to `step_no + STRESS_HOLD_STEPS` (default 600 = 60 s) |
| Δhp | int | 0–6000 | calculated | hp lost this step |

**Output Range:** `Δhp_ovl` is 0 for `ρ ≤ 1000`, 1 bp/step at `ρ` = 1050, 4 at 1100, 16 at 1200, 64 at 1400, clamped at 2000 (`ρ` ≥ 3236). Short burn is 4 bp per step on a HOME section (8 on a DERELICT section) once a cable is FAILED in SHORT with a live source behind it, until the breaker is opened *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Wear is coarse by design: hp falls in visible lumps every 10 s (50 bp for a fatigued plate), so the tell has a rhythm, not a creep. Duty wear is the floor under that rhythm *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: 2 bp per period on every running machine is 360 bp over a 30-minute session (a healthy machine ends it at `hp` 9640, still OK, no descriptor — the HEALTHY criterion in Section H) and 720 bp per hour, so `HP_OK` falls at 4.2 h and the machine carries a WORN sev-1 descriptor from then on; a fully repaired ship is therefore quiet for a session and never permanently safe (P5), and because the term is inside `W_base` it is doubled on a DERELICT section and under STRESS like every other wear. The multipliers scale only **endogenous damage** — never D.1 gas physics, D.7 heat or D.5 supply (P1: a stressed ship fails faster; its air does not leave faster than the hole allows). With `rate_pm` 2000 and `stress_pm` 2000 the maximum product is ×4; the overload term at `DHP_MAX` under ×4 is 8000 bp, which is the "6000" ceiling of `Δhp` only after the gate: in practice no subject loses more than its remaining hp. The gate floor at `HP_FAIL + 1` is what makes every endogenous failure arrive at least 10 s after its first trace, whatever the rate *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*.
**Example:** cable `cap` = 3000 carrying 3300 W → `ρ` = 1100 → `Δhp_ovl` = `⌊4 × 100² / 10^4⌋` = 4 bp/step on a HOME section: DEGRADED (hp < 7000) at step 750 (75 s: box glows, hum climbs), FAILED (cap 0, branch dark) at 1750 (2.9 min), DESTROYED at 2500 (4.2 min) — the R21 power band; from step 1750 the cable is FAILED in SHORT with the reactor's island behind it, so it burns on at 4 bp per step (short burn) for the last 3000 bp, 750 steps *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The same overload on a DERELICT section (`rate_pm` 2000) is 8 bp/step → branch dark in 1.5 min (step 875) and DESTROYED at step 1250. With the upstream breaker opened at step 1760 the cable holds `hp` 2960 and is never DESTROYED. A fatigued hull plate at `hp` 6999: 50 bp per 100 steps → `hp` 3999 after 60 periods = 6000 steps = 10 min (6999 − 3000; it never reads exactly 4000), at which instant D.2 opens a 1-area pinhole *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. A coolant pipe at `hp` 3800 in LEAK: 16 bp per 100 steps → FAILED after `⌈800 / 16⌉` = 50 periods = 8.3 min. Duty *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: a SCRUBBER at `hp` 10000 with `served_pm` 1000 loses `⌈2 × 1000 / 1000⌉` = 2 bp on every period step and reads `hp` 6998 — DEGRADED, WORN sev 1, `eff` 999 — on period 1501, step 150,100 = 4.17 h; the same scrubber in a brownout at `served_pm` 400 loses `⌈2 × 400 / 1000⌉` = 1 bp per period and takes 8.3 h.

**D.10 Dirty-key budget per step — confirms `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS`**

The `dirty_key_budget` formula is defined as:

`K_step = C_q + B + N_t + F_c + L_q + S + T_f` with the invariant `K_step ≤ MAX_DIRTY_KEYS`, else the kernel collapses the notification to `ALL`

Derived: a continuous value contributes to `C_q`, `F_c` or `L_q` only when `⌊value / quantum⌋` differs from the last-emitted bucket; quanta: `T` 1000 mC, `P` 10 dkPa, `p_o2` 5 dkPa, store/charge 20 ‰, descriptor intensity 100 ‰, `hp` 250 bp
Derived: one key per compartment per step (`C/<cid>`), one per band change (`B/<cid>`), one per node or edge state, forced-mode or severity transition (`N/<id>`), one per descriptor intensity bucket change (`F/<id>`), one per loop component summary quantum (`L/<loop>/<anchor>`), one per seam (`S/<seam_id>`), plus `T` and `SEC/<section_id>` for structural change; keys are encoded as `(KIND << 32) | id` ints (LS-20)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| C_q | int | 0–C | calculated | Compartments that crossed a quantum |
| B | int | 0–3C | calculated | Band changes |
| N_t | int | 0–N+E | calculated | Node/edge transitions |
| F_c | int | 0–F | calculated | Descriptor intensity bucket changes |
| L_q | int | 0–components | calculated | Loop summaries that crossed 20 ‰ |
| S, T_f | int | 0–seams / 0–1 | calculated | Seam keys; topology flag |
| MAX_DIRTY_KEYS | int | 256 | registry (`MAX_DIRTY_KEYS`, "keys per block per tick"; "Above this a notification collapses to ALL") | Kernel constant — this formula confirms it, it does not set it |

**Output Range:** steady state 0–3 keys per step (a `T` drifting 7 mC/step crosses a 1000 mC quantum once per ~143 steps; a pinhole's `P` crosses a 10 dkPa quantum once per ~100 steps). Worst designed cascade: MVP (`C` = 12) `12 + 3 + 6 + 8 + 7 + 1 + 1` = 38; VS-A (`C` = 48) `48 + 12 + 20 + 30 + 20 + 4 + 1` = 135 — both under 256; the MVP figure is far under the 128 floor below which this document asks the kernel never to go, while VS-A's 135 is above it, so VS-A relies on 256 and not on the floor *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Structural events (weld, cut, instantiate, discard) use `T` + `SEC/<id>` only; a derelict instantiation legitimately collapses to `ALL` and the Projector rebuilds. The S1 spike reports keys-per-run percentiles against a P95 ≤ 32 steady-state target.
**Example:** the step a reactor SCRAMs: `N_t` = 1 (reactor) + 6 (loads to STARVE), `L_q` = 2 (power and O2 component summaries), `F_c` = 3, `C_q` = 1 → 13 keys.

**D.11 Fairness gate — `warn_elapsed` and `alarm_eligible`**

The `fairness_gate` formula is defined as:

`warn_elapsed = step_no − warn_since_step` if the subject's `has_warn` bit (compartments: its bit of `warn_bits`, LS-13 (b)) is set *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* (it has carried `sev ≥ 1` on every run since `warn_since_step`), else 0 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* ; `gate_open = warn_elapsed ≥ W_MIN_STEPS` with `W_MIN_STEPS = W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS`

Derived: `warn_since_step` is set to `step_no` and `has_warn` set on the run a subject's `sev` goes 0 → ≥ 1, and `has_warn` cleared (`warn_since_step` reset to 0) on the run it returns to 0 (phase 9); both are persisted. The explicit bit replaces the first draft's `warn_since_step > 0` test, which read every back-dated authored warning (M1 allows `warn_since_step` ≤ 0) as "no warning" and held every back-dated derelict fault at `hp` 1 for ever *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. An OVERLOAD cable's `sev` is ≥ 1 from the first step with `ρ` > 1000 (LS-11), so its `warn_since_step` is that step and its window opens there, not at the `HP_OK` crossing *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
Derived: an **endogenous** transition to FAILED or DESTROYED, and a hull or seam LEAK → BREACH escalation, is applied only if `gate_open`; otherwise hp is floored at `HP_FAIL + 1` (D.9), at 1 for a FAILED subject heading to DESTROYED, or at `BREACH_HP` for the breach case, and the transition happens on the first run the gate opens. **Exogenous** causes — player verbs (cut, vent, salvage, overload by breaker), footprint-channel RUPTURE and GOUGE, `apply_damage` with `cause = EXOGENOUS` — bypass the gate (R7)
Derived: `alarm_eligible = alarm_capable(mode) ∧ (sev ≥ ALARM_SEV_MIN) ∧ gate_open(subject)`, subject = node / edge / seam for modes 1-10 and 27 (6, 10 and 27 are never capable, so their gate is moot for `alarm_eligible`; a live STARVE or WORN still carries `warn_since_step` like any `sev ≥ 1` descriptor, LS-11), and (compartment, tracker) for 20-23 with tracker(20) = tracker(21) = `t`, tracker(22) = `o2`, tracker(23) = `p` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* — alarm-class presentation is allowed only at severity ≥ 3 and never as the first channel (R7, R17)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| W_MIN_WARNING_S | int | 4–20 (default **10**; *Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN*) s | data file (registry candidate `W_MIN_WARNING_S`) | Minimum readable warning before an endogenous loss |
| TICK_RATE | int | 60 | registry (`TICK_RATE`, 60 Hz, set-once) | Ticks per simulated second |
| GRAPH_STEP_TICKS | int | 6 | constant (registry candidate; equals the kernel divisor of the three Graph hooks) | Ticks per step |
| W_MIN_STEPS | int | 40–200 (default **100**) | calculated at boot | The gate in steps |
| warn_since_step | int | any (negative for a back-dated authored warning) | state (per node, edge, seam) | Step the current sev ≥ 1 run began; meaningful only while `has_warn` is set *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| has_warn | flag bit | 0/1 | state (per node, edge, seam) | A warning is running; set by phase 9 or by M1 for any template that supplies a `warn_since_step` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* |
| warn_since_o2, warn_since_p, warn_since_t | int | any | state (per compartment) | Step the current sev ≥ 1 run of that band machine began; meaningful only while its bit of `warn_bits` is set *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| warn_bits | u8 | bits 0-2 | state (per compartment) | One has-warn bit per tracker (o2, p, t) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| warn_elapsed | int | ≥ 0 | calculated | Consecutive steps of visible warning |
| alarm_capable | bool | — | calculated | True for modes 1-5, 7-9 and 20-23; false by definition for 6 STARVE, 10 WORN and 24-27 (LS-13 (a)) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* |
| ALARM_SEV_MIN | int | 2–4 (default 3) | data file | First severity at which `alarm_eligible` may be true |
| gate_open, alarm_eligible | bool | — | calculated | Published on the descriptor (LS-14) |

**Output Range:** the gate can only delay, never hasten, and it delays by at most `W_MIN_STEPS − warn_elapsed`; for compartment descriptors `alarm_eligible` is withheld for at most `W_MIN_STEPS − warn_elapsed_x` steps after `sev` reaches `ALARM_SEV_MIN` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. For an overload the window opens on the first step over (LS-11), so the gate binds only when a cable would fall from `HP_FULL` to `HP_FAIL` (7000 bp) in under 100 steps, i.e. at 70 bp/step or more — `ρ` ≥ 1419 (`⌊4 × 419² / 10^4⌋` = 70; at 1418 it is 69 and the gate is already open when `hp` crosses) — or an arc-flash; for a non-overload cause (a gouge, repeated `apply_damage`) the window opens at the `HP_OK` crossing and the gate binds above 40 bp/step (4000 bp in under 100 steps); everything slower has warned for longer than 10 s by the time it fails *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN; regenerated 2026-10-27 — design review, the first draft computed it as if the window opened at `HP_OK` for overloads too)*. For forced modes it binds through their pre-stages: a pipe shows FREEZE sev 1 (frost on the lagging) from `FREEZE_WARN_MC` (−10000) and may freeze solid at `FREEZE_ON_MC` (−20000) only after 100 steps of frost; a reactor shows OVERHEAT sev 3 for `OVERHEAT_SCRAM_STEPS` (300) before SCRAM. Patch re-leak (R15) is covered without a special case: a patched subject sits at `hp_cap` ≤ 6000 < `HP_OK`, so it carries sev 1 continuously and its window is open long before decay begins.
**Example** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (regenerated from LS-11: OVERLOAD opens the window on the first step): cable `cap` 3000 carrying 4500 W (a fixture figure, like D.9's 3300 W: `ρ` 1500; `Δhp_ovl` = `⌊4 × 500² / 10^4⌋` = 100 bp/step on a HOME section). Step 1: `ρ` > 1000, `sev` 3 (hazard rule `ρ` ≥ 1500), `warn_since_step` = 1, `hp` 9900. The physics alone reaches `hp` 3000 (FAILED) on step 70 with `warn_elapsed` 69 < 100, so `hp` is floored at 3001 for steps 70..100 (31 steps held, `graph_gate_holds` records (cable, 31)); on step 101 `warn_elapsed` = 100, the gate opens, `hp` falls to 2901 and the cable goes FAILED. The player had 10.0 s of a humming, glowing, crackling box — 3.1 s more than the 7.0 s the physics alone would have given. `sev` is 3 from step 1 but `alarm_eligible` needs the gate as well, so it is false on steps 1..100 and true from step 101: the only thing that changes on that run is `gate_open` (the case in which `sev ≥ ALARM_SEV_MIN` holds while the gate is closed), and the hum and glow were first, the alarm sweep second, exactly as R17 requires. Boundary: the same cable at `ρ` 1400 (64 bp/step) reaches 2960 on step 110 with `warn_elapsed` 109 — the gate is already open, nothing is held and the cable fails on step 110; at `ρ` 1419 (70 bp/step) `hp` reaches 3000 on step 100 with `warn_elapsed` 99 and is held one step.
**Example B — compartments** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)* (Suit D.1 / D.3 DOOR-BREACH fixture, registry defaults, `W_MIN_STEPS` = 10 × 60 / 6 = 100): `band_o2` THIN from step 25 (`warn_since_o2` = 25), HYPOXIC from 64 (sev 3), ANOXIC from 116 (sev 4); `band_p` LOW from 56 (sev 2; `warn_since_p` = 56), DECOMP from 143 (sev 3), VACUUM from 360. ATMO_THIN is sev 3 at step 64 with `warn_elapsed` 64 − 25 = 39: `alarm_eligible` is false on steps 64..124 and true from step 125 (125 − 25 = 100; sev 4 on both sides of the flip, only `gate_open` changes). ATMO_DECOMP is sev 3 at 143 with `warn_elapsed` 143 − 56 = 87: false on 143..155, true from 156 (156 − 56 = 100). The first `alarm_eligible` descriptor is ATMO_THIN at step 125 (about 12.5 s in); `alarm_sections()` is empty before and holds the room's section from step 125. Hiss, draught and thin air came first (steps 25, 56, 64), the alarm second.

**D.12 Stability rule — Σ k ≤ 1 per node, per-edge cap, re-check at weld**

The `stability_rule` formula is defined as:

`Σ_j k_ij ≤ 10^6` for every compartment `i`, with `k_ij = ⌈G_OPEN × A_max_ij × 10^6 / (V_i × GAS_SCALE)⌉` ppm over its OPENING edges and potential virtual holes

Derived: the per-edge runtime cap in D.1 — `Δn ≤ ⌊equalising amount / deg_max⌋` — guarantees the convex combination whatever the current areas are; the boot and weld assertion above guarantees it for the *maximum* areas so that the cap is never the binding term in normal play
Derived (thermal analogue) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: `Σ_th_i = Σ_j ⌈K_OPEN × A_max_ij × 10^6 / (4 × C_i)⌉ + ⌈K_HULL × S_ext_i × 10^4 / C_i⌉ + ⌈K_HOLE × Σ_h A_HOLE_MAX × 10^6 / C_i⌉ ≤ 10^6` ppm, with `C_i = V_i × C_TILE` — the opening, insulated-plate and breach-hole terms of D.7 per unit of thermal mass — asserted at boot and by `can_join` the same way. The first draft called it "three orders of magnitude smaller" and left out the plate and hole terms; it is about four times smaller than the gas sum at the defaults (the weld example below: 3,333 ppm for openings + 5,333 for holes + 42 for the plate, about 8,700 against 36,000, taking `S_ext` 12), and at the knob extremes (`K_HOLE` 10, eight doors at `K_OPEN` 8 in a 4-tile closet) it exceeds 10^6, where the Jacobi step overshoots and shows as frost flicker
Derived: `A_max_ij` is the door's full area for a stored OPENING, `A_HOLE_MAX` for every HULL_SEGMENT bound to the compartment, and `L_seam × A_SEAM_MAX` for a seam; `can_join` recomputes the sum for both seam compartments with the plan's openings added and rejects with `STABILITY` if either the gas sum or the thermal sum exceeds 10^6 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| k_ij | int | 0–10^6 ppm | calculated | Fraction of compartment `i`'s imbalance that edge `j` can move in one step |
| A_max_ij | int | 1–512 | data file / calculated | Largest area edge `j` can ever have |
| V_i | int | 4–200 | state | Volume |
| deg_max | int | 1–16 | calculated | As in D.1 |
| Σ_j k_ij | int | 0–10^6 | calculated at boot / weld | The convex-combination bound |

**Output Range:** satisfied with large margin by any MVP content: a 20-tile room with two doors (64 each), two breachable segments (64 each) and a 3-tile seam (48) sums to `2 × 304 × 10^6 / 20,000` = 30,400 ppm — 3 % of the bound. It binds only for absurd content (a 4-tile closet with eight doors: 8 × 32,000 = 256,000 ppm, still fine; a 4-tile closet with sixteen door-sized holes at `G_OPEN` 10 fails). Without this rule a room of degree ≥ 3 under Jacobi exchange can overshoot and oscillate with its neighbours — pulsing gauges and frost that flickers, which looks like a hallucination when it is a bug (TD R1b); with it, S1's monotone-gradient test holds: the maximum `|ΔP|` over all open edges is non-increasing run over run and no edge's flux direction alternates.
**Example:** weld plan adds an opening (`A_max` 64) and a 3-tile seam (48) to a cargo bay `V` = 24 already carrying two doors and three segments (5 × 64 = 320): new sum `⌈2 × (320 + 112) × 10^6 / 24,000⌉` = 36,000 ppm ≤ 10^6 → accepted. Runtime: that bay at `P` 1000 with all five openings to vacuum at once (`deg_max` 5): each edge moves at most `⌊24,000,000 / 5⌋` = 4,800,000, far above the `2 × 64 × 1000` = 128,000 the first term allows — the cap is dormant, as designed.

**D.13 Boot product-max assertion — overflow is designed out, not detected**

The `product_max_assertion` formula is defined as:

`∀ (a, b) ∈ PRODUCTS : max_a × max_b < 2^62` asserted once at boot over the table below; a violation refuses to start the build (`GRAPH_BOOT_PRODUCT_OVERFLOW(a, b)`)

Derived: three-factor products are asserted pairwise on their running maximum (`max_ab = max_a × max_b`, then `max_ab × max_c`); every field maximum is a `GraphTuning` constant and a numeric soft invariant (LS-24): in release a field exceeding its max is clamped and `graph_saturations[field]` increments; in debug it raises `GraphFault` (LS-24; never GDScript `assert`)

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| PRODUCTS | list | fixed | constant (the table below, maintained with the code) | Every multiplication the propagation path forms |
| max_x | int | documented per field | data file (`GraphTuning.max_*`) | Field maxima |
| 2^62 | int | 4.61 × 10^18 | constant | Headroom below the int64 limit (GDScript `int` wraps silently [UNV, S0-b]) |

| Product (formula) | Operands and maxima | Worst value | Headroom |
|---|---|---|---|
| `G_OPEN × A_m × abs(g)` (D.1) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* | 10 × 512,000 × 6 × 10^10 | 3.1 × 10^17 | 15 |
| `n_a × V_b` (D.1 gradient `g`) | 3 × 10^8 × 200 | 6.0 × 10^10 | 7.7 × 10^7 |
| `G_OPEN × A_m × n_tot_src` (D.1 exterior) | 10 × 512,000 × 3 × 10^8 | 1.5 × 10^15 | 3.1 × 10^3 |
| `Δn × n_o2_src` (D.1 split) | 3 × 10^8 × 3 × 10^8 | 9.0 × 10^16 | 51 |
| `A_SEAM_MAX × leak_bp × (1000 − q)²` (D.2) | 32 × 10^4 × 10^6 | 3.2 × 10^11 | 1.4 × 10^7 |
| `1000 × n_o2` (D.3) | 10^3 × 3 × 10^8 | 3.0 × 10^11 | 1.5 × 10^7 |
| `R_SCR × eff × sat` (D.5) | 1000 × 1000 × 1000 | 10^9 | 4.6 × 10^9 |
| `SP_IN × V × GAS_SCALE` (D.5) | 1500 × 200 × 1000 | 3.0 × 10^8 | 1.5 × 10^10 |
| `R_MAKEUP × eff × sat` (D.5) | 6000 × 1000 × 1000 | 6.0 × 10^9 | 7.7 × 10^8 |
| `K_HULL × S_ext × |ΔT|` (D.7) | 100 × 80 × 350,000 | 2.8 × 10^9 | 1.6 × 10^9 |
| `K_HOLE × Σ A_hole × |ΔT|` (D.7) | 10 × 512 × 350,000 | 1.8 × 10^9 | 2.6 × 10^9 |
| `1000 × Q_net` and `ΔT × C` (D.7) | 10^3 × 10^6 ; 5000 × 1.2 × 10^6 | 10^9 ; 6 × 10^9 | ≥ 7.7 × 10^8 |
| `K_CAP × circ × ΔT` (D.8) | 150 × 1000 × 350,000 | 5.3 × 10^10 | 8.8 × 10^7 |
| `K_RAD × eff_r × circ × ΔT` (D.8) | 150 × 1000 × 1000 × 350,000 | 5.3 × 10^13 | 8.8 × 10^4 |
| `C_COOL × level_ml` and `Δt_cool × C_COOL × level_ml` (D.8) | 8 × 10^5 ; 5000 × 8 × 10^5 | 8 × 10^5 ; 4 × 10^9 | ≥ 10^9 |
| `OVL_K × (ρ − 1000)²` (D.9) | 16 × 39,000² | 2.4 × 10^10 | 1.9 × 10^8 |
| `Δhp × rate_pm × stress_pm` (D.9) | 10,000 × 2000 × 3000 | 6 × 10^10 | 7.7 × 10^7 |
| `W_DUTY_BP_PER_100 × duty_pm` (D.9) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* | 6 × 1000 | 6 × 10^3 | 7.7 × 10^14 |
| `G_OPEN × A_max × 10^6` (D.12) | 10 × 512 × 10^6 | 5.1 × 10^9 | 9 × 10^8 |

**Output Range:** the worst product in the system is `G_OPEN × A_m × abs(g)` at 3.1 × 10^17, a headroom of 15 below 2^62 (the corrected gas maximum is ten times the first draft's and `A_m` a thousand times its area, so the margin is thin on purpose and is exactly why the table is asserted at boot) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, and the worst thermal product is `K_RAD × eff_r × circ × ΔT` at 5.3 × 10^13, a headroom of 8.8 × 10^4 below 2^62 — so TD's two-stage temperature form and high-pressure tank caveat (both written against SI mmol/mK units) do not apply to the game units of R1, and no intermediate ever needs a divide-before-multiply. The assertion exists so that this stays true when a designer raises a maximum: a `V_max` of 20,000 tiles or a `GAS_SCALE` of 10^6 would trip it at boot rather than wrap at 2 a.m. in a playtest.
**Example:** at boot `max_K_RAD` = 150, `max_eff` = 1000, `max_circ` = 1000, `max_|ΔT|` = 350,000 → 150 × 1000 = 150,000; × 1000 = 1.5 × 10^8; × 350,000 = 5.25 × 10^13 < 4.61 × 10^18 → pass. If a mod raised `max_|ΔT|` to 10^9 mC the final product would be 1.5 × 10^17 — still a pass — but raising `GAS_SCALE` to 10^6 raises `n_max` to 3 × 10^11 and `G_OPEN × A_m × abs(g)` to 3 × 10^20, which the assertion refuses at boot (`GRAPH_BOOT_PRODUCT_OVERFLOW(G_OPEN × A_m, abs(g))`), and Section G's safe range forbids it anyway.

**D.14 Seam properties at weld — capacity, integrity, weep, and the disconnected start**

The `seam_at_weld` formula is defined as:

`cap_seam = ⌊min(cap_a, cap_b) × (500 + q / 2) / 1000⌋` per loop sub-edge ; `hp_seam = q × 10` ; `leak_bp = SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP` ; `A_seam` per D.2

Derived: every seam sub-edge is created **disconnected** (R9): OPENING `area` = 0 (hatch closed), PIPE and DUCT sub-edges carry a VALVE state `open` = false, CABLE sub-edges a breaker `closed` = false; each is opened afterwards by its own verb (`set_opening_area`, `set_valve`, `set_breaker`)
Derived: `surge[]` returned by `can_join`, one entry per sub-edge, informational: openings `|P_a − P_b|` dkPa (flag `warn` when > `WELD_DP_WARN`); pipes `|P_a − P_b|` and `|t_cool_a − t_cool_b|`; cables the served/charge differential `|sat_a − sat_b|`
Derived: wear-in `leak_bp' = max(0, leak_bp − SEAM_WEARIN_BP_PER_100)` on each wear period (D.9's cadence) — a seam that is left alone tightens over `SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP / SEAM_WEARIN_BP_PER_100` periods, and the weep of D.2 falls linearly with `leak_bp`, so every period of wear-in already counts *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; V11 SEAL_SEAM (VS-A) sets `leak_bp` to 0 at once (at MVP M7 PATCH on a SEAM does the same, LS-18)
Derived *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN: the closed-hatch witness, P4)*: every run in phase 11, not only at weld, a stored OPENING at `area` 0 **between two COMPARTMENTs** (any interior door, hatch or seam hatch) emits a `SEAL_STRAIN` descriptor *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* — an OPENING with an EXTERIOR endpoint (the airlock's outer door, the V6 vent hatch) never does: it holds 1000 dkPa against vacuum and 50 °C against ambient on every run of the game, so the first draft's rule made a healthy ship creak and frost for ever, and its failure is its hull segment's BREACH, so a degraded exterior seal has no separate witness at MVP (Open Question 35) — (LS-14 id 26; `subject_kind` OPENING; `sev` 1; `intensity_pm = min(1000, |P_a − P_b|)`; `dir` toward the lower-pressure side and `compartment_id` that side) while `|P_a − P_b| > WELD_DP_WARN` — `medium` GAS, `acoustic` CREAK — or, when only `|T_a − T_b| > HATCH_DT_FROST_MC` holds, with `medium` HEAT, `acoustic` TICK and `compartment_id` the warmer side (the rim frost); it clears with hysteresis when `|ΔP| ≤ WELD_DP_WARN − H_P` and `|ΔT| ≤ HATCH_DT_FROST_MC − H_T` (D.3's margins), and on any run the opening has `area > 0`, where `AIRFLOW` and D.1 take over. With `area` 0 no gas moves (D.1 is 0 at `A` = 0), so this descriptor is the only thing the pressure behind a closed hatch does to the world — and `surge[]` has no other outlet than it and the corruptible instruments

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| cap_a, cap_b | int | loop units/step | state (the two ports' `cap` from their module defs) | Capacities of the joined ports |
| q | int | 400–990 ‰ (Assembly clamps once at data load: `Q_MIN = max(400, ⌈max(LEAK_HP, BREACH_HP) / 10⌉)`, `Q_MAX` 990; the first form guarded `LEAK_HP` only, so a tuned `BREACH_HP` above `LEAK_HP` could start a minimum-quality weld BREACHED — routed to Assembly, registry `weld_quality`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | calculated by Assembly (registry: `weld_quality`), stored on the SeamRecord | Weld quality *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from modular-ship-assembly)* |
| cap_seam | int | 70 %–99.5 % of the lesser cap | calculated at weld, then state | Seam sub-edge capacity |
| hp_seam | int | 4000–9900 | calculated at weld, then state | Starting integrity of every sub-edge of the seam |
| SEAM_LEAK_INITIAL_BP | int | 0–10000 (default **10000**) | data file (registry candidate) | Fraction of the geometric weep that a fresh seam actually leaks |
| SEAM_WEARIN_BP_PER_100 | int | 0–1000 (default 100) | data file | Wear-in per 10 s; 100 → a seam is tight after 10,000 steps = 16.7 min |
| WELD_DP_WARN | int | 100–900 (default **300**) dkPa | data file (registry candidate) | Opening surge above which `can_join` flags a warning; never a reject; also the `SEAL_STRAIN` pressure threshold on every closed opening |
| HATCH_DT_FROST_MC | int | 5000–50000 (default **20000**) mC | data file (registry candidate) | Temperature difference across a closed opening above which its rim frosts (`SEAL_STRAIN` with `medium` HEAT) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)* |
| H_P, H_T | int | 5 dkPa / 500 mC | data file (D.3) | The hysteresis `SEAL_STRAIN` clears with |

**Output Range:** `cap_seam` is 70 % of the lesser port at `Q_MIN` 400 and 99.5 % at `Q_MAX` 990 (925 ‰ at the MVP default `q` = 850); `hp_seam` is 8500 at `q` = 850 — OK, sev 0 — and 4000 at `Q_MIN`, exactly `LEAK_HP`: a fresh seam never starts in LEAK or BREACH (the worst weld starts DEGRADED, its sub-edges WORN and weak), and a `q` below 400 is unreachable (registry `weld_quality`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from modular-ship-assembly)*. The seam still weeps gas to EXTERIOR through `A_seam_m` from its first run. Because every sub-edge starts closed, **a weld moves nothing**: welding onto a vacuum, frozen or dead section changes no pressure, temperature or charge on the home-ship until the player opens the hatch, the valve or the breaker, each of which has its own surge read-out and its own diegetic warning (the `SEAL_STRAIN` creak and rim frost at the closed hatch, which is where the surge lives until the hatch opens; *Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN*). The weep through `A_seam_m` is the weld's P1 tell and the only leak a weld itself creates.
**Example:** cable caps 3000 and 4000, `q` = 850 → `cap_seam` = `⌊3000 × 925 / 1000⌋` = 2775 W; `hp_seam` = 8500; `leak_bp` = 10000; `L_seam` = 3 → `A_seam_m` = 1,080 (D.2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Home-ship cargo bay at 1000 dkPa, derelict compartment at 150 dkPa → `surge` for the opening = 850 > 300 → warning flagged, weld permitted; opening the hatch afterwards through `set_opening_area(64)` moves `2 × 64 × 850` = 108,800 mGU in the first step and the two rooms (`V` 24 and 20) are within 20 dkPa of each other by step 315 (32 s) and settle at 613 dkPa (LOW) — with the bay's vent and the scrubber running both are back in NORMAL (P ≥ 705) after about 13,500 steps (22.5 min) on that one vent, and opening the seam duct valve (V5) puts the derelict room's vents on the same pool and divides that by the vent count *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* — the "draught equalising rooms" the MVP must show. Wear-in: `leak_bp` 10000 → 0 after 100 periods and `A_seam_m` falls with it — 1,080 at period 0, 540 at period 50, 9 at period 99, 0 at period 100 — so a fresh seam weeps for 16.7 min at a tapering rate (the 24-tile bay ends at 797 dkPa, D.15 SEAM-WEEP) and then stops; the player can wait it out or patch it (V1) at once. (The first draft held `A_seam` at 3 until period 100 and then dropped it to 0.) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* Closed-hatch witness *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: the same cargo bay (1000 dkPa, 20,000 mC) against the derelict room (150 dkPa, −30,000 mC) with the seam hatch at `area` 0: `|ΔP|` 850 > 300 → `SEAL_STRAIN` sev 1, `medium` GAS, CREAK, `intensity_pm` 850, `dir` into the derelict room; `|ΔT|` 50,000 > 20,000 as well, so the rim frosts. Once the hatch is opened and the rooms meet near 613 dkPa the pair sits at `|ΔP|` 0 and, if the hatch is closed again, the descriptor stays off until a gradient above 300 rebuilds; a hatch that was live and whose gradient falls to 299 stays live until it reads 295.

**D.15 Time-to-band estimates — the dread clock, closed-form, against the R21 targets**

The `time_to_band` formula is defined as:

`t_leak = ⌈ ln( (n_0 + R_B / k) / (n_band + R_B / k) ) / k ⌉` steps for a compartment losing gas at fraction `k` per step (D.1) and breathing `R_B` mGU/step, with `n_band = p_band × V × GAS_SCALE`

Derived: pure leak (`R_B` = 0): `t = ⌈ln(P_0 / P_band) / k⌉`; stock drain: `t = ⌈stock / drain⌉`; wear to a threshold: `t = ⌈(hp − hp_thr) / W⌉ × WEAR_PERIOD_STEPS`; thermal: `t = ⌈τ × ln((T_0 − T_amb) / (T_band − T_amb))⌉` with `τ = C / (K_HULL × S_ext / 100 + K_HOLE × Σ A_hole)` (a whole ship with its doors open: Σ C over Σ conductance); growing hole (fatigue past breach): `t` is the first `m` with `0.01 × Σ_{j=1..m} A_j ≥ ln(P_0 / P_band)` where `A_j = ⌈64 × 50 j / 4000⌉`, `m` in wear periods
Derived: evaluated by the acceptance harness and this document only — never by the sim (`ln` is forbidden on the propagation path, LS-1); the harness compares the estimate with the simulated step count and both against the R21 target ±15 %

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| k | float (harness) | 10^−5–10^−2 per step | calculated (D.1) | Leak fraction |
| n_0, n_band | int | mGU | calculated | Starting O2 amount and the band's amount |
| R_B | int | 0–1200 | `R_BREATH` × `N_crew` (D.5; `N_crew` 1–4) | Respiration in the room; O2-ROOM is stated for `N_crew` 1 (the R21 target), O2-ROOM-N for 2 and 4 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| p_band | int | dkPa | constant (LS-7 table) | Band boundary |
| τ | float (harness) | steps | calculated (D.7) | Thermal time constant |
| W | int | bp per period | data file (D.9) | Applicable wear rate |
| t | int | steps | calculated | Estimate, rounded up |

**Output Range:** each row below names its fixture exactly (so the ±15 % test is unambiguous), the estimate at the Section G defaults, the R21 target and its band. All eight MVP faults land inside their bands at the defaults; the harness is the proof, this table is the design intent. **Tolerance policy** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: a single-mechanism row (O2-ROOM, O2-SHIP, PWR-OVL, PUMP-STALL, SEAM-WEEP) asserts the absolute step count of its closed form to ±2 % (PWR-OVL, being exact integer arithmetic, asserts ±1 step — one tolerance in its criteria) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; an integrated multi-mechanism row (CLT-LEAK, FUEL-DRIP, HULL-FATIGUE, BREACH-RECOVERY, HEALTHY) asserts ±15 % of the integrated estimate printed in the row, which was produced by integrating D.2 / D.5 / D.8 step by step rather than by a closed form.

| Fixture | Mechanism | Estimate | R21 target (±15 %) |
|---|---|---|---|
| **O2-ROOM**: `V` 20, one hull pinhole (`hp` 3999 → `A` 1), `G_OPEN` 2 → `k` = 10^−4; one crew `R_B` 300; vent isolated, scrubber off; `p_o2` 210 | THIN (< 180, `n` 3.6M): `⌈ln(7.2/6.6)/10^−4⌉` = 870 steps = 1.5 min; HYPOXIC (< 140): 2163 = 3.6 min; ANOXIC (< 100, `n` 2.0M): `⌈ln(7.2/5.0)/10^−4⌉` = 3646 steps = **6.1 min** | 6 min room (5.1–6.9) ✓ |
| **O2-SHIP**: pool 3,000,000 mGU (one MVP tank), scrubber unpowered, exposed DUCT at `hp` 2000 → `leak_mgu` 250 to EXTERIOR, **authored as a spur** (a branch off the trunk with no vent downstream of it), because `hp` 2000 is FAILED with `cap` 0 and a duct on the vents' path would cut their `path_cap`, silence the vents and change the clock; the leak draws on the pool whatever the duct's own `cap`; vents hold 210 against one crew (300) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | drain 550/step → `⌈3,000,000/550⌉` = 5455 steps = **9.1 min** to pool empty, after which every room is on O2-ROOM's clock | 9 min ship (7.65–10.35) ✓ |
| **PWR-OVL**: cable `cap` 3000 at 3300 W, HOME section | 4 bp/step → DEGRADED 75 s, FAILED (branch dark) 2.9 min, then the FAILED cable burns on in SHORT at 4 bp/step (D.9 short burn; the player stops it by opening the upstream breaker) → DESTROYED **4.2 min** (step 2,500) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | 4.5 min (3.8–5.2) ✓ |
| **CLT-LEAK**: coolant PIPE at `hp` 3800, reservoir 20,000 mL, reactor 1500 dJ/step *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (integrated; a PIPE leak draws from the reservoir) | wear 16/100; the leak `⌈20 × (4000 − hp) / 4000⌉` = 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 mL/step in turn is debited from the reservoir; `level_pm` reaches `L_CAV_LO` (`circ` 0) at step 4,756 with 16,000 mL gone and the pipe still at `hp` 3032 — **cavitation shuts the loop before the pipe fails**; `T_mach` > 70 °C from step 4,520 as `circ` falls, OVERHEAT 50 steps later, SCRAM 300 steps after that: **4,870 steps = 8.1 min** (the first draft's 8.9 min assumed the pipe failed first) | 9 min (7.65–10.35) ✓ |
| **FUEL-DRIP**: fuel PIPE flange at `hp` 3999, reactor at its rated 2500 W (0.3 mL/step), FUEL_TANK 20,000 mL *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (integrated; a PIPE leak draws from the tank) | wear 16/100; the leak `⌈20 × (4000 − hp) / 4000⌉` is debited from the **FUEL_TANK** — 1 mL/step for 13 periods, 2 for 12, 3 for 13, 4 for 12, then 5 — so the tank is empty at step 6,151 (10.3 min), about 150 steps before the pipe would reach `HP_FAIL` at period 63 (step 6,300), and the reactor starves; SCRAM `FUEL_STARVE_STEPS` 100 steps later: **6,250 steps = 10.4 min** | 10 min (8.5–11.5) ✓ |
| **HULL-FATIGUE**: HULL_SEGMENT `hp` 6999, `fatigued`, bounding a `V` 20 room, one crew | 50/100 → BREACH_HP at 60 periods = 10 min; then growing hole: `0.01 × Σ A_j ≥ 0.916` (DECOMP) at `m` = 15 periods → **12.5 min** to DECOMP; ANOXIC about 10 s earlier | 12 min (10.2–13.8) ✓ |
| **BREACH-EVENT**: footprint RUPTURE sets a segment to `RUPTURE_HP` 3600 → `A` 7, `k` = 7 × 10^−4; section stressed (`stress_pm` 2000, fatigued) | DECOMP `⌈ln(2.5)/7×10^−4⌉` = 1309 steps = **131 s** at the initial hole; with the stressed fatigue growing it, 105–120 s | lethal 90–150 s ✓ |
| **DERELICT**: any of the above on a DERELICT section | the `hp` clocks run ×2 (`rate_pm` 2000): PWR-OVL FAILED 875 and DESTROYED 1,250 steps and HULL-FATIGUE breach at 3,000 steps (exact halves; HULL-FATIGUE DECOMP 6.2 min); the **drain clocks do not halve** because a stock drains in mL or mGU whatever the wear rate: FUEL-DRIP SCRAM at about 3,300 steps = 5.5 min (the pipe reaches `HP_FAIL` at period 32, step 3,200, with about 10,000 of the 20,000 mL gone, so the reactor starves on the FAILED pipe before the tank empties — within 6 % of half of 6,250) and CLT-LEAK SCRAM at about 2,850 steps = 4.75 min (the pipe fails at period 25, before cavitation, and `circ` 0 follows: 17 % over an exact half of 4,870, so the CLT-LEAK row keeps its own figure); hand estimates [UNV], the harness replaces them *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* | compression 0.5 on pure `hp`-clock rows only ✓ |
| **DUTY** (reference, not a fault) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: SCRUBBER, PUMP, REACTOR and HEATER at `hp` 10000, `duty_pm` 1000, HOME section | 2/100 → `HP_OK` at `⌈3000 / 2⌉` = 1500 periods = **4.2 h** (first WORN sev 1); `HP_FAIL` at 3500 periods = 9.7 h; a 30-minute session costs 360 bp | P5: a fully repaired ship is quiet for a session, never permanently safe; an (auto) criterion in Section H |
| **HEALTHY** (the baseline, not a fault) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: MVP home-ship, heater 500 W with thermostat (`T_SET_MC` 22 °C), lamps 160 + scrubber 240 + pump 120 dJ/step, Σ `S_ext` 80; for **heat, any door configuration** | doors open: plate loss 20 dJ/°C → uniform equilibrium where `520 + 500 × duty_t = 20 × (T + 30)`, i.e. **≈ +20 °C** (heater duty ≈ 0.96, so the open ship is nearly heater-limited; the fixed-heater draft read +21 °C); every compartment COMFORT / NORMAL / NORMAL indefinitely; no descriptor at sev ≥ 1 **with the doors open** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*; F-MVP-HEALTHY starts the cryo bay at 20 °C, because the authored −2000 mC cryo bay reads `ATMO_COLD` sev 2 by design (LS-26 onboarding content, a consequence-channel body reading and not a fault) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The heater room (cryo bay, `V` 42, `S_ext` 13) with all doors closed settles just under `T_SET_MC` (≈ 21 °C) and never reaches HOT. Unheated rooms that stay isolated for 20 min or more cool toward ambient (COOL, then FROST): the player's closed door, a physical consequence and not a fault. **Default applied 2026-10-27, confirmed 2026-10-28 (delegated ruling R20) (D12, Open Question 34): this cold is the intended consequence of closing the door, not a warning.** "A healthy ship never warns" means the Graph raises no hazard-mode, SEAL_STRAIN or WORN descriptor on a healthy ship; the `ATMO_COLD` descriptor of a room the player has isolated (sev 1 at COOL, 2 at FROST) (and the rim frost of a closed door across a large `|ΔT|`) is the body reading its true temperature, so a closed-door ship is HEALTHY with respect to faults while its isolated rooms read cold *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*. **For alarms** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix; delegated ruling R21)*: no `alarm_eligible` descriptor exists with the doors open; with the doors closed an unheated room left isolated long enough reaches ATMO_COLD FREEZING (sev 3, `COLD_DMG_ONSET_MC` −15 °C) and then the annunciator sounds after the 100-step gate. Accepted by the creative-director lean, delegated ruling R21, 2026-10-28: FREEZING is the first level at which the unprotected take cold damage, and COOL / FROST (sev 1, 2) preceded it for the whole approach. The whitelist form of D12 is confirmed | the constraint the thermal defaults were set to; an (auto) criterion in Section H |
| **FREEZE-BREACH** (reference): `V` 30, `S_ext` 22, `T_0` 20 °C, `T_amb` −30 °C, sealed, with a RUPTURE hole `A` 7 | conductance 5.5 + 14 = 19.5 → `τ` = 4,615; FROST (0 °C) `ln(50/30)` → 2,357 steps = 3.9 min, pipe pre-stage (−10 °C) `ln(2.5)` → 4,229 = 7.0 min, FREEZING (−15 °C) 5,550 = 9.3 min, `FREEZE_ON` (−20 °C) `ln 5` → 7,428 = **12.4 min** | C7 "4–10 min" to the pre-stage ✓; SD's unheated-room "8–10 min" is superseded by the healthy-baseline constraint (a sealed room with no hole now takes 44 min — a slow cold, not a clock) |
| **SHIP-COLD** (reference; the intentional dark-ship clock *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*): whole home-ship after power loss, doors open, Σ `C` 300,000, Σ conductance 20 | `τ_ship` = 15,000; COOL (10 °C) `ln(51/40)` → 3,650 steps = 6 min, FROST (0 °C) `ln(51/30)` → 7,960 = 13 min, FREEZING (−15 °C) 18,360 = 31 min; **liquid pipes: FREEZE pre-stage (−10 °C) `ln(51/20)` → 14,040 = 23 min, `FREEZE_ON` (−20 °C) `ln(51/10)` → 24,440 = 41 min, with no hull hole at all** | the dark is cold within a session and freezes the loops within 41 min; kept on purpose (Pillar 5 dread: hiding in the dark has a deadline, and a frozen loop bursts on thaw, C5); a breach only brings it forward, to 12 min |
| **DOOR-BREACH** (reference): `V` 20, `A` 64 to vacuum | `k` 0.0064: LOW 56 steps = 5.6 s, DECOMP 143 = 14 s, VACUUM 360 = 36 s | the "chaos" end; no target |
| **SEAM-WEEP** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: default weld `q` 850, `L_seam` 3 (`A_seam_m` 1,080 at period 0, 540 per room), bay `V` 24 at 1000 dkPa, vents isolated, scrubber off | `k` 4.5 × 10^−5 tapering linearly to 0 over 100 periods: 1,080 mGU/step at the start, 455 at step 5,000, 0 at step 10,000; the bay loses 20 % and ends at **797 dkPa** (905 at step 2,500, 843 at 5,000) | intended seam clock: tight at period 100 = 16.7 min, `band_p` NORMAL on every run (P ≥ 780); with the vents isolated, as in this fixture, `band_o2` enters THIN at about step 4,325 (7.2 min, P < 857) and does not return before the vent runs — a deliberate sev-1 tell of the unvented bay, absent with the vent live *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* ✓ |
| **BREACH-RECOVERY** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (integrated): `V` 20 room, one crew, vent and scrubber running, pool 3,000,000, RUPTURE hole (`A` 7) open for 600 steps then patched (`hp` ≥ `BREACH_HP`) | P 664 / p_o2 145 at the patch; O2 first at net +300/step; NORMAL (P ≥ 705, p_o2 ≥ 185) after **2,712 steps = 4.5 min**; both setpoints after 22,045 steps = 37 min. Patched after 900 steps (P 542): NORMAL after 10,832 steps = 18 min, setpoints after 30,166 = 50 min | stated target: NORMAL within 3,100 steps of the patch in the 600-step case (P5: a patched breach recovers) ✓ |
| **O2-ROOM-N** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (co-op crew count): O2-ROOM with `N_crew` 2 and 4 (`R_B` 600 and 1,200), vent isolated, scrubber off | ANOXIC at `⌈ln((4.2M + R_B/k) / (2.0M + R_B/k)) / k⌉`: `N_crew` 2 → 2,429 steps = **4.0 min**; `N_crew` 4 → 1,459 steps = **2.4 min** | R21 states 6 min for `N_crew` 1 only; four crew miss it by 3.6 min. Decision recorded: the co-op tuning pass scales the **template** (a scrubber, tank and vent per crew pair, VS-B), not `R_BREATH`, which is Suit's registered constant (Open Question 32) |
| **BROKEN-BREAKER** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (static fault): CABLE_JUNCTION at `hp` 2000 (FAILED, `cap` 0), its branch carrying the scrubber, two lamps and a door motor | no clock: on run 1 the island splits (phase 1), every load behind the junction reads `served_pm` 0 in phase 2 and carries STARVE; SHORT / STARVE descriptors are live by run 2 (one hop per run); the scrubber is then O2-ROOM's sealed-room clock once the pool is spent (per crew in a sealed 20-tile room: THIN at 3.3 min, HYPOXIC 7.8, ANOXIC 12.2, no O2 left at 23.3) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; fixed by FULL repair or replacement | static; first descriptor within 2 runs (0.2 s) ✓ |
| **PUMP-STALL** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: PUMP on a dead branch (breaker open), RESERVOIR full, reactor 1500 dJ/step, engine room 20 °C | `circ` 0 on run 1, `Q_cap` 0, `T_mach` = 20 °C + 1500 × 40 mC = 80 °C on run 2 (the HOT and OVERHEAT thresholds are crossed together); OVERHEAT after `OVERHEAT_ON_STEPS` 50, SCRAM `OVERHEAT_SCRAM_STEPS` 300 later: **350 steps = 35 s** (C3) | C3 35 s ✓ |

**Example:** O2-ROOM, ANOXIC: `k` = `2 × 1 / (20 × 1000)` = 0.0001; `R_B / k` = 3,000,000; `n_0` = 210 × 20,000 = 4,200,000; `n_band` = 100 × 20,000 = 2,000,000 → `ln(7,200,000 / 5,000,000)` = `ln(1.44)` = 0.3646 → `⌈0.3646 / 0.0001⌉` = 3646 steps = 364.6 s = 6.08 min. The sim will land within a few steps of this (D.1's floor loses under 1 mGU per step against a 2,000 mGU flow).

**D.16 Dormancy and partition cost bound — what one run may cost**

The `run_cost_bound` formula is defined as:

`W_run = Σ_{s ∈ ACTIVE} ( N_s × c_n + E_s × c_e + C_s × c_c ) + F × c_f + K × c_k` with the requirement that the `W_run` of the hooks that run on one hook tick stays within the Graph's **spike share** `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS` of the kernel's 2 ms target *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*

Derived: a section with `dormant` = true contributes 0 — its slots are skipped by every phase (R13); its state is persisted and hashed unchanged
Derived: `F ≤ MAX_SPREAD_PER_STEP` new failures and `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` footprint writes per run bound the only two terms that are not proportional to content size
Derived *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (the decision ladder, written before spikes S0–S3 run; a rung is taken only when the rung before it misses the share on the Release template): rung 0 typed GDScript with preallocated scratch and no per-run allocation; rung 1 hook stagger by offset 0 / 2 / 4 (the three hooks already exist, R3) — **the MVP baseline, not a fallback** (Suit registers its vitals at offset 2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; rung 2 island quiescence, **VS-A only** (on a fully active MVP ship every flux is non-zero, so it saves nothing there) (a power island or section whose every flux was 0 and whose nodes produced and consumed nothing for `QUIESCE_STEPS` runs sleeps until a mutator or a neighbouring non-zero seam flux wakes it; the flag is persisted), then — rung 3 — GDExtension, only if the VS-A fixture still misses after rungs 1 and 2. **MVP has no rung after rung 1** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: if S1 still misses the share at MVP, none of this document's levers solves it — the Graph re-requests its budget from the technical-director (Open Question 31), and it is the number that moves, not the design. **S1 sample population:** P99 and max are taken over every (tick, hook) sample of the F-MVP 36,000-tick run after a 600-tick warm-up, at offsets 0 / 2 / 4 and once unstaggered for comparison. Every wall-clock figure is measured on RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK on the R12 release-export build (X14 closed 2026-10-28, delegated ruling R12; the developer's machine is indicative only; pending spike measurement) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*. **Budget clauses** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: (a) *allocation* — zero heap allocations per phase in steady state: scratch arrays are sized once at M1 and `resize`d only by structural mutators, the descriptor list is a preallocated array reused every run, and no Dictionary is touched on the path; (b) *read side* — `failures()` is the per-step cache built once in phase 11 (FT-15), every reader receives the same array, and the whole presentation read side is budgeted at ≤ 0.1 ms P99 per frame; `read_view` builds no Dictionary per call on the hot path (typed getters or a reusable view object), and descriptors live in a pooled columnar store so that a filtered `failures()` allocates nothing *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; (c) *hash cadence* — `block_hash()` is recomputed only after a write and at most once per kernel snapshot or `state_hash` request; (d) *tombstones* — slots are append-only and never compacted at MVP, so tombstones count toward `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` and `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES` and the content lint warns above 25 % tombstones

**Variables:**
| Variable | Type | Range | Source | Description |
|----------|------|-------|--------|-------------|
| N_s, E_s, C_s | int | per section | state | Live nodes, edges, compartments of section `s` |
| c_n, c_e, c_c | int | µs (first-draft estimates 1.0 / 2.0 / 3.0, probably an order of magnitude low; review estimate 5 / 10 / 15 [UNV]; replaced by S0-f's **per-phase** benchmarks) | calculated (spike) | Per-node, per-edge, per-compartment cost in typed GDScript on the Release template *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* |
| F, c_f | int | 0–32 / ~1 µs | calculated | Failure-spread candidates evaluated and their unit cost |
| K, c_k | int | 0–256 / ~0.5 µs | calculated | Dirty keys emitted and their unit cost |
