| GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS | ms | 0.5 P99 / 1.0 max per hook tick (MVP; the sum of the Graph hooks that run on that tick); 1.0 P99 per tick (VS-A) | registry (`GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS`; its unit is re-worded by a registry request) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from debug-overlay-true-values)* | The Graph's **spike share** of the kernel's 2 ms P3 target — requested, not granted (apportionment is Open Question 31) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; `STEP_BUDGET_MS` (registry: 4 ms, "Diagnostic alarm for P3; target is 2 ms") is the kernel's logging threshold, not this bound |
| MAX_GRAPH_NODES, MAX_GRAPH_EDGES | int | 4096 / 8192 | data file (registry candidates) | Hard capacity; `can_join` and derelict load reject beyond them |
| MAX_SPREAD_PER_STEP | int | 8–64 (default **32**) | data file (registry candidate) | New failures applied per run, ascending id; the rest wait a run |
| MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP | int | 4–64 (default **16**) | data file (registry candidate; SD's `FOOTPRINT_BUDGET_PER_STEP`) | Footprint writes honoured per run across all callers and subjects (global, registry `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP`); the rest are counted in `graph_footprints_dropped` and dropped *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from attention-system, horror-entities, failure-and-trail-system)* |
| QUIESCE_STEPS | int | 5–50 (default 10) | data file | Runs of zero activity before an island sleeps (VS-A) |

**Output Range:** MVP at the weld (home-ship 40 nodes / 55 edges / 5 compartments + derelict 35 / 45 / 5, plus the seam), nothing dormant *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. At the first draft's coefficients (1.0 / 2.0 / 3.0 µs) that is `75 × 1.0 + 100 × 2.0 + 10 × 3.0` = 305 µs plus a worst cascade `32 × 1 + 38 × 0.5` = 51 µs ≈ 0.36 ms. The performance review's realistic coefficients (5 / 10 / 15 µs for typed GDScript with array indexing and bounds checks) give `75 × 5 + 100 × 10 + 10 × 15` = 1,525 µs plus a cascade of about 0.2 ms ≈ **1.7 ms, in a plausible range of 0.5–2.5 ms** — against a 2 ms target for the whole tick that every other divisor-6 hook (Suit vitals, Attention, Emitter) also draws on. The first draft's framing "10 ms at 10 Hz" was wrong: the Graph does not spend its time evenly over a 10 ms window, it spends it on one 60 Hz frame in six, and a 1.7 ms spike on a 16.7 ms frame is a frame-time hitch, not a budget saving. That is why the ladder above exists and why S1, not this arithmetic, gates approval: at offsets 0 / 2 / 4 the spike on one tick is the largest single hook (about 0.6 ms at the realistic coefficients). With the second derelict loaded but dormant it costs 0; loaded and active it adds about a third. VS-A naive (1,200 nodes, 2,000 edges, 120 compartments) is 5.6 ms at the first-draft coefficients and about 28 ms at the realistic ones — the reason quiescence exists; at 20 % active it is about 1.1 ms per run at the first-draft figures. The work is O(N + E) by construction (one Jacobi pass per loop, no iteration to convergence, bounded spread), so the bound is linear in content and the content lint (Section H) checks every derelict template against it. **Wall-clock acceptance criteria are gated on RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK on the R12 release-export build (pending spike measurement); the developer's machine is indicative only** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*.
**Example:** home-ship alone during the opening five minutes: `40 + 110 + 15` = 165 µs at the first-draft coefficients and `40 × 5 + 55 × 10 + 5 × 15` = 825 µs at the realistic ones, plus a single pinhole's descriptor and three keys — 8 % of the 10 ms the first draft compared it with, but 41 % of the kernel's 2 ms tick target on every sixth frame; the stagger would put about 0.3 ms on each of three ticks *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*.

## Edge Cases

*Every outcome below is the exact behaviour of the Graph owner module — its three hooks, its mutators, its codec — never of a presentation system; "diagnostics" names the counter the Debug Overlay exposes from the Graph's diagnostics channel (`graph_*`, following the kernel's `WRITE_OUTSIDE_SCOPE` style; TD's `graph_saturations[field]` is the numeric-tier counter, LS-24). Where an entry resolves a degenerate strategy from the player-facing design (D1–D9) the countermeasure is the sim rule that defeats it, not a content promise. Nothing here is "handled gracefully": each entry says what the numbers do.*

**Diagnostics counters** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Every `graph_*` counter named in this section is a **diagnostic: never persisted, never in `canonical_bytes()`, never hashed, never read by sim code**; it is reset at BOOTING and at restore (a restore does not restore it), and the Debug Overlay reads it through the diagnostics channel. A *per-run* counter is overwritten each run; a *cumulative* one only increases.

| Counter | Counts | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| `graph_saturations[field]` | numeric soft-tier clamps per field (LS-24); every suite asserts all zeros | cumulative |
| `graph_stall_edges` | open edges with `g ≠ 0` and `Δn` = 0 this run (D.1) | per run |
| `graph_gate_holds` | (subject, steps held) records of the D.11 gate, capped at 64 | cumulative list |
| `graph_spread_deferred` | cascade candidates deferred past `MAX_SPREAD_PER_STEP` | per run |
| `graph_stale_calls` | Class B calls with an unknown, tombstoned or dormant id | cumulative |
| `graph_draw_shortfall` | mGU of M15 requests unmet | per run |
| `graph_footprints_dropped` | M17 calls past the per-run cap | per run |
| `graph_footprints_clamped` | M17 calls clamped to their per-kind cap | per run |
| `hash_invocations` | `block_hash()` recomputations (test builds only) | cumulative |

**Arithmetic and conservation**

- **If two compartments are joined by an opening and the pressure gradient falls below `1 / (G_OPEN × A)` dkPa**: `Δn` floors to 0 and the rooms stop exchanging gas — **equalisation stalls within `⌈1/k⌉` mGU** of exact balance (10,000 mGU = 0.5 dkPa for a pinhole in a 20-tile room; 156 mGU for a door). This is expected behaviour, stated so QA does not file it: the residual is below the 10 dkPa key quantum and below any band boundary's hysteresis, and TD's truncation discipline (floor on non-negative magnitudes) buys exact conservation in exchange for it. Diagnostics: `graph_stall_edges` (count of open edges with `g ≠ 0` and `Δn` = 0 this run; informational; floors apply once, on the final division of D.1 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*).
- **If a power island has `avail` = 0** (the reactor SCRAMmed or failed and every cell flat — the dark-ship moment of C2) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G5)*: D.6's share would be 0 / 0; the rule is `gen_i` = 0 for every source and `served_k` = 0 for every tier, so every consumer reads `served_pm` 0 and carries STARVE on the next phase 9, no division is attempted and `graph_saturations` stays 0; the boundary has its own unit test (Section H).
- **If a compartment's `n_o2 + n_inert` is 0** (vented to vacuum, or a derelict authored empty): `P` = `p_o2` = `o2_pm` = 0 with no division (the `o2_pm` rule returns 0 on an empty room); D.1 from it moves nothing; D.1 into it is bounded by the source's degree share; the band is VACUUM/ANOXIC and the `ATMO_DECOMP` sev 4 descriptor is live. No diagnostics — this is a dead room, which the MVP derelicts contain by design.
- **If a thermal step's `ΔT` would exceed `MAX_DT` (2000 mC)**: `ΔT` is clamped, `t_rem'` carries the whole excess (`E − ΔT_clamped × C`, so `|t_rem|` may exceed `C` for a few runs and the heat is not lost), and `graph_saturations[DT_CLAMP]` increments. A hit in play is a content bug (a BURN footprint or a heater in a 4-tile closet), never a mechanic; every test suite asserts the counter is 0.
- **If a room's temperature is within `⌈100000 / (K_HULL × S_ext)⌉` mC of `T_amb` (182 mC for a 22-tile hull at `K_HULL` 25)**: the plate term floors to 0 and the room holds there — the thermal stall, the sibling of the gas stall. It cannot prevent `FREEZE_ON_MC` (−20000) because `T_AMB_MC` is −30000 and Section G's build assertion `FREEZE_ON_MC ≥ T_AMB_MC + 10000` keeps the margin; a tuning that sets `T_AMB_MC` to −20000 refuses to boot rather than silently making C5 unreachable.
- **If a SCRAMmed reactor is reset the moment its clear condition holds**: it can be — `out_w` ramps to 0 within 25 steps of the SCRAM, `Q_src` with it, so `T_mach` collapses to `T_room` and `STILL_HOT` clears about 3 s after the trip; the cost of a SCRAM is the 25-step ramp back to full output (the lamps come up slowly) and, if the cause is still there (`circ` 0), a second OVERHEAT in 50 steps and a second SCRAM 300 steps later — a 40-second trip-reset cycle the player can hear and must break by fixing the loop. This is the MVP rule (`T_mach` has no inertia of its own; the machine's casing is a proxy, not a store); VS-A may add a persisted machine heat store if playtest wants a SCRAM to cost minutes. Not a defect.
- **If any product on the propagation path would exceed 2^62 because a designer raised a field maximum**: the build does not start — D.13's assertion reports `GRAPH_BOOT_PRODUCT_OVERFLOW(a, b)` with the two operand names. GDScript `int` wraps silently [UNV, S0-b], so overflow is designed out at boot, not detected at run time.
- **If a release build finds an amount below 0, a value above its documented maximum, or a conservation sum that does not close**: the value is clamped to its range, `graph_saturations[field]` increments and the run continues (numeric soft tier, LS-24); the same condition in a debug build raises `GraphFault` and halts, and the kernel goes FAULTED. Rationale: a one-mGU rounding defect must not eject a player from a 40-minute session, and CI still fails because every suite asserts the counter array is all zeros.
- **If `step_no` approaches 2^43** (the RNG key's step field): it cannot — 2^43 steps is 27,000 years at 10 Hz. The assertion `subject_id < 2^20` is the live one; `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES` 8192 keeps ids below 2^14 and a build assertion in Section G keeps the cap below 2^20.

**Openings, leaks and breaches**

- **If two leaks open in the same compartment** (two breached segments, or a seam weep and a breach): each is its own virtual OPENING edge in phase 6 with its own `Δn` from the same start-of-phase snapshot, both count toward the room's degree (so `deg_max` rises and each edge's equalisation share falls — D.12's cap), and each emits its own descriptor with its own anchor and `intensity_pm`. The room loses gas at the sum of the two rates; the hiss has two sources, which is the P1 reading the player needs to find both. Diagnostics: none.
- **If a DUCT in mode LEAK is `exposed` = false** (runs inside the ship): its `leak_mgu` moves from the pool into the duct's compartment — conserved, so the room's `P` rises above 1000 (a 250 mGU/step leak lifts a 20-tile room 7.5 dkPa per minute — the first draft said 0.75 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*) and the room pushes a draught out of its doors (`AIRFLOW` descriptor). It is a loss of regulation, not of air; the ship-clock fault of LS-26 is therefore authored on an `exposed` duct, whose leak goes to EXTERIOR and `vented_mgu`.
- **If a VALVE upstream of a leaking PIPE or DUCT is closed**: `path_cap` to the leak becomes 0, `leak_ml`/`leak_mgu` draws from nothing, the mode LEAK stays (`hp` is unchanged — no leak wear while `intensity_pm` is 0, D.9; re-opened, the leak resumes from the stored `hp`) but the descriptor's `intensity_pm` is 0 — the drip stops, the frost stops growing, the sight-glass stops falling. Everything downstream of the valve is STARVE'd of fluid. The valve is the surgical verb (V5); its wrong use is "a silent dead consumer elsewhere", exactly as the player design intends.
- **If a vent's only path to the pool crosses a FAILED duct, a closed valve or a CUT seam**: `path_cap` = 0, `inj_o2` = `inj_in` = 0, the vent is silent and the room is on its own air — THIN in `⌈ln(7.2/6.6)/k⌉`-type times from respiration alone (per crew in a sealed 20-tile room with no leak: THIN at 3.3 min, HYPOXIC 7.8, ANOXIC 12.2, no O2 left at 23.3 — the first draft's "23 min to THIN" was the time to empty) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The vent's node carries STARVE (medium GAS) so the "off, not broken" distinction is readable.
- **If an O2_TANK is DESTROYED** (`hp` 0): its stock leaks at `LEAK_MGU_MAX` per step (500) into its compartment or to EXTERIOR by its `exposed` flag until empty; it still counts in the pool only for what remains. A tank that was the pool's only store leaves the vents with `pool` 0 within `stock / 500` steps.
- **If a hull segment reaches `hp` 0 by an endogenous cause**: the transition FAILED → DESTROYED is gated like any other (the segment has carried sev ≥ 3 since `BREACH_HP`, so the gate is long open) and `A_hole` becomes `A_HOLE_MAX` — a door-sized hole, VACUUM in 36 s for a 20-tile room. It is not repairable; the room is lost until a plate is installed (M5 on the segment's hardpoint, VS-A content; the Graph consumes no item — which part the install consumes is Assembly's and the Item DB's, Open Questions) — "loss is not total" (P5): the ship is not.
- **If a breach opens in a compartment whose stored OPENINGs to neighbours are all at `area` 0**: the loss stays in that room (C6 does not propagate), its neighbours' `AIRFLOW` descriptors stay silent, and the sealed doors are the reason — this is the designed payoff of V4 and the reason the pull-toward-the-hole descriptor matters only on the breached side.
- **If a breach is patched and the room had lost gas** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the room is on the recovery path of D.5 — its vent spends the 600 mGU/step budget on the O2 deficit first and on the inert deficit after, from the scrubber's make-up — so a `V` 20 room patched 600 steps into a RUPTURE is back in NORMAL after 2,712 steps (4.5 min) and at its setpoints after about 37 min (D.15 BREACH-RECOVERY). Pressure no longer has a ceiling sitting in the DECOMP band: the first draft injected O2 only, so total pressure could only fall and the D5 refill and the D.14 weld settle described states the ship could never leave. Diagnostics: none — the recovery is the rule.
- **If the scrubber is FAILED, salvaged, unpowered or shed while a room is below its setpoints** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the pool's O2 still reaches the vent (tank stock), but `mk_offer` is 0, so the inert deficit is never filled: `p_o2` recovers to its setpoint while the tank lasts and total pressure tops out at the setpoint plus the inert the room still holds — back in NORMAL (P ≥ 705) if it kept about 63 % or more of its inert gas (the 600-step BREACH-RECOVERY room tops out near 729), otherwise LOW or DECOMP persists until the scrubber runs again *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G4)*; a scrubber at `eff` 500 offers half the make-up. This is the web of loops (C2) doing its job, not a defect. Diagnostics: none.

**Fairness gate**

- **If an endogenous failure would cross `HP_FAIL` before `warn_elapsed` reaches 100** (an overload at `ρ` ≥ 1419, 70+ bp/step, because OVERLOAD opens the window on its first step; a non-overload cause above 40 bp/step from the `HP_OK` crossing) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: `hp` holds at `HP_FAIL + 1` and the excess damage is discarded, run after run, until the gate opens; on that run the pending transition applies with whatever damage that run brings. The hold is at most `100 − warn_elapsed` steps: 31 steps (3.1 s) for the D.11 example at `ρ` 1500, and 97 steps (9.7 s) for an arc-flash at `DHP_MAX` (sev 1 on step 1, `HP_FAIL` reached on step 4, held to step 100, FAILED on step 101). Diagnostics: `graph_gate_holds` (subject, steps held). *Alternative considered:* deferring the discarded damage as a debt — rejected because a debt makes the eventual FAILED → DESTROYED jump larger than the physics, which is a surprise the gate exists to prevent.
- **If `sev ≥ ALARM_SEV_MIN` holds while the gate is still closed** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* (an overload at `ρ` ≥ 1500: sev 3 from step 1): `alarm_eligible` is false for the first 100 steps and becomes true on step 101 purely because `gate_open` changed — `sev` is the same on both sides of the flip. It is the one case in which the two clauses of `alarm_eligible` come apart, and its test is a boundary pair (`warn_elapsed` 99 and 100).
- **Compartment alarm gate (LS-13 (b))** *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*: (i) a band flap THIN → NORMAL → THIN clears the tracker at the return and reopens it at the re-entry, with no accumulation across gaps; (ii) a derelict room instantiated already thin or empty opens its tracker on run 1 and its alarm comes 100 steps later (the body cues first); (iii) a restore inside a window resumes from the persisted trackers and flips at the same step as the uninterrupted run; (iv) `alarm_sections()` between hooks returns the last committed run (descriptors are rebuilt in phase 11, the failure hook, `T mod 6` = 4).
- **If an exogenous cause (RUPTURE, GOUGE, `apply_damage` EXOGENOUS, a RAGGED cut, a breaker closed onto an overload, a salvage) takes a subject below `HP_FAIL` or to 0 in one apply**: the transition happens that run, ungated, with `cause` = EXOGENOUS on the descriptor. The player did it or a horror did it; the gate protects against the sim, not against agency. `warn_since_step` is set on that run so that any *later* endogenous escalation (the gouged plate's fatigue) is gated normally.
- **If an endogenous and an exogenous cause damage the same subject on the same run**: phase 0 applies the exogenous damage first (queued inputs), phase 8 applies the endogenous damage against the gate; the floor is computed from the post-exogenous `hp`, so a GOUGE that lands at 3200 and an overload that wants 300 more leave the cable at 3001 until the gate opens — the exogenous part is never held, the endogenous part always is.
- **If a subject's `sev` returns to 0 before the gate opens** (a leak patched at `hp` 4100 after 30 steps): `has_warn` clears (`warn_since_step` resets to 0) and a later decline starts a fresh window. A warning that stopped is not a warning the player can still be charged with.
- **If a forced mode's pre-stage is skipped by content** (a derelict authored at `T_room` −25000 with liquid pipes): on the first run the pipes show FREEZE sev 1 and `warn_since_step` is set; they may freeze solid only 100 steps later *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. A derelict that is "already frozen" must be authored with `forced_mode` = FREEZE in its template, which is a content fact, not an endogenous transition, and is not gated. The content lint checks for the inconsistent case (liquid pipe below `FREEZE_ON_MC` without the mode) and reports it.
- **If `alarm_eligible` is requested by a reader for a descriptor at sev 3 whose gate is still closed**: it is false; the sweep may not run; the hum, glow and scorch are the only channels (R17 "never first"). Any consumer that plays an alarm from `sev` alone, bypassing the flag, is in breach of the exported requirement and the (lint) criterion in Section H catches it.

**Patch, repair, salvage, replace**

- **If a patch (`PATCH_CAP_BP` 6000) is applied to a leaking pipe**: `hp` rises to at most 6000, LEAK clears (`hp ≥ LEAK_HP`), the drip stops, but the subject stays DEGRADED at sev 1 (`eff` 750) — a patch weeps. After `PATCH_DECAY_STEPS` (2400 = 4 min) it loses `PATCH_DECAY_BP_PER_100` (100) per wear period and re-enters LEAK when `hp < 4000`, 20 periods = 200 s later; the re-leak is endogenous and gated, and the gate is already open because the sev-1 PATCH_WEEP descriptor has been live for 4 min (R15). A second patch restarts the clock; a FULL repair (Item DB part) ends it by restoring `hp_cap`. Diagnostics: none — the decay is the rule.
- **If a duty machine runs at partial `run_pm`** (a brownout, a throttled pump, a thermostat-idle heater) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: its duty wear scales with `duty_pm` — `⌈W_DUTY_BP_PER_100 × duty_pm / 1000⌉` per period, so at the default 2 bp a machine at `run_pm` 1..500 loses 1 bp per period and at 501..1000 loses 2; a machine at `run_pm` 0 (STARVE, breaker open, SCRAM, a pump at `throttle_pm` 0, a thermostat-satisfied heater) loses nothing *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, and a FAILED or DESTROYED one is not running and loses nothing. The ceiling is deliberate (a machine that runs at all wears), the coarseness is accepted (it is 1 or 2 bp per 10 s under a 250 bp dirty-key quantum), and the only way to stop duty wear is to stop the machine — which has its own cost on every other clock. Diagnostics: none.
- **If `repair_step` targets a DESTROYED subject**: the per-step check in the channel hook (`check_repair_step` → `NOT_REPAIRABLE`) fails and that step's apply is skipped — the deferred reveal, Interaction IA-12 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from interaction-system)*. The path is `salvage_node` (scrap) then `install_module`; a DESTROYED hull segment is replaced through its hardpoint.
- **If a subject is at `hp` 0, SALVAGED or ABSENT** — three different things: `hp` 0 is DESTROYED, present, ports connected, leaking at maximum (a DESTROYED duct leaks 500 mGU/step, a DESTROYED plate is a 64-area hole), descriptors live at sev 4; SALVAGED is a tombstoned slot whose fluid ports are `open_end` (SEVERED on the edges that touched it, leaking at `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` of cap until `cap_port`), no descriptor of its own; ABSENT is an empty hardpoint, inert, with no ports connected and nothing to leak. The reader's rule: DESTROYED is the loudest thing in the room, SALVAGED hisses at the stubs, ABSENT is silence.
- **If the player salvages the node that is currently running the ship** (the home-ship BATTERY during a SCRAM, the only SCRUBBER): M6 applies (it is a verb, exogenous), the island re-solves next run with `D` = 0 or `out_scr` = 0, every dependant goes STARVE on that run with HUM_DROP descriptors, and the room clocks begin. The tell precedes the act — the hum of the thing being cut is its warning — and the "stupid but plausible death" is nameable afterwards. No special case.
- **If `install_module` is called with a `def_id` whose ports do not match the hardpoint's declared port types**: `check_install_module` fails with `PORT_INCOMPAT`; the Item DB's salvage yield and the Layout's hardpoints are responsible for never offering it, and the lint over templates reports a hardpoint with no compatible module.
- **If `reset_node` is called on a SCRAMmed reactor while `T_mach ≥ OVERHEAT_OFF_MC` or with `starve_steps > 0`**: the result is `BLOCKED` with the physical reason (`STILL_HOT` or `NO_FUEL`), not a command reject — the casing is still scorching, the sight-glass is empty; the player reads why. The command succeeds (it was valid) and did nothing.

**Seams, weld and cut**

- **If a weld joins the home-ship to a section whose compartments are at vacuum** (a dead derelict): nothing moves (R9) — the seam OPENING is created at `area` 0, the seam DUCT valve closed, the seam CABLE breaker open; the home-ship's pressure, temperature and charge are unchanged on the weld run and every run after it until the player opens something. `can_join` returned `surge` = 1000 dkPa for the opening with `warn` = true (> `WELD_DP_WARN` 300) — not a reject, and not a number: from the weld run the closed seam hatch carries a `SEAL_STRAIN` descriptor (CREAK at the rim, `intensity_pm` 1000, rim frost because `|ΔT|` 50,000 > `HATCH_DT_FROST_MC`) and the corruptible gauge swings *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*. Opening the hatch is then the door verb (V4 through `set_opening_area`) with the full consequence: 128,000 mGU on the first step, LOW in the home-ship room within 6 s if it is the only connected room. The soft-lock of the first draft (welding drains the ship) cannot occur.
- **If a hatch under `SEAL_STRAIN` is opened** (`set_opening_area` > 0 at `|ΔP|` 850) *(Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN)*: the mutator applies in P2; on the next run the opening has `area > 0`, so phase 11 emits no `SEAL_STRAIN` for it and the gas flow follows D.1 in that run's phase 6 (108,800 mGU on the first step at `A` 64), with `AIRFLOW` taking the hatch's descriptor slot from the same run; the draught is the consequence. If the hatch is instead left closed the descriptor clears only when `|ΔP|` falls to `WELD_DP_WARN − H_P` (295) — through the seam weep, a leak on either side or the player venting — and the rim frost only when `|ΔT|` falls to `HATCH_DT_FROST_MC − H_T` (19,500) — a closed door has `A_o` 0 and conducts nothing (D.7), so this happens only as each room's own hull loss pulls the two toward ambient, or once the door is opened *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. Nothing moves through a closed hatch at any `|ΔP|`: a hatch never bursts, because `area` is the Graph's and only M13 writes it. Diagnostics: none.
- **If a closed OPENING has an EXTERIOR endpoint** (the airlock's outer door, the V6 vent hatch) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: it emits no `SEAL_STRAIN`. It holds 1000 dkPa against vacuum and 50 °C against ambient on every run of the game, so a creak and rim frost there would make a healthy ship warn for ever (P1, P2; the first draft emitted the descriptor on every closed OPENING, including this one). Its failure is its hull segment's: a degraded airlock is a BREACH pre-stage (STRUCT / TICK) and then a hole. Opening it is V6 and moves gas per D.1 as before. Diagnostics: none.
- **If a weld joins a dead section** (no power, batteries flat, every node FAILED or SALVAGED): the seam CABLE's breaker starts open; closing it (V3) merges the islands and, if the derelict's bus is a dead short (a FAILED CABLE_JUNCTION at `hp` 2000, energised → SHORT), the home-ship's cable serving the seam overloads per D.9 with its OVERLOAD descriptor from the first step — the scorch at the seam box is the tell, the breaker is the fix. Nothing on the derelict wakes until served.
- **If a weld joins a frozen section** (pipes in FREEZE, reservoir frozen): the seam PIPE valve starts closed; opening it (V5) connects a path through a FAILED (frozen) pipe whose `cap` is 0, so `circ` through that path is 0 and the home-ship's coolant loop is unaffected except that the seam pipe itself now sits in a FROST room and begins its own FREEZE pre-stage. Thawing the section (heater, M14) brings the burst roll (C5) — the readable cost of growth.
- **If a weld's seam CABLE has `cap_seam` lower than the load the player then routes across it** (a `Q_MIN` 400 weld gives 70 % of the lesser cap *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from modular-ship-assembly)*): the seam cable overloads under D.9 with its OVERLOAD descriptor at the seam — a rough weld is a weak bus, which the player sees before it fails. `q` is Assembly's number; the Graph only makes its consequence physical.
- **If a welded section carries `open_end` ports** (a derelict stub left by salvage before the weld) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from boarding-and-salvage)*: each stub leaks from whatever pool its loop component reaches — while the seam's PIPE/DUCT valve is closed only from the section's own stock; once the player opens it, from the home network's pool at `⌊cap × OPEN_END_RATE_PM / 1000⌋` per step until `cap_port`. Nothing special: the component rule applies.
- **If a seam is cut `SEALED`**: openings removed, sub-edges tombstoned, both sides' fluid ports `capped`, no leak on either side, one batch `T` + `S/<seam_id>` + affected `C/`; the derelict's compartments keep their gas. **If cut `RAGGED`**: each side's compartment gets a virtual hole `A_CUT` 24 on a hull stub at `hp` 2500 (sev 4 BREACH — D.4 gives sev 4 at `hp` ≤ 2500 — exogenous, ungated) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review, delegated ruling G4)* — DECOMP in `ln(2.5)/(2 × 24 / 20,000)` = 382 steps = 38 s for a 20-tile room (DECOMP, not "vented": VACUUM follows at 960 steps = 96 s) — and every fluid port is `open_end`, hissing at `OPEN_END_RATE_PM` of its cap until `cap_port` (M12). The two hazards are independent: `cap_port` never changes the hull hole, which has no MVP verb (LS-16); `seam_view(seam_id).cut_sides` reports each side's `a_hole` and `uncapped_open_ends` *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix, routed from modular-ship-assembly D54)*. Items in the section are Assembly's and the Item DB's loss; the Graph only takes the air.
- **If a breach opens on either side while Assembly's weld channel is in progress** (before the `weld` command's apply): the Graph does nothing special — the breach is an ordinary D.2 event in its own section; when `weld_join` applies, the seam opening starts at 0 so the breach cannot cross into the other section until the player opens the hatch, and `can_join` (run at the command's `validate`, the same tick as apply) reports the now-larger `surge`. The "breach window during the weld" the player design describes is the seam's own weep (`A_seam_m` > 0 from the weld run, 1,080 for a default weld *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*) plus the player's choice to open — not a hole the weld creates.
- **If a weld plan would close a cycle in POWER wiring** (both sections already share an island through another seam's cable, or the plan pairs two cables): `can_join` fails with `POWER_CYCLE` and the command is rejected at `validate`; the same check at data load refuses a template with a cycle. R12: islands are trees, no iterative solver; VS-A may lift it with a routing rule, not by removing the check.
- **If a weld or a derelict load would exceed `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` (4096) or `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES` (8192)**: `can_join` returns `CAPACITY` (domain reject `VALIDATION_FAILED(graph.CAPACITY)`); M1 refuses the section with the same reason before allocating anything. The caps are set so that MVP (≤ 120 nodes) and VS-A (≤ 1,200) content never reach them; the content lint reports any template within 10 % of a cap.
- **If a weld plan passes `can_join` but the D.12 sum for a seam compartment would exceed 10^6 ppm**: `can_join` returns `STABILITY` — in practice only for absurd content (Section D.12); the reject names the compartment and the sum so the content author can see it.
- **If `can_join` is called from presentation (the weld preview) rather than from `validate`**: it is pure and allowed; it reads the already-derived caches (`ensure_derived()` is eager at the end of every structural mutator and of `post_restore`, so the drag never pays a rebuild) and leaves `state_hash` unchanged with zero dirty keys (S2's atomicity criterion) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

**Degenerate strategies (D1–D9 from the player-facing design)**

- **If the player seals every door and lives in one room (D1)**: the room's O2 falls by `R_BREATH` per crew per step with no vent supply unless its vent has `path_cap` > 0 and the pool is fed — which needs the scrubber powered, which needs the reactor fuelled and cooled; the sealed room's `P` and `T` are its own, so a single hull plate's fatigue (50 bp per period, content-authored on outer rooms) is its only breach risk but its heat loss through `S_ext` continues; door motors are tier-2 LOADs that draw while holding; and nothing the Graph does refills the fuel tank or the O2 tank. The bot test in Section H drives this strategy for 30 sim-minutes and asserts that the ship's own clocks force a live descriptor of `sev ≥ 3` inside the window (a sealed room with a running scrubber, reactor and fuel stays NORMAL for the 111 min the tank lasts; THIN needs the scrubber unpowered) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. Countermeasure is the physics, not a script.
- **If the player tries to obtain unlimited power (D2)**: `S` is the reactor's rated `out_w × eff` ramp-limited; `D` is bounded by `Σ stock`; `CH_CAP` caps recharge at 300 W; the home-ship has one reactor, and after the weld derelict B's reactor is a second source (D.6 Example D) that is just as ramp-limited and SCRAM-able *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; the reactor's heat needs `circ` > 0 or `T_mach` reaches OVERHEAT in 35 s; and Attention reads `power_use`, so surplus draw is never free. The bot test asserts no command sequence of ≤ 200 commands raises `Σ stock` above the initial cell plus the reactor's integrated surplus.
- **If the player repeats patches to farm anything (D3)**: `repair_step` writes `hp` and `hp_cap` only; the Graph has no progression field, no loot hook and no reward callback (brief: no player progression from the Graph); a PATCH consumes no item and caps `hp` at `PATCH_CAP_BP`; a patch decays *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from item-and-module-database)*. There is nothing to farm.
- **If the player idles in a sealed scrubber room ignoring sorties (D4)**: the fuel tank drains at `FUEL_UML_PER_W` (111 min for 20,000 mL at 2500 W), outer plates authored `fatigued` creep toward breach, and the heater's draw keeps the reactor burning; the Graph's clocks run whether or not the player acts. The meso-loop pull is the concept's; the Graph supplies the slow decay.
- **If the player vents every compartment to purge hazards (D5, V6)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the vented room's gas goes to `vented_mgu` and is gone; refilling a 20-tile room costs 4,200,000 mGU of O2 from the pool (more than the 3,000,000 the MVP tank holds — the scrubber's 400 mGU/step covers the rest) at the vent's 600 mGU/step budget, 7,000 steps = **11.7 min for the O2 alone** (the first draft's "2 h per room" was an arithmetic slip), plus 15,800,000 mGU of inert make-up from the scrubber — a full refill of one vented 20-tile room on one vent is 33,333 steps = 55.6 min, slower still while it also feeds respiration. The room's heat is lost with its air only through the open hole's conduction (`A_hole` in D.7). Venting remains the nuclear option, but a recoverable one: the cost is time, scrubber power and the pool's O2, not the ship.
- **If the player keeps the breakers off permanently to hide (D6)**: every load behind the open breakers (scrubber, heater, pump, lamps, door motors) is unserved → STARVE; the pool stops refilling, the ship cools toward −30 °C with `τ_ship` 25 min — breath fog at 6 min, frost at 13 min, FREEZING at 31 — and any breached room freezes its pipes in 12 min with the burst roll waiting on thaw; door motors do not hold. **With no breach at all every liquid pipe still freezes solid at 41 min** (FREEZE pre-stage, frost on the lagging, at 23 min — D.15 SHIP-COLD): the first draft said pipes freeze only where a hole adds conductance, which was wrong, and the dark-ship clock is kept on purpose — hiding has a deadline, and a frozen coolant loop bursts on thaw (C5) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The Graph makes the dark expensive; the suit clock is Suit's.
- **If the player cycles weld and cut to duplicate anything (D7)**: a weld adds edges only (no nodes), a cut tombstones them; `cut_detach` never creates a node; `discard_section` destroys mass into `discarded_*`; ids are never reused; the section item is Assembly's and conserves the section. No Graph quantity is created by any mutator except M1 (content instantiation) and M5 (an Item DB part).
- **If the player plays by sound alone, never looking (D8)**: every descriptor has an `acoustic` class and every band a felt consequence; the Graph is indifferent. Not degenerate; listed so no one "fixes" it.
- **If the player save-scums a rupture (D9)**: the RNG is keyed by `(step_no, subject_id)` from `world_seed`, so a thaw-burst roll at step N on edge E has the same outcome after any number of reloads of the same world; the only way to change it is to change the state (thaw later, patch first). The autosave generation ring (`AUTOSAVE_GENERATIONS` 3, Save/Load SL-3) is Save/Load's *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from save-load-and-versioned-format)*; the Graph's determinism removes the incentive either way.

**Mutators, API and dirty keys**

- **If Suit's `draw_air` requests more O2 than the compartment holds** (`request > n_o2`): the grant is `n_o2`, the room goes to `n_o2` = 0 (ANOXIC), and the shortfall `request − granted` is returned to Suit, which owns what a lungful of nothing does. Multiple crew in one room are applied in ascending crew order within phase 0 against the running balance, so the last to breathe gets the least — order is deterministic (the queue is sorted by (compartment id, crew id)). Diagnostics: `graph_draw_shortfall` (total mGU unmet this run).
- **If a Class B mutator (M14–M18) is called with an unknown, tombstoned or dormant-section id**: it is a no-op returning 0/false and increments `graph_stale_calls`; it never rejects (CR-9 path (a) cannot fail inside an apply). A `set_load_request` on a SALVAGED lamp changes nothing; a `deposit_trace` on a discarded section is dropped.
- **If the footprint channel receives more than `MAX_FOOTPRINTS_PER_STEP` (16) writes in one run** (the cap is per run across all callers and subjects — global, not per subject): every M17 call is queued and drained at the Graph's next phase 0 (drain rule *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: a call drains at the first execution of `graph.propagate.power` that follows it in kernel order — tick first, then P2 before P3 hooks, then `after:` order — so a P2 command on tick 6k drains at 6k, a hook call on tick 6k ordered after `graph.propagate.power` (the default) drains at 6(k+1), and a hook that declares `before: graph.propagate.power` drains at 6k; Horror's and Emitter State's hooks declare no `before:` and take the 6(k+1) drain, Open Question 41); the first 16 by arrival (kernel tick, then call order) are honoured and applied in ascending subject id *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from horror-entities, attention-system, failure-and-trail-system)*, the rest are counted in `graph_footprints_dropped` and dropped — not deferred, because a horror's trace is a moment, not a queue. A single call above its per-kind cap (`FOOTPRINT_MAX_Q`, `FOOTPRINT_MAX_GOUGE_BP`) is clamped to the cap and counted in `graph_footprints_clamped`.
- **If `set_opening_area` is called with `area > area_max`** (the door's full size): clamped to `area_max` by the Class A check (`AREA_RANGE`); a door cannot be opened wider than it is. A hatch on a seam has `area_max` from the weld plan.
- **If a breaker is closed onto a branch whose request exceeds the cable's cap** (V3 after a short, or inrush on a loaded branch): the cable's OVERLOAD descriptor appears on the same run's phase 11 (sev 1 at `ρ` > 1000, sev 3 at ≥ 1500) and D.9 damage begins; the transition that follows is **exogenous** (overload by breaker, R7) for the first `INRUSH_STEPS` and endogenous thereafter — the player who slams a breaker onto a short gets the arc. Attention sees the spike in `delta_w_last5`.
- **If a run would emit more than `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` (256) keys** (a derelict instantiation of 35 nodes + 45 edges + 5 compartments with every band and descriptor; a VS-A multi-section cascade beyond D.10's 135): the kernel collapses the notification to `ALL` and every subscriber re-reads its view once; the Graph does nothing different. `ALL` is legitimate at structural events and the S1 spike reports its frequency in a breach window; steady state must stay ≤ 32 keys P95 or the quanta are revisited — the kernel constant is not.
- **If a continuous value crosses a quantum boundary back and forth on consecutive runs** (`P` at 699/700 with a pinhole): the `C/<cid>` key fires on each crossing — at most once per run, bounded by the run rate; bands do not flicker because of `H_P`, and the key cost is 1 per run per such room. If more than a handful of rooms sit on a quantum boundary at once the P95 criterion catches it and the quantum, not the kernel constant, is adjusted.
- **If a reader subscribes with class PRESENTATION and polls `read_view` between hook ticks**: it reads the values committed at the last Graph run (up to 5 ticks old); there is no interpolation inside the Graph, and `tick_alpha` is the presentation's business. Readers that need the same-run value (Suit, Attention) are hooks with `after:` and read in the same tick.

**Hooks, order, FAULTED, budget**

- **If a structural invariant fails in `graph.propagate.failure` while a cascade is mid-flight** (phase 10 has queued `forced_mode_next` on twelve subjects and an edge endpoint is found pointing at a tombstone): the kernel's step-invariant path fires — FAULTED, `Dropped(FAULTED)` for the tick's commands, no P5 notification, no autosave; the partial writes of the run stay in the block for the diagnostic dump; the dev build halts, the release build returns to the menu. Because `forced_mode_next` is persisted, the last good snapshot (taken at a tick boundary) carries the cascade as it stood then and a restore resumes it exactly — no hop is lost or doubled. Diagnostics: the kernel's FAULTED record names the invariant (`GRAPH_INV_EDGE_ENDPOINT(edge_id)` etc.).
- **If a mutator's `GraphFault.raise` fires after a passing `validate`** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)* (a bug, since `check_*` is meant to make apply infallible): FAULTED with partial writes, per kernel CR-9 — the fault-injection criterion in Section H proves the path. Mutators are written so that the only operations after the check are array appends, element writes and flag sets, with no lookup that can miss.
- **If the kernel pauses, snapshots or saves "mid-step"**: it cannot mid-hook — each hook is atomic inside P3 of its tick, every buffered delta is applied before its hook returns, and the kernel snapshots only at tick boundaries after P5. A snapshot therefore never observes a half-applied phase; under the baseline stagger it may fall between two hooks of one step (after power at tick 6n, after atmo at 6n + 2), where the block is consistent and the per-hook cursors `last_step_power`, `last_step_atmo` and `last_step_failure` record which hooks of step `n` have run, so the restored block resumes with the next hook due *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. **A snapshot may, however, be taken on any tick, between two hook ticks, and the input queues (`pending_draw`, `pending_load`, `pending_trace`) hold state across that gap: they are persisted and hashed (LS-21), so a restore on tick 1,803 with a crew member breathing and an M17 pending resumes with the same breath and the same trace at the next phase 0 as the uninterrupted run** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. PAUSED simply stops ticks; the Graph has no wall-clock dependence.
- **If the kernel's lag policy drops ticks** (`LAG_FORGIVE_TICKS`): the Graph runs on whichever ticks the kernel steps and never catches up — a dropped tick that was a hook tick is a step that did not happen, simulated time is shorter, and the clocks in D.15 are measured in steps that ran. No catch-up, no double step (LS-1 (j)). **Ruled (user, X13 / D2, 2026-10-27): `LAG_FORGIVE_TICKS` = 0 — the kernel never fast-forwards**, so the Graph sees no repaid ticks at all and the cases above reduce to the dropped-tick case; the kernel's per-catch-up-frame merge of presentation notifications (OQ 21, D3) is confirmed and changes nothing the Graph reads (the Graph is a SIM hook consumer, not a presentation subscriber) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — user rulings)*.
- **If a run exceeds `STEP_BUDGET_MS`** (4 ms): the kernel logs it; the Graph does not truncate, skip a phase or change any result — the budget is a diagnostic, and the Graph's own bound (`GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS`, measured in S1) is what approval rests on. Diagnostics: the kernel's overrun log carries the three hooks' individual times from the diagnostics wrapper, never visible to sim code.
- **If `MAX_SPREAD_PER_STEP` (32) is reached in phase 10**: the 33rd and later candidates (in ascending subject id) are not queued this run and are re-evaluated next run from the same persisted states; the front advances at most 32 subjects per 0.1 s. A fully dead bus of 40 loads takes two runs to show every STARVE. Diagnostics: `graph_spread_deferred`.
- **If a section is dormant** (derelict unloaded or zoomed out): its slots are skipped by every phase — no leak, no cooling, no wear, no roll; its atmosphere and `warn_since_step` values are frozen in place (on resume M19 advances every absolute step stamp by the dormant duration, LS-17, so a warning is never counted as served while the player was away) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `can_join` against it returns `SECTION_NOT_LOADED`; its state is in every snapshot and hash unchanged. Loading it resumes from the same values with no fast-forward (R13): a derelict left mid-breach is still mid-breach when the player returns, with `since_steps` reflecting the steps it *ran*, not the time away — which is what "the derelict does not degrade on its own" means and what Web Map's VS-A deltas will serialise.
- **If a sealed room with a running scrubber or heater is a candidate for VS-A quiescence**: it does not sleep — the rule is "every flux 0 **and** every node produced and consumed nothing"; a producer or consumer keeps the island awake. A truly sealed, dead, equalised room sleeps after `QUIESCE_STEPS` and wakes on any mutator touching it or a non-zero flux across a seam into it.
- **With the baseline hook stagger (power 0, atmo 2, failure 4; rung 1 of the D.16 ladder)** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: Suit's `after: graph.propagate.atmo` must share offset 2 or it reads a run-old atmosphere (kernel edge case), Attention follows power at offset 0, and Emitter State reads at offset 4; nothing in the Graph's rules changes, because no rule assumes same-tick cross-loop consistency (LS-8). A restore on any tick resumes from the per-hook cursors (LS-21). The registrations in Suit, Attention and Emitter State are in place (applied 2026-10-28) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*.

**Serialization and restore**

- **If a snapshot is restored with any of the following**, restore fails with the named reason, the live block is untouched (two-phase: decode and validate into scratch, swap only on success) and the kernel's restore-failure path reports it — never a silent default, never a partially applied world: `GRAPH_RESTORE_BAD_VERSION(stored, supported)` (anything but 1 in MVP, including 0 and `current + 1`); `GRAPH_RESTORE_BAD_TAG(tag)` (unknown or duplicated field tag); `GRAPH_RESTORE_BAD_LENGTH(field, expected, got)` (array lengths disagree or a field is truncated); `GRAPH_RESTORE_BAD_ENUM(field, slot, value)` (state, kind, mode, port type, loop or edge kind outside the type tables); `GRAPH_RESTORE_OUT_OF_RANGE(field, slot, value)` (a value 1 above its documented max — for gas `n_max(c) = P_MAX × V × GAS_SCALE` of the restored room, not a global 3 × 10^7 *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* — or below 0 — a numeric *soft* violation in play is a *hard* failure on load, because a corrupt save must not be "fixed" into a different world); `GRAPH_RESTORE_ID_ORDER(slot)` (ids not strictly ascending); `GRAPH_RESTORE_QUEUE(queue, length)` (a `pending_*` queue longer than its bound, or naming an unknown compartment, node or subject) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*; `GRAPH_RESTORE_COUNTER(counter, max_id)` (`next_id ≤ max id`); `GRAPH_RESTORE_BAD_EDGE_ENDPOINT(edge_id)` (slot out of range or a live edge pointing at a tombstone); `GRAPH_RESTORE_SEAM_MISMATCH(seam_id)` (a seam sub-edge whose record is missing, or a record naming a dead edge); `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF(def_id)` (the Item DB does not know the module — silent substitution would change ports); `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_ROOM(room_ref)` (Layout, restored first, has no such room); `GRAPH_RESTORE_INVARIANT(name)` (any LS-24 structural check that the above did not already name). S3's 1,000-mutation fuzz drives each.
- **If a restore succeeds**: `post_restore` rebuilds every derived cache (components, trees, `path_cap`, degrees, `V` and `exposed_tiles` from Layout, id → slot maps) and also `served_pm` and the failure-descriptor list from the restored canonical state — the derivation parts of phases 2 and 11, which are pure functions of it, writing no persisted field and drawing no RNG — so lamps and fault glows are correct on the first presented frame, not up to `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS − 1` ticks later *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from emitter-state-and-light-exposure-query)*; seeds the last-emitted dirty buckets from the restored values (the band and SEAL_STRAIN memory is read from the snapshot, never re-derived from the values, so the first run after a restore reproduces an uninterrupted run *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*), emits exactly one `ALL`, and the next hook due runs — the power hook of step `last_step_no + 1`, or the atmo or failure hook of the step in progress when the per-hook cursors (LS-21) show it pending *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. The canonical bytes immediately after restore are byte-identical to the snapshot's (tombstones included) — the S3 criterion.
- **If a save is written as JSON text by Save/Load**: every Graph int must come back through `int()` and be range-checked, and the Graph's canonical bytes must travel as the base64 of the raw `canonical_bytes()` (`Marshalls.raw_to_base64`; no `var_to_bytes`, whose `allow_objects` argument is Godot 3-era) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)* — constraint S-1, routed to Save/Load; a save that stored a packed array as a JSON number list is rejected by `GRAPH_RESTORE_BAD_TAG` because the Graph's decoder accepts only its tagged byte form. The Graph's own values are all below 2^53, so the float hazard is the kernel's 64-bit counters' and the hash's, not this block's — but the decoder does not rely on that.
- **If `restore_after: [layout.state]` is not honoured** (Layout restored after the Graph): `post_restore` finds no rooms and fails with `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_ROOM` for the first compartment — loud, by design, because a compartment without a volume cannot derive a pressure. The kernel's block ordering is the fix, not a default `V`.
- **If a template's `def_id` is renamed between builds**: every save referencing it fails with `GRAPH_RESTORE_UNKNOWN_DEF` — pre-release saves are disposable (Save/Load SL-14: the migration obligation begins with the first build given to anyone outside the project, the three-person MVP playtest) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from save-load-and-versioned-format)*; after release a rename needs a migration step that maps the old string, which is why `def_id` is a string and type ids are numbers.

**Content and composition**

- **If a derelict template contains a liquid pipe, a vent and a hull segment all in one 4-tile compartment with eight openings**: D.12's sum is `⌈2 × (8 × 64 + 64 + 48) × 10^6 / 4000⌉` = 312,000 ppm — accepted; the content lint also reports any compartment above 500,000 ppm as "fragile" so a later `G_OPEN` change does not surprise. Only a sixteen-door closet at `G_OPEN` 10 is refused.
- **If a template places the HEATER (500 W, `waste_permille` 1000) in a sealed 4-tile compartment** (`C` 12,000, `S_ext` 4 → 1 dJ/°C of plate loss) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*: the thermostat (D.7) throttles it — the closet settles where `500 × duty_t` equals its loss, just under `T_SET_MC` (about 21.8 °C), with no SCORCH and no `MAX_DT` saturation (41 mC per step at full request). The first draft's fixed heater would have climbed 500 °C above ambient in the same closet; with its door open to a corridor the 640 dJ/step per 10 °C of `K_OPEN` conduction carries the heat out and the closet sits a few degrees above the corridor. The content lint now computes `ΔT_eq` for every heated compartment from its **non-heater** sources with the doors closed and reports any above 60 °C, because a heater can no longer drive a room past `T_SET_MC`; the heater's `req_max` is a module-def cap, not a Graph clamp.
- **If a fuel puddle (`fuel_puddle_ml > 0`) sits in a compartment that goes below 0 °C**: nothing changes in MVP — the puddle descriptor stays, fire is VS-A (R8), and the Body's "slows walking" is read from `fuel_puddle_ml` whether frozen or not. VS-A may add a `frozen` presentation hint from the room band; the Graph will not add a field for it.
- **If the home-ship's cryo bay is authored at `T` −2000 mC (FROST band) so the first fault's coolant drip frosts** (LS-26): the room reads FROST from the first run with its `ATMO_COLD` sev 2 descriptor, the heater (tier 3, 500 W) warms it once powered: for a generic V 20 room (`C` 60,000 dJ/°C) at `ΔT` ≈ 500 × 1000 / 60,000 = 8 mC per step, 2.5 min to COMFORT; the authored 42-tile cryo bay (registry `room_volume`: V 42, `C` = 42 × 3000 = 126,000 dJ/°C) at ≈ 4 mC per step gross — about 5 min to COMFORT before plate loss and about 6.5 min with it (Derelict Authoring D.18) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from derelict-authoring-handmade-kits)* — and the frost trail from the leak stops growing when the band leaves FROST. The onboarding sequence is a physical one: restore power, feel the room warm, follow the drip to the clamp, patch it.
- **If Layout changes a compartment's `V` while the world runs** (not possible in MVP; a VS-A Assembly feature): the Graph re-bases the energy pair so `U = T × C + t_rem` is preserved (`T` unchanged, `t_rem` recomputed against the new `C`) and `P` changes by the volume ratio on the next derivation — the gas did not move, the room did. Recorded here so the VS-A rule is written against conservation, not against `P`.

## Dependencies

**Upstream (this system depends on):**

| Dependency | Nature | Interface and locked facts | Status |
|------------|--------|----------------------------|--------|
| Command & Simulation Layer | Hard | The Graph registers **one** state block `graph.state` (CR-8: `SIM`, `persist`, `WORLD`, `SHARED`, `repl_hint SHARED_SUMMARY`, schema v1) and **three** step hooks at divisor 6 with offsets 0 / 2 / 4 (the baseline stagger, LS-8) chained by `after:` with both chain edges declared `tolerates_stale: true` (`graph.propagate.power` → `.atmo` → `.failure`, CR-3) *(Amended 2026-10-28 — cross-GDD fix)*. All writes go through its published mutators inside a kernel `WriteScope` in P2 (CR-9); Class A mutators pair with pure `check_*` used by the owning command's `validate`, Class B mutators clamp and never reject. It registers **no commands of its own** — the verbs live with Interaction, Assembly, Suit. It cites the registered `rng_derive` (keyed counter), `state_hash`, `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS = 256` (confirmed, not set, by D.10), `STEP_BUDGET_MS = 4` (a logging threshold), `TICK_RATE = 60`. Two amendments are **routed** to the kernel owner and are not this GDD's rule: K-1 (per-block cached hash under D.12) and a one-line D.10 clarification (an owner-defined 63-bit key is a valid `counter`). Applied in the kernel GDD at the 2026-10-26 consistency check: Interactions row 1 "Blocks" → one block and three chained hooks; CR-3 and the Suit row examples `after: graph.propagate` → `after: graph.propagate.atmo` (the bare id `graph.propagate` does not exist and would fail boot under CR-3); "sets `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS`" → confirms. | Designed (locked) |

Compartments are keyed by Layout `room_id`; sockets reference Graph port ids. Full repair consumes an Item DB part (`repair_part_for`); patch does not. | Designed (`item-and-module-database.md`); IDs defined here *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from item-and-module-database)* |
| Art bible | Constraint (hard) | Trail vocabulary (frost, mist, scorch, drip, hiss, hum, flicker) and the per-loop hue/pipe grammar (O₂ Cyan thin round pipe with ring clamps; Power Amber flat bus; Coolant Ice lagged pipe; Fuel Ember thick flanged pipe; Hull Grey plates) are what Failure & Trail renders from this GDD's semantic descriptors; the Graph emits no visuals and names no colours. Diegetic-only readability; no meter, no schematic. | Approved |

**Load order and the Layout / Item DB cycle** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. The systems index lists Layout and the Item DB as depending on the Graph (they use its port-type and loop ids) while this GDD lists them as upstream (it reads room volumes and module defs): a cycle on paper. It is resolved by making both **data-contract peers** with a fixed BOOTING order and no runtime edge in either direction: (1) the id tables (loop, node kind, port type, edge kind, mode, state) are data owned here and consumed by both peers as constants; (2) the Item DB catalogue loads first and is validated by `connectable` and F-12; (3) Layout templates and `layout.state` load next; (4) `graph.state` registers last (`restore_after: [layout.state]`) and M1 consumes both read-only. Neither peer reads Graph state at load, and the Graph reads defs and rooms only through their read views, so the graph of dependencies is acyclic.

**Downstream (systems that depend on this one).** Hard unless marked. Every MVP dependent is now designed (2026-10-27); each row is the contract this GDD offers and the dependent GDD repeats it.

## Tuning Knobs

All knobs are **provisional until spikes S0–S3 and the MVP playtest**; every one traces to a Section D symbol or a Core Rule. *Kind*: **kernel** = owned by the Command & Simulation Layer and cited from the registry — this GDD points to the kernel's value and never redefines it; **registry candidate** = a new cross-system fact this GDD introduces, to be added to `design/registry/entities.yaml` on approval (marked ★); **set-once** = fixed at project start, changing it is a data migration of every save; **build** = asserted at data load, a violation refuses to boot; **data** = read from `assets/data/life_support_graph.json` at boot into `GraphTuning`, changeable between runs by a designer with no code change; **Suit** = owned by Suit & Equipment, listed because the Graph applies it. Every step-expressed value is invalidated by a `TICK_RATE` or divisor change; every mGU value by a `GAS_SCALE` change. Nothing in this table may take Attention, sanity, rhythm state or any perceived value as an input (LS-22) — that is an invariant, not a knob. Where a default differs from the specialist proposals it was tuned to the R21 bands and the reason is in the "Too low / Too high" cells.

| Knob | Kind | Default | Safe range | Affects (D ref) | Too low | Too high | Interacts with |
|------|------|---------|------------|-----------------|---------|----------|----------------|
| `TICK_RATE` | kernel | 60 Hz | 60 (set-once) | every per-step figure (D.11 `W_MIN_STEPS`) | — | — | points to the kernel's; `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` and `W_MIN_STEPS` are derived from it |
| `MAX_DIRTY_KEYS` | kernel | 256 keys per block per tick | 256; this GDD asks ≥ 128 | D.10 collapse to `ALL` | < 128: a modest cascade collapses to `ALL` every run and presentation re-reads the whole graph at 10 Hz | — | points to the kernel's; confirmed by D.10 (38 MVP / 135 VS-A) |
| `STEP_BUDGET_MS` | kernel | 4 ms | 4 | logging threshold only | — | — | points to the kernel's ("Diagnostic alarm for P3; target is 2 ms"); the Graph's bound is `GRAPH_HOOK_BUDGET_MS` |
| hook divisor | kernel | 6 (`hook_runs_this_tick` = `(T mod divisor) == offset`) | 6 | LS-8; one step = 0.1 s | 3: doubles every cost and halves every "per step" constant's meaning | 12: 5 Hz propagation — a door breach visibly steps | points to the kernel's; `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` mirrors it |
| `GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` ★ | registry candidate, set-once | 6 ticks | 6 (equals the divisor) | D.11, D.15, every rate | must equal the divisor — a build assertion | same | `W_MIN_STEPS = W_MIN_WARNING_S × TICK_RATE / GRAPH_STEP_TICKS` |
| `GAS_SCALE` ★ | registry candidate, set-once | 1000 mGU per GU | 1000 | D.1, D.3, D.5, D.13 | 100: pinhole fluxes floor to 0 (`G_OPEN × A × ΔP` < 1 more often); equalisation stalls at 5 dkPa | 10^6: `ΔP × V × V × GAS_SCALE` reaches 6 × 10^13 and D.13's `1/k` residuals become meaningless | `STD_PRESSURE_DKPA`, every mGU constant |
| `STD_PRESSURE_DKPA` ★ | registry candidate, set-once | 1000 dkPa | 1000 | D.3; what a healthy room reads | — | — | `STD_O2_PP_DKPA`, band tables |
| `STD_O2_PP_DKPA` ★ | registry candidate, set-once | 210 dkPa | 200–230 | D.5 default `SP`; D.15 O2 clocks | 190: rooms sit one band from THIN — the pinhole clock halves | 230: vents overfill against NORMAL's 180 floor; clocks lengthen 10 % | `HYPOXIA_PP`, vent `SP` |

Registered since the design review and no longer open: `R_MAKEUP`, `T_SET_MC` / `T_PROP_MC`, `P_MAX`, the P-band and T-band boundaries, `MAX_GRAPH_EDGES` and the milli-area form of `seam_at_weld` *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*.

## Visual/Audio Requirements

The Graph draws nothing and plays nothing. It owns the **semantics** that Failure & Trail, In-World Instruments and Adaptive Audio render, and it constrains them:

- **Descriptors, not visuals.** Every presentation of a failure is derived from a `FailureDescriptor` (mode, medium, severity 0–4, intensity ‰, anchor ids, acoustic class, `alarm_eligible`). Medium selects the art bible's trail word — O₂ → mist, coolant → frost, power → scorch, fuel → drip, integrity → hairline/buckling — and severity selects the band; the Graph never names a colour, a sprite or a sound.
- **One trail per source, continuous along its edge** (art bible rule). A descriptor carries the source anchor and the edge it travels, so a trail is thickest at the source and never appears at a node without upstream evidence on the connecting edge. Trails are on the consequence channel: the Perception Filter can neither remove nor add one, so an orphan trail cannot occur.
- **Spatial gradients are presentation.** Atmosphere is per compartment; frost creeping from a leak point is Failure & Trail's interpolation of the true compartment value plus the leak anchor and its age — honest to the Graph, never a per-tile simulation.
- **Lamps dim honestly.** `served_pm` drives lamp brightness under brownout; a lamp never renders brighter than the power it is served (P1).
- **Audio is true-state.** Hiss pitch/rate, hum and ticking are read from true values and belong to the consequence channel: a hallucination may neither remove a real hiss nor add a sound in a witness acoustic class (the eight LS-14 ids; WIND is the whistle; there is no loop-whisper class) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from adaptive-audio-and-tells)* (phantom-entity audio is the Filter's own, in its own classes); `alarm_eligible` (severity ≥ 3) is the only state in which an alarm-class sound or the Alarm Red sweep may play — never as the first warning.
- **The first fault the player ever sees** is the seeded coolant frost trail running from the cryo bay leak to the far wall (art bible carried element).

> 📌 **Asset Spec** — no sprites are owned here; the descriptor→trail mapping table is the input to `/asset-spec system:failure-and-trail-system` once that GDD exists.

## Game Feel

The Graph's feel is **slow burn, then cascade, then silence** — and the player should be able to say afterwards exactly why.

- **A body that decays on its own clock.** Left alone, the home-ship wears: its authored faults run on minute-scale clocks (first tell to lethal ≈ 6 min for an O₂ pinhole in a room, ≈ 9 for the ship; 4.5 for a power overload branch; 9 for coolant; 12 for hull fatigue), and the machines that keep it alive wear a little every hour they run (`W_DUTY`), so a fully repaired ship is quiet, never safe. Nothing resolves itself.
- **Long tell, short collapse.** A long tell, a spreading wrongness, a short collapse, then silence. The real tells run in minutes (D.15); the sim's own floor beneath them is `W_MIN_WARNING_S` = 10 s of two-channel physical warning before any loss the ship causes by itself, so "death is a bitch" is always nameable, never arbitrary. Horrors and the web have their own tells (Attention, Horror Entities) that must precede anything they do to the ship.
- **Every fix moves a cost.** Power for air (the scrubber draws, the web watches); air for heat; a sealed door for a stranded room; a weld for a leaky seam; a salvaged battery for a dark ship. Triage of five coupled loops, not a repair list.
- **Derelicts are already dead.** They don't tick until the player brings power; then faults that wake wear twice as fast (the wear clock, not the leaks and not the drains) — but still warn first *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; a breach is already past its tell by the time the hull gives.
- **Rooms never quite settle.** A faint draught at a nearly-shut hatch is real and stays; two rooms do not balance to the last breath. Expected, not a bug.
- **Silence is a tell, and there are two silences** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. A leak that has stopped hissing has either nothing left to leak (its descriptor is gone) or a closed valve upstream (the mode stays, `intensity_pm` is 0 and `isolated_at_step` is set). The sound cannot tell them apart; the valve lever can — its position is a TRUTH world-state witness (LS-14) — and so does the trail, which stops growing but stays. Silence alone never means "fixed".
- **The dark has a clock** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. A ship left without power does not only go quiet: it goes cold, and with no hole in it at all its coolant and fuel lines freeze solid in about 41 minutes (frost on the lagging first, at 23). Hiding in the dark is a choice with a deadline, and what freezes bursts when it thaws.

*Anti-targets*: self-healing faults, a rate dial that speeds everything, scripted "if A then B" cascades, dice-roll deaths, a status screen.

## UI Requirements

The Graph has no screens. It defines what three diegetic surfaces must be able to show, and forbids a fourth:

1. **In-World Instruments & Inspect View** — Instruments read values only through `perceived`; the honest needle moves on LS-20 quantum crossings as a one-step ramp (In-World Instruments IW-11) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — consistency pass, routed from in-world-instruments-and-inspect-view)*; a lit inspect card shows one instrument's precise reading and nothing else; gauges are per node, so two instruments on different nodes are the player's "second opinion".
2. **Failure & Trail** — trail spawn/despawn and band changes from `F/`, `C/`, `B/` keys; `T`/`SEC/` for structural rebuilds.
3. **HUD & Suit Layer** — the suit O₂ numeral on the critical strip is the suit's reading of compartment atmosphere via Suit, not a Graph overlay.
4. **Forbidden** — any schematic, overlay, minimap, ship-status or loop-summary screen that shows Graph state at a distance. The Debug Overlay (true values) is a development tool and is never shipped as a player surface.

> **📌 UX Flag — Life-Support Graph**: instruments, inspect card and trail legibility need `/ux-design` for In-World Instruments before epics; stories should cite `design/ux/instruments.md`.

## Acceptance Criteria

*Every criterion is pass/fail without reading the rest of this document. Tags in brackets name the Core Rule (`LS-n`), the Section D formula (`D.n`), the state, edge case or ruling the criterion discharges. Evidence class per criterion: **(auto)** a gdUnit4 test run headless by `commands.test` (exit 0 = pass; 100 = failures; 101 = passed but leaked orphan Nodes, which counts as a FAIL for this system and is impossible by construction for a `RefCounted`-only module) — pure-function tests under `tests/unit/graph/` (propagate, mutators, codec, conservation, overflow, fixtures from `GraphFixtures.*` factory functions, never file I/O) and through-the-kernel tests under `tests/integration/graph/` (hook order, weld/cut atomicity, G3/G4 on the real blocks); only criteria that are pure functions of fed inputs may carry (auto); **(lint)** a static scan run in CI over `src/sim/graph/` and over dependents' sources; **(review)** a code-review checkbox; **(manual)** an instrumented play or capture on a dev build with retained evidence (PNG, CSV, audio capture) in `production/qa/evidence/graph/`; **(spike)** a pass/fail number from TD's spike plan S0–S3 measured on the R12 release-export build on RM-DESKTOP and RM-DECK *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*, with prototypes in `prototypes/lsg-spike/` — these gate approval of this GDD's budget of record (R22). Fixture names (F-MVP, F-VSA, F-K, O2-ROOM, O2-SHIP, PWR-OVL, CLT-LEAK, FUEL-DRIP, HULL-FATIGUE, BREACH-EVENT, FREEZE, DOOR-BREACH) are defined in D.15 and TD's §5. All numeric defaults are the Section G values and are provisional until the spikes and the MVP playtest.*

**Units, fixtures and tiers** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*. *Units:* every time in this section is in **steps** (0.1 s each) unless a unit is written; 600 steps = 1 min and 6 ticks = 1 step; a figure in minutes is the step count over 600 to one decimal and is informative — the step count is the assertion. Pressures are dkPa, gas mGU, temperatures mC, integrity bp. *Fixture identities:* **F-MVP** is the MVP home-ship template as shipped, authored faults included; **F-MVP-HEALTHY** is F-MVP with the authored faults removed (the cryo bay's −2000 mC start is first-fault onboarding content, LS-26, and is removed with them: the cryo bay starts at 20 °C) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, doors open unless a criterion says otherwise, one crew member breathing and no commands; **F-MVP-REPAIRED** is F-MVP-HEALTHY with every subject at `hp` 10000 and `hp_cap` `HP_FULL`, every machine at `run_pm` of at least 501 (the open-ship heater's thermostat duty is about 960), no crew, and the harness refilling the FUEL_TANK between runs; **F-K** is the kernel integration world (the real blocks and hooks); **F-VSA** is the synthetic VS-A world of D.16; **F-TWO-SRC** is D.6 Example D; the D.15 fixtures keep their names. *Tiers:* `fast` (every PR, ≤ 60 s for the whole tier), `slow` (nightly, ≤ 15 min), `soak` (weekly and before a release, > 15 min); an (auto) criterion without a tag is `fast`. The `fast` tier is capped at 40,000 Graph runs in total, a full-ship criterion above 5,000 runs is `slow`, and every run-heavy criterion carries its tag (the untagged criteria of the first draft summed to about 185,000 runs against the 60 s budget) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. *Reference hardware (delegated ruling R12, 2026-10-28)* *(Amended 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*: every wall-clock criterion (marked spike) is gated on RM-DESKTOP (6-core i5-9400F / Ryzen 5 2600 class, GTX 1050 Ti / RX 570 class, 8 GB, 1080p60, P99 ceiling 16.6 ms) and RM-DECK (Steam Deck LCD, SteamOS stable, 30 fps target, P99 ceiling 33.3 ms), on a release export (Godot 4.7.2 release template, `forward_plus`, vsync on, 120-frame warm-up, startup read-back recorded); pass needs P99 ≤ 90 % of the ceiling; the figures are pending spike measurement. A relative bound (for example ≤ 30 % of the S1 baseline) is a regression guard against a recorded baseline and never an acceptance criterion by itself, because the baseline is the thing under test; the reference machines (RM-DESKTOP, RM-DECK) are a prerequisite *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review; 2026-10-28 — delegated ruling R12)*. *Seeds:* a criterion that names a search or a sample states its seeds and its bound. **Fixture catalogue** *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*: one definition per population, so no criterion uses a private list.

| Name | Definition | Used by |
|---|---|---|
| F-MVP, F-MVP-HEALTHY, F-MVP-REPAIRED, F-K, F-VSA, F-TWO-SRC | as defined above | their named criteria |
| MVP-FAULTS (9) | O2-ROOM, O2-SHIP, PWR-OVL, CLT-LEAK, FUEL-DRIP, HULL-FATIGUE, SEAM-WEEP, BROKEN-BREAKER, PUMP-STALL (LS-26's eight faults; the O2 pinhole has a room fixture and a ship fixture) | MVP-content and derelict criteria |
| CLOCKED-10 | MVP-FAULTS without BROKEN-BREAKER (a static fault with no clock) plus BREACH-EVENT | the snapshot sweep and the fairness-gate audit (with S1's 6,000 runs) |
| reference rows | BREACH-RECOVERY, O2-ROOM-N, DUTY, HEALTHY, FREEZE-BREACH, SHIP-COLD, DOOR-BREACH | their own criteria |

### Determinism & conservation

- **GIVEN** the Graph's registration, **WHEN** inspected after BOOTING, **THEN** exactly one block `graph.state` exists with `class == SIM`, `persist == true`, `persist_scope == WORLD`, `scope == SHARED`, `repl_hint == SHARED_SUMMARY`, `schema_version == 1`, `restore_after == [layout.state]`; and three hooks `graph.propagate.power` (`after: []`), `graph.propagate.atmo` (`after: [graph.propagate.power]`), `graph.propagate.failure` (`after: [graph.propagate.atmo]`), each with divisor 6 and offsets 0, 2 and 4 respectively (the baseline stagger of LS-8 and D.16) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*. (auto) [LS-1, LS-8; R2, R3] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*
- **GIVEN** `src/sim/graph/`, **WHEN** scanned, **THEN** zero `float` type annotations, zero float literals and zero calls to `sqrt`, `exp`, `pow`, `sin`, `cos`, `randf`, `randi` or `Dictionary` iteration appear in `graph_state.gd`, `graph_propagate.gd`, `graph_mutators.gd`, `graph_restore.gd`, `graph_derived.gd` or any other file of `src/sim/graph/`; the untyped `floor`, `ceil`, `round`, `abs`, `min`, `max`, `clamp`, `sign`, `snapped`, `lerp` and `hash()` are also forbidden (they return a Variant that may be a float; the module uses `floori`, `ceili`, `roundi`, `absi`, `mini`, `maxi`, `clampi`, `signi`) *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; `GraphState` declares only `PackedInt64Array`, `PackedInt32Array`, `PackedByteArray`, int fields and the interned `def_table` (`PackedStringArray`, the one string store, LS-1); nothing in the module extends `Node` or `Resource`. (lint) [LS-1 (b), (g), (i); H10]
- **GIVEN** F-MVP with every source and sink off, **WHEN** 6,000 runs execute with scripted breaches and leaks, **THEN** on every run `Σ n_o2 + Σ n_inert + Σ O2_TANK stock + Σ vented_mgu` equals its initial value exactly *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review)*, `Σ_c (T × C + t_rem) + Σ_r (C_COOL × level_ml × t_cool + tc_rem) + 1000 × radiated_dj + spilled_heat_mdj` (milli-dJ) equals its initial value exactly *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*, `Σ stock_ml + Σ spilled_cool_ml` is constant, and fuel `10^6 × (Σ stock_ml + Σ fuel_puddle_ml + discarded_fuel_ml + withdrawn_fuel_ml) + burned_uml − Σ fuel_rem` is constant *(Amended 2026-10-27 — re-review)*; no field is negative; `graph_saturations` is all zeros. (auto, slow) [LS-1 (e); D.1, D.2, D.7, D.8; S1 correctness]
- **GIVEN** F-MVP-HEALTHY with a crew member breathing (scrubber, vent, pump, reactor and heater all running), **WHEN** 18,000 runs execute, and again on a breach-and-patch script (RUPTURE at run 1,000, patch at run 1,600) over 40,000 runs, **THEN** on every run the four conservation sums of LS-1 (e) — gas including tank stock, `breathed_mgu` and `produced_mgu`; heat including the RESERVOIR heat store, `produced_dj` (full `Q_src`) and `spilled_heat_mdj`; coolant; fuel including `burned_uml` and `fuel_rem` — equal their initial values exactly, with the coolant loop circulating (`circ` > 0) on every run of the 18,000. (auto, slow) [LS-1 (e); D.5 counters] *(Amended 2026-10-27 — design review; re-review)*

### Loops & atmosphere

- **GIVEN** the type tables in the data file, **WHEN** loaded, **THEN** loop ids are exactly {0..5}, node kinds exactly the LS-3 set, port types exactly the LS-4 set, edge kinds exactly {1..7}, modes {1..10} with descriptor-only ids {20..27} (*Amended 2026-10-26 — CD-GDD-ALIGN*), states {0..5}, all unique, and a duplicate or renumbered id refuses to boot with `GRAPH_BOOT_TYPE_ID(table, id)`. The renumber baseline is the Graph's code-side enum constants: the data file must equal them exactly, so a renumber needs a reviewed code change. `GRAPH_BOOT_TYPE_ID` is a fault domain 2 (content data) code raised by the start-up content loader, while `GRAPH_RESTORE_*` codes stay in the Graph's domain 3 *(Amended 2026-10-28 — ADR-0007 sync, § 2.3 and § 3.2; ADR-LSG-08 is decided by ADR-0007)*.
